# Criticality tiers — by RESOURCE, not by app

**Hand-written on purpose. `python tools/criticality_tier_check.py` checks it and never rewrites it** — the tier and the sentence explaining it are a judgement, and a tool that regenerated this file would delete exactly the part that matters. Same reasoning as `docs/SOUP-REGISTER.md` and `tools/sairn_app_map_check.py`.

## Why this is by resource, and what the app-level version got wrong

Started 2026-09-10 as the third standing discipline, alongside the SOUP register and the traceability matrix. **The first version tiered whole apps, and measuring it is what proved that wrong.**

Eight apps sat at Tier B on the sentence *"nothing recorded ties it to money or a regulated record"* — an absence of RECORDING, because nobody had looked. Measured: every one of the eight had a money-bearing resource reaching a server. All eight moved to A, **and 21 of 22 verticals became Tier A**, at which point the register had stopped discriminating.

**The measurement was honest and the granularity was wrong.** A roofing app's invoicing panel and its colour-theme settings do not carry the same consequence, and one label per app forces them into the same answer. Michael's decision, 2026-09-10: tier by RESOURCE.

**The unit is `api/_resources/<app>.js`** — the same unit the SOUP register and the traceability matrix already operate on, reused rather than invented.

## The tiers

Same A/B/C vocabulary as `docs/SOUP-REGISTER.md`, which already tiers components. One scheme, not two. The tier is the **worst consequence of that resource being wrong**, not how likely it is.

| Tier | A resource qualifies when it... |
|---|---|
| **A** | handles **money** (invoices, payments, ledgers, billing, pricing that feeds them), or **regulated/protected data** (patient records, controlled substances, legal deadlines, financial trust accounts), or **its failure has already caused a documented incident** |
| **B** | is **neither money nor regulated** — internal operational data, scheduling, non-financial records |
| **C** | is **cosmetic, device state, or preference** — the shape already excluded from server sync for being regenerable or per-device |

**The B rule used to open *"is employee-auth-gated but…"*, and that clause was removed on 2026-09-22.** It made an ACCESS-CONTROL claim — a fact about code — inside a rule about CONTENT, and it was false for all 41 `SV_RESOURCES` rows: SAIRNvet had no per-employee authentication at all, `role` was a self-selected dropdown, and `api/sd-data.js` recorded that absence as a decision in its own comment. **PAST TENSE, CORRECTED 2026-09-24 &mdash; every clause of that sentence is now false and it was still written in the present.** `api/sv-auth.js` exists, `ROLES_BY_APP.sairnvet` is a server-side list, and `api/sd-data.js` refuses EVERY `SV_RESOURCES` resource with 401 NO_SESSION unless `verifySessionToken(token, licHash, 'sairnvet')` holds &mdash; verified live on 2026-09-23 (bootstrap 200, login 200, re-bootstrap 409) and asserted by `tests/app_session_isolation.js`, whose posture tripwire is what surfaced this. **THE CORRECTION DOES NOT WEAKEN THE RULE CHANGE, it strengthens it:** the clause was removed because a table must not assert a code fact, and the code fact then moved &mdash; twice &mdash; which is precisely the argument. Do not take the current state from this paragraph either; read the handler. The sentence was not describing something that had regressed; it was asserting something nothing had ever checked. **This table states what the data IS. Whether a gate exists is a code fact it does not assert**, and `tools/criticality_tier_check.py` now refuses a row that asserts one without citing a file or a test for it.

## The second axis — Confidentiality, added 2026-09-22

One tier could not say both things at once. `sd_exec_msgs` sat at **B** on the sentence *"an internal message lost"* — a perfectly reasonable DURABILITY reading of a resource whose actual risk was who could READ it — and the missing gate looked acceptable for as long as it did partly because nothing in its row invited the question. The fix was not a stricter rule; it was a second axis for the existing rule to apply to.

| Column | Question it answers |
|---|---|
| **Tier** | the **worse of the two axes** — derived, never hand-entered, and cross-checked by the checker |
| **Confidentiality** | how bad is it if the wrong person **READS** this |
| Worst consequence if it is wrong or lost | the integrity/availability axis, in words |
| Worst consequence if it is read by the wrong person | the confidentiality axis, in words |

**Confidentiality-A** means disclosure to the wrong party is itself the harm: PHI, a clinical record, legally privileged or client-confidential material, vital records and government identifiers, an individual's compensation, an authorisation map, or the firm's own confidential commercial position. **Confidentiality-B** means no elevated class — the row may be commercially sensitive, but no third party's protected data sits on it. Where the two axes disagree, **the Tier is the worse of them**, which is why 59 rows are Tier A on money while carrying Confidentiality-B.

**The 99 Tier A rows AS OF 2026-09-22 were read individually** in the §3.2 pass of `docs/2026-09-21-criticality-tiers-two-axis-spec.md` and carry a stamped group name in their evidence cell, so a reader can disagree with a GROUP rather than with 99 separate judgements. **THAT SENTENCE IS NOW DATED ON PURPOSE.** It read *"All 99 Tier A rows"* in the present tense against a table holding 186, and the B/C half read *"the 288 B/C rows"* against 202 — the same drift already recorded three times for the headline bullet, in a paragraph nothing checks. Every A row added since carries individually-written evidence naming its own source rather than a group stamp, which is the stricter of the two and is why the §3.2 group names stop where they do. **7 rows are marked `CONFIDENTIALITY NOT YET INDIVIDUALLY READ`** (17 when this sentence was written; the rest have since been read — `law_bankstatements`, and `bld_comm_log` in the SAIRNbuild re-audit of 2026-09-23, where the flagger's `name` signal was checked against the app and NOT supported) — flagged by `tools/confidentiality_candidate_flagger.py` and left at the default with the flag showing, because a could-not-tell is a third state and is never folded into a pass. **Re-count it rather than quoting it, AND COUNT ROWS RATHER THAN LINES:** ``grep -c '^| `.*CONFIDENTIALITY NOT YET INDIVIDUALLY READ' docs/CRITICALITY-TIERS.md``. **THE COMMAND THAT USED TO BE HERE COUNTED THIS SENTENCE (corrected 2026-09-24).** It was `grep -c "CONFIDENTIALITY NOT YET INDIVIDUALLY READ"`, with no anchor, so the prose that REPORTS the count matched too and the figure came back one too high -- permanently and reproducibly, because the prescribed re-derivation reproduced the same off-by-one every time anybody ran it. **A SELF-COUNTING NUMBER IS WORSE THAN A STALE ONE:** a stale number is wrong until somebody checks, and this one was wrong *including* when they did. The row anchor `^| ``` is what separates a table row from a paragraph about table rows. It also read NINE against an actual SEVEN when this was corrected, so it had drifted on the other axis too.

**Do not quote any count from this prose.** Run `python tools/criticality_tier_check.py`; it prints the tier count, the migrated count and the outstanding ones, and this file's own gaps note already records that a prose running-total was found wrong once.

## How to read the Evidence column, because it is the whole point

A tier asserted with no evidence is a label. **Every Tier A row cites something real** — a commit, an incident, a schema file, a registry line — so a reader checks the claim against that source rather than against this table. The checker refuses a Tier A row with an empty evidence cell, and refuses a Confidentiality-A row with an empty one for the same reason.

## `stonedesk` — all 36 registered resources

| Resource | Tier | Confidentiality | Worst consequence if it is wrong or lost | Worst consequence if it is read by the wrong person | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `sd_invoices` | **A** | **B** | A customer billed wrongly | A competitor or an unauthorised employee learns the company's own commercial figures. Commercially sensitive, not an elevated class: no PII, PHI, privileged communication or financial-account detail belonging to a third party sits on this row | Money. In the 21-resource backup (`93d37aa5`) and carries `soft_delete`, so a wrong invoice is hidden rather than destroyed. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (MONEY_INTERNAL)** |
| `sd_fin_jobs` | **A** | **B** | A job costed wrongly, and the invoice built from it wrong | A competitor or an unauthorised employee learns the company's own commercial figures. Commercially sensitive, not an elevated class: no PII, PHI, privileged communication or financial-account detail belonging to a third party sits on this row | Money. Backed up and soft-deletable in the same registry block. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (MONEY_INTERNAL)** |
| `sd_pricing_rules` | **A** | **B** | Every quote built afterwards priced wrong | A competitor or an unauthorised employee learns the company's own commercial figures. Commercially sensitive, not an elevated class: no PII, PHI, privileged communication or financial-account detail belonging to a third party sits on this row | Money, and upstream of it: pricing rules feed quotes, so one wrong rule is not one wrong record. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (MONEY_INTERNAL). Confidentiality-B, and the boundary against `sd_negotiated_prices` is stated rather than left to be inferred: a general rate table discloses the company's OWN margin structure, which is its own risk; a per-customer price discloses an identified customer's terms to somebody else, which is that customer's** |
| `sd_negotiated_prices` | **A** | **A** | A customer-specific price applied to the wrong customer | The firm's own confidential position is disclosed -- the material a competitor, a counterparty or a filing adversary would act on, and which cannot be un-disclosed | Money. Per-customer pricing is the shape where a leak is also a commercial disclosure. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (FIRM_CONFIDENTIAL)** |
| `stonedesk_quote_history` | **A** | **B** | A quote wrong, or a superseded quote presented as current | A competitor or an unauthorised employee learns the company's own commercial figures. Commercially sensitive, not an elevated class: no PII, PHI, privileged communication or financial-account detail belonging to a third party sits on this row | Money. The open-work row on the 26 local-only collections named quote history first. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (MONEY_INTERNAL)** |
| `sd_aiquotes` | **A** | **B** | An AI-drafted quote priced wrong and sent | A competitor or an unauthorised employee learns the company's own commercial figures. Commercially sensitive, not an elevated class: no PII, PHI, privileged communication or financial-account detail belonging to a third party sits on this row | Money, and generated rather than typed -- the fabricated-number class Guardian check 0b exists for. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (MONEY_INTERNAL)** |
| `sd_order_history` | **A** | **B** | An order recorded against the wrong job or customer | A competitor or an unauthorised employee learns the company's own commercial figures. Commercially sensitive, not an elevated class: no PII, PHI, privileged communication or financial-account detail belonging to a third party sits on this row | Money. Named in the local-only measurement alongside invoices. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (MONEY_INTERNAL)** |
| `sd_quote_requests` | **A** | **B** | A public quote request written into a money surface by an unauthenticated caller | A competitor or an unauthorised employee learns the company's own commercial figures. Commercially sensitive, not an elevated class: no PII, PHI, privileged communication or financial-account detail belonging to a third party sits on this row | Inherits by the 2026-09-10 public-surface decision: `stonedesk-catalog.html` issues `quote_request` through `/api/stonedesk-public`. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (MONEY_INTERNAL)** |
| `sd_hr_employees` | **A** | **A** | An employee record wrong, or readable by the wrong role | An employee's own record is read by a role that should not see it. Personnel identity, not roster presence | Identity. The employees seam is the one three other apps read; `api/sd-data.js` gates it on a StoneDesk session and refused a SAIRNbiz owner on 2026-09-10. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (STAFF_IDENTITY)** |
| `sd_hr_certs` | **A** | **B** | A lapsed certification shown as current | Little or nothing. A professional licence or certification is a public fact by design, and this row is what a customer or an inspector is meant to be shown | Regulated-adjacent: a certification record is what a customer or inspector is told about competence. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (LICENSURE)** |
| `sd_customers` | **B** | **B** | Customer contact records lost or merged wrongly | No elevated confidentiality class -- no PII, PHI, privileged communication, or financial-account detail on this row. Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read | Operational data lost or wrong: neither money nor a regulated record. Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read |
| `sd_approvals` | **A** | **A** | An executed approval is lost or altered, and the document proving a customer agreed to a price -- signature, total, deposit -- no longer proves it | A NAMED CUSTOMER's captured e-sign approval is disclosed: `client_name`, `signature_png` (a reusable signature image), `total_amount`, `deposit_amount`, `deposit_status` -- the same reusable-instrument class `rf_proposals` was promoted A/A for | **RE-TIERED B -> A on BOTH axes, 2026-09-25 (H1 log #537, read individually out of the app).** READ OUT OF THE HANDLER: `api/sd-data.js:2051` stores `{approval_id, client_name, quote_num, signed_date, total_amount, deposit_amount, signature_png, deposit_status, signed_by}` -- a named person joined to an executed signature image and the money they agreed to. **THE CODE'S OWN COMMENT ALREADY RECORDED THE INTEGRITY STAKE** while this row still said "neither money nor a regulated record": the branch header at `:1984` says the pre-server version meant *"the document proving a customer agreed to a price lived in one browser and died with its cache"* -- one cache clear destroyed the acceptance evidence, which is a loss event, not a rebuild. **THIS IS StoneDesk's UNREAD TWIN OF `rf_proposals` (A/A)**, whose confidentiality cell reads *"a customer's captured acceptance signature ... together with who accepted"* -- the identical shape, and the asymmetry between the two rows stood only because this one was never individually read. **WHAT THE ROW ALREADY HAS, so the tier is not an alarm:** session-gated read AND write (`api/sd-data.js` `:1997`, verifySessionToken app stonedesk), APPEND-ONLY enforced in three places (plain INSERT, unique constraint, no UPDATE grant), and `signed_by` taken from the verified session, never the body |
| `sd_blocks` | **B** | **B** | Block inventory wrong | No elevated confidentiality class -- no PII, PHI, privileged communication, or financial-account detail on this row. Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read | Operational inventory, same family as slabs |
| `sd_bundles` | **B** | **B** | Bundle grouping wrong | No elevated confidentiality class -- no PII, PHI, privileged communication, or financial-account detail on this row. Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read | Operational inventory, same family |
| `sd_slab_history` | **B** | **B** | Slab lineage wrong | No elevated confidentiality class -- no PII, PHI, privileged communication, or financial-account detail on this row. Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read | `sql/sd_slab_lineage_schema.sql` documents it as append-only event history |
| `sd_inventory` | **B** | **B** | Stock counts wrong | No elevated confidentiality class -- no PII, PHI, privileged communication, or financial-account detail on this row. Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read | Operational. In the 21-resource backup |
| `sd_remakes` | **B** | **B** | A remake recorded against the wrong job | No elevated confidentiality class -- no PII, PHI, privileged communication, or financial-account detail on this row. Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read | Operational rework tracking; cost lives on the job, not here |
| `sd_comms` | **B** | **B** | A customer message lost | **CONFIDENTIALITY NOT YET INDIVIDUALLY READ** -- flagged by `tools/confidentiality_candidate_flagger.py` (signal: name) and carried at the B default until somebody reads it. The default is what the flagger says may be wrong here, so this cell is a could-not-tell, not a pass | Operational communications log |
| `sd_sms_log` | **A** | **A** | A message record is lost or altered, and the shop's own account of what it told a customer and when is wrong. Not money and not regulated -- the integrity axis is genuinely B, and the TIER is A because the worse axis is confidentiality | A NAMED CUSTOMER, THEIR MOBILE NUMBER AND THE VERBATIM TEXT SENT TO THEM. The default B sentence's claim that no PII sits on this row is simply false | **RE-TIERED B -> A on CONFIDENTIALITY, individually read out of the app 2026-09-23 (cc). Integrity individually read and left at B.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `sdSMSSend()` at `stonedesk.html:32183` stores `{id, to, phone, msg, date, status}` -- `to` is a customer name, `phone` is their mobile, `msg` is the message body verbatim. Rendered per row (`:32151-32161`), searchable (`sdSMSRender()`, `:32605`), and PRINTED in full including the phone column (`sdSMSPrint()`, `:32216-32223`). A name joined to a mobile number joined to what was said to them is contact PII, and the row is the `sd_comms` shape rather than the `sd_inventory` shape. **THE STRONGER VERSION OF THIS CLAIM DOES NOT HOLD AND IS RECORDED AS REFUSED:** the Balance Due quick template (`:32129`) reads *"a balance of $[Amount] is due"* -- `[Name]` and `$[Amount]` are LITERAL PLACEHOLDERS a person types over, nothing substitutes a real balance, and `$[` does not interpolate inside the template literal it is written in. So the row can carry a customer's balance only because a human typed one, which is the `sc_auth.proc` argument and was rejected there. **CONFIDENTIALITY-A RESTS ON THE NAME AND THE NUMBER, WHICH ARE ALWAYS THERE, not on what somebody might type into `msg`.** Integrity stays B deliberately: the module's own comment (`:32188-32192`) says the log records that a message was COMPOSED, never that it was delivered, and the KPI beside it is labelled *"Sent This Month"* rather than Delivered -- honest, and checked, because the element id is still `sms-delivered` |
| `sd_email_threats` | **A** | **A** | A flagged scan is lost or mis-rated, and the shop's record that it was targeted goes with it. Neither money nor regulated -- integrity is genuinely B, and the TIER is A because the worse axis is confidentiality | A VERBATIM EXCERPT OF SOMEBODY ELSE'S EMAIL, captured because it was suspicious. Whatever the sender put in their first 120 characters is now stored: a name, an invoice reference, wire or account detail. The shop does not choose the content | **RE-TIERED B -> A on CONFIDENTIALITY, individually read out of the app 2026-09-23 (cc). Integrity individually read and left at B.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `sdEmailScan()` at `stonedesk.html:37258` stores `{id, type, risk, summary, date}` where **`summary` is `txt.slice(0, 120)` -- the pasted email itself, verbatim** (`summary`, `:37842`), rendered (`render`, `:37788`) and printed (`sdEmailPrint`, `:37862`). **THIS IS NOT THE `sc_auth.proc` CASE AND THE DIFFERENCE IS THE WHOLE ARGUMENT.** That row was left at B because tiering on what a user COULD type would make every free-text field A. This field is not user-authored: it is a CAPTURE of third-party correspondence, and the panel exists precisely because that correspondence is hostile -- the commonest real shape being invoice or wire fraud naming a real customer or supplier. Same limb as `sc_coded_items`, which is A for holding a verbatim quote of somebody else's text. **BOUNDED, NOT UNBOUNDED, and saying so matters:** the 120-character slice is a real cap, so this is a bounded excerpt rather than a whole message -- it lowers the volume and not the class. **FOUND WHILE READING AND NOT FIXED HERE, because `stonedesk.html` is another session's claim:** `risk` is derived by substring match on the AI's prose (`:37268`), so an answer containing *"not a high risk"* or *"no critical issues"* is stored as High or Critical. That is a wrong label on a security record and it is why the integrity cell says *mis-rated* as well as *lost* |
| `sd_field_stops` | **B** | **B** | A field visit record lost | No elevated confidentiality class -- no PII, PHI, privileged communication, or financial-account detail on this row. Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read | Operational scheduling |
| `sd_exec_msgs` | **A** | **A** | A private executive message read, altered or deleted by somebody it was never for | The firm's own confidential position is disclosed -- the material a competitor, a counterparty or a filing adversary would act on, and which cannot be un-disclosed | **RE-TIERED 2026-09-21 on the third A criterion — its failure has already caused a documented one.** It was **B** on the sentence *"an internal message lost"*, which is a DURABILITY reading of a resource whose actual risk is CONFIDENTIALITY, and that framing is plausibly why the missing gate looked acceptable for as long as it did: a lost internal message is a nuisance, so nothing about the B row invited anyone to ask who could read it. `sd_exec_msgs` is StoneDesk's private CEO/CFO/CTO channel and it was served through `SD_LOCAL_RESOURCES`, ungated, from `93d37aa5` until `fcd622df` — readable, writable and deletable by anything holding the shop's licence key, which is a BEARER credential the whole shop has. Reproduced live with a valid key and no `X-SD-Auth` header: 200 `ok:true`, the private message returned verbatim. Recorded **critical** in `docs/defect-density-register.json`. Same use of the incident clause as `sv_controlled` above, and for the same reason: the consequence is not hypothetical here, it is on the record. **NOT a general promotion of PII** — `sd_customers` stays B, and the difference is stated rather than assumed: this resource is defined by who may NOT read it, and it has a failure behind it. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (FIRM_CONFIDENTIAL)** |
| `sd_drawings` | **B** | **B** | A drawing lost | No elevated confidentiality class -- no PII, PHI, privileged communication, or financial-account detail on this row. Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read | Operational production artefact |

Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read | Operational configuration, read individually and placed IN the backup on purpose |
| `sd_nesting_saved` | **B** | **B** | A saved nesting layout lost | No elevated confidentiality class -- no PII, PHI, privileged communication, or financial-account detail on this row. Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read | Operational production artefact |

| `sd_seamai` | **B** | **B** | A seam plan lost | No elevated confidentiality class -- no PII, PHI, privileged communication, or financial-account detail on this row. Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read | Operational production artefact |
| `sd_photos` | **B** | **B** | A job photo lost | No elevated confidentiality class -- no PII, PHI, privileged communication, or financial-account detail on this row. Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read | Operational. Measured NOT server-backed before the backup work and moved in deliberately |
| `sd_business_snapshots` | **B** | **B** | A saved snapshot misreports what the dashboard said on a given day. Nothing is priced, billed or decided from it | A bundle of the company’s own commercial figures. Commercially sensitive, not an elevated class — the MONEY_INTERNAL line already settled for `sd_order_history` and `sd_quote_requests`, and aggregating B-class figures does not make them A | **CONFIRMED B on BOTH axes 2026-09-23, individually read rather than defaulted, and it was read because it looked like an A.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `sdBizSnapshot()` at `stonedesk.html:6808` writes `{id, date, kpis}` where `kpis` is built by `document.querySelectorAll('#panel-business [data-metric]')` → `el.textContent`. **IT IS THE `rf_photos` SHAPE: the PROVENANCE of a number, not the number.** The panel’s own subtitle says what it is — *“What the dashboard said, on the day it said it”* — and every figure on it is a COPY of a figure that lives on a resource already Tier A. **NOTHING DOWNSTREAM CONSUMES IT, and the app says so against itself:** the panel comment records that `sdBizSnapshot()` *“has always written a KPI capture to sd_business_snapshots and NOTHING read it back except a ‘last snapshot’ date”*. No pricing path, no billing path, no gate. Losing a snapshot loses a historical view, not a record of business. **ONE HAZARD RECORDED RATHER THAN TIERED ON, because it is real and is not a tier argument:** the values are SCRAPED RENDERED TEXT, so a snapshot preserves whatever the cell displayed — including a placeholder like `--` or a stale render — as though it were a measurement, and it is capped locally at 90 by `sdCapLocal()` rather than deleted. **NAMED TRIGGER: the day anything COMPUTES from a snapshot rather than displaying it, re-read this row** — at that moment a scraped string becomes an input |
| `sd_crm` | **A** | **A** | An invented pipeline is presented as the shop's own -- WHICH ALREADY HAPPENED. A wrong or absent `val` also moves a Pipeline Value KPI an owner makes staffing and investment decisions from | A NAMED PROSPECT, THEIR PHONE NUMBER, their project and what this shop thinks they will spend. The app restricts the row PER EMPLOYEE, server-side, which is the platform saying out loud that it is not ordinary operational data | **RE-TIERED B -> A on BOTH axes, individually read out of the app 2026-09-23 (cc). THE INTEGRITY LIMB IS THE DOCUMENTED-INCIDENT ONE, not an argument:** `stonedesk.html:30664-30680` records it in the source -- with no local cache and a FAILED server read, `load()` returned 13 invented leads and `render()` computed five KPIs off them, *"including a **$35,900 pipeline** and a conversion rate. A shop whose sync had failed was shown somebody's demo pipeline as its own, with nothing saying so."* Fixed 2026-09-04 by the `_crmReadFailed` guard; the row qualifies under the third A limb regardless. **AND THE MONEY LIMB IS REAL TOO:** `val` is a per-lead dollar estimate entered at `:30816`, summed into the Pipeline KPI at `:30764` and averaged into Avg Deal at `:30768`, rendered per row and exported to CSV (`:30870`). **CONFIDENTIALITY-A ON TWO GROUNDS.** The row carries a prospect's name and phone (`:30636-30645`), so the default sentence's *"no PII on this row"* is false. And `api/sd-data.js:2866-2887` narrows the READ per employee -- management sees every lead, anybody else sees only leads assigned to them, and an UNASSIGNED lead is management-only -- enforced server-side with the client-side copy documented as UI convenience only. **A RESOURCE THE PLATFORM GATES ROW BY ROW IS NOT OPERATIONAL DATA**, the same reasoning `sc_auth_requests` carries for its sign-off gate, and it puts this row in the `sen_clients` / `sdn_clients` / `bld_bids` assignment-gated family the handler itself names at `:5539` |
| `sd_public_shop` | **B** | **B** | The public shop listing wrong | No elevated confidentiality class -- no PII, PHI, privileged communication, or financial-account detail on this row. Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read | Public-facing catalogue content; the WRITE path is `sd_quote_requests`, tiered A above |
| `locations` | **B** | **B** | A location record wrong | No elevated confidentiality class -- no PII, PHI, privileged communication, or financial-account detail on this row. Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read | Shared operational reference; `api/_lib/dnt-location.js` stamps location on writes elsewhere |
| `remnants` | **B** | **B** | Remnant stock wrong | No elevated confidentiality class -- no PII, PHI, privileged communication, or financial-account detail on this row. Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read | Operational inventory |
| `supplier_lead_times` | **B** | **B** | A lead time wrong | No elevated confidentiality class -- no PII, PHI, privileged communication, or financial-account detail on this row. Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read | Operational supplier reference |
| `exec_context` | **A** | **A** | The firm's own confidential position leaks, or is acted on while wrong | The firm's own confidential position is disclosed -- the material a competitor, a counterparty or a filing adversary would act on, and which cannot be un-disclosed | **RE-TIERED B -> A 2026-09-22**, hover-audit confirmed. Not "executive context" in the abstract: this resource carries the CHART OF ACCOUNTS, FUNDRAISING TARGETS, STRIPE PRICING INTERNALS, the SECURITY ARCHITECTURE and PROVISIONAL-PATENT FILING AND DEADLINE DATES. Money and a legally time-barred record in one row, which is the stated A rule twice over. **AND THE TIER IS ARGUED BY ITS OWN HISTORY:** it is owner/admin-only precisely because it used to LEAK VIA VIEW SOURCE before being pulled out of `stonedesk.html` -- a resource whose gate exists because of a real disclosure cannot be classified as operational data nobody has looked at. A patent filing date that is wrong or missed is not recoverable by re-entering it. `api/sd-data-exec-context.test.js` covers the endpoint; the tier is about what the endpoint carries. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (FIRM_CONFIDENTIAL)** |
| `style_profile` | **B** | **B** | A style profile resets and an assistant’s prompt loses its per-person tuning. Contained | A named employee’s writing habits are disclosed — observed behaviour, keyed to their employee id. No elevated class, but it IS about a person | **2026-09-23 `*_settings` SWEEP, prompted by `rf_settings`.** **C -> B, ON THE CONFIDENTIALITY AXIS ONLY, BECAUSE THE OLD CELL SAID SOMETHING FALSE.** It read *“nothing on the row is about a person at all”*, and `api/_resources/stonedesk.js:27` records that this resource is **keyed (license_hash, employee_id)**. It is a per-EMPLOYEE behavioural profile: running counts of how that person writes plus a small term tally. **THE INTEGRITY AXIS GENUINELY IS CONTAINED** — a reset profile degrades one prompt and nothing else — so the Tier moves only because it is the worse of the two axes. **AND THE DESIGN IS BETTER THAN THE ROW GAVE IT CREDIT FOR, which is worth recording rather than only the correction:** `api/_lib/style-profile.js` stores NO raw text, the client analyses locally and posts deltas, and `style-profile.test.js` asserts against a sentence of known rare words that no message can be reconstructed. That is why this is B and not higher |

## The other fifteen apps

### `sairnbiz` — 12 resources

| Resource | Tier | Confidentiality | Worst consequence if it is wrong or lost | Worst consequence if it is read by the wrong person | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `sb_ap` | **A** | **B** | Money is paid to the wrong vendor, twice, or a bill is settled that was never owed | A competitor or an unauthorised employee learns the company's own commercial figures. Commercially sensitive, not an elevated class: no PII, PHI, privileged communication or financial-account detail belonging to a third party sits on this row | **RE-TIERED B &rarr; A 2026-09-22 (cc) on the MONEY limb, not the incident clause.** The B sentence read *&ldquo;neither money nor a regulated record&rdquo;* over ACCOUNTS PAYABLE. Read rather than assumed: the rows carry `vendor`, `inv`, `due`, `amt`, `bal` and `status` (Open / Paid / Overdue), and `sbPayBill()` at `sairnbiz.html:3678` WRITES `status`, `bal` and `paidDate` &mdash; so this is not a passive list of bills, it is the record of which ones have been settled. A wrong row here is a supplier paid twice or not at all. **It already carries a real guard for exactly that reason:** the file&rsquo;s own comment at :2039 records that the Pay button stopped being a no-op, and sbPayBill refuses a bill unless BOTH the stored verdict and a fresh evaluation agree, so a PO edited or deleted after the fact cannot make a held bill payable. A resource with a three-way payment gate written into it is not *&ldquo;operational data lost or wrong&rdquo;*. Same correction as `bld_change_orders`, `rf_draws`, `bld_draws` and `law_pimedical` &mdash; four rows in two days found carrying money under that one sentence. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (MONEY_INTERNAL)** |
| `sb_bud` | **A** | **B** | Spending decisions are made against a budget position that is wrong | A competitor or an unauthorised employee learns the company's own commercial figures. Commercially sensitive, not an elevated class: no PII, PHI, privileged communication or financial-account detail belonging to a third party sits on this row | **RE-TIERED B &rarr; A 2026-09-22 (cc) on the UPSTREAM-OF-MONEY limb, and that is a weaker claim than the other two &mdash; stated so a reader can disagree with it specifically.** `sb_bud` holds `{cat, annual, actual}` per category, and `sairnbiz.html:3748` says `sb_bud.actual` is a YEAR-TO-DATE figure. No money moves when a row is wrong. What moves is the decision: an `actual` understated is headroom that is not there, and the A criteria are explicit that they cover *anything upstream of money* rather than money alone. **THE HONEST COUNTER-ARGUMENT, since nobody else will make it:** budgets are advisory in many businesses and a wrong figure is noticed at the next reconciliation. What decides it here is that the file has ALREADY been repointed once for this exact hazard &mdash; the comment at :2050 records that budget figures now come from `sbBudgetActuals()` rather than from `sb_bud.actual` directly, because reading the stored figure was producing wrong answers. A field a session has already had to route around is not one to leave under *&ldquo;operational data lost or wrong&rdquo;*. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (MONEY_INTERNAL)** |
| `sb_exps` | **A** | **B** | An expense is lost, duplicated or recorded at the wrong amount, and net profit, budget actuals and the deductible total are all wrong with it | A competitor or an unauthorised employee learns the company&rsquo;s own commercial figures. Commercially sensitive, not an elevated class: no PII, PHI, privileged communication or third-party financial-account detail sits on this row | **RE-TIERED B &rarr; A 2026-09-22 (Fourth) on the MONEY limb.** The B sentence read *&ldquo;neither money nor a regulated record&rdquo;* over the EXPENSE LEDGER, and **both clauses are false. READ, NOT INFERRED, at three consumers:** (1) **P&amp;L &mdash; `sairnbiz.html:3469`** splits `sb_exps` by category, sums `cat==="Materials"` into **COGS** and the remainder into opex, and reports `net = revenue &minus; COGS &minus; opex`. A wrong row moves the reported NET PROFIT. (2) **BUDGET ACTUALS &mdash; `sbBudgetActuals()` at `:3840`** adds every `status==="Approved"` row&rsquo;s `amt` to its budget category&rsquo;s actual, with a baseline-date guard against double-counting &mdash; a guard that exists only because these figures are relied on. (3) **A TAX ATTRIBUTE &mdash; `rExps()` at `:3621`** totals `ded==="Yes"` into a deductible figure. **AND IT IS NOT A PASSIVE LIST:** rows carry `status` Pending/Approved and only APPROVED rows reach budget actuals, so there is an approval gate written into the resource for exactly this reason. Same shape as its siblings `sb_ap` (payables) and `sb_invs` (receivables), both re-tiered by cc the same day &mdash; **but this row was not corrected on the pattern: a business&rsquo;s payables, receivables and expenses are the three legs of the same ledger and classifying two as money and the third as operational data would leave the P&amp;L half-protected.** Confidentiality UNCHANGED at B and individually read: `vendor`, `desc`, `cat` and `amt` are the company&rsquo;s own figures, and no third party&rsquo;s identity or account detail is on the row. **Confidentiality individually read 2026-09-22 (MONEY_INTERNAL)** |
| `sb_hire` | **B** | **B** | Operational data lost or wrong | No elevated confidentiality class -- no PII, PHI, privileged communication, or financial-account detail on this row. Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read | Operational data lost or wrong: neither money nor a regulated record. Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read |
| `sb_incidents` | **A** | **A** | An OSHA injury log wrong or incomplete | A named employee's workplace injury is disclosed. The OSHA 300 log is deliberately not the posted summary, and its privacy-case provisions exist because the injury itself is personal medical information | REGULATED. The OSHA Form 300 log built in `fb85a4d9` after the open-work row found the Safety tile counting a collection nothing could write. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (OSHA_LOG)** |
| `sb_invs` | **A** | **B** | A customer is billed the wrong amount, or a payment already received is recorded as outstanding | A competitor or an unauthorised employee learns the company's own commercial figures. Commercially sensitive, not an elevated class: no PII, PHI, privileged communication or financial-account detail belonging to a third party sits on this row | **RE-TIERED B &rarr; A 2026-09-22 (cc) on the MONEY limb.** ACCOUNTS RECEIVABLE, under the same *&ldquo;neither money&rdquo;* sentence. Read: rows carry `cust`, `date`, `due`, `amt`, `paid`, `status` (Paid / Sent / Partial / Overdue / Draft) and `paidDate`. `amt` and `paid` are separate fields, which is what makes this the ledger of what a customer OWES rather than a list of documents sent &mdash; a wrong `paid` is a debt collected twice or written off unpaid. **The A criteria name money and anything upstream of money; this is money itself.** Paired with `sb_ap` deliberately: a business&rsquo;s payables and receivables classified apart would be the same resource tiered two ways. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (MONEY_INTERNAL)** |
| `sb_payruns` | **A** | **A** | A payroll run wrong | An identified employee's pay is disclosed. Individual compensation is the one personnel field whose disclosure is itself the harm, independently of whether the figure is right | Money, and the most consequential kind -- payroll. `sairnbiz.html` is also the only writer of the platform-shared `employees` roster. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (COMPENSATION)** |
| `sb_po` | **A** | **B** | A bill paid against a purchase order that does not say what it says | A competitor or an unauthorised employee learns the company's own commercial figures. Commercially sensitive, not an elevated class: no PII, PHI, privileged communication or financial-account detail belonging to a third party sits on this row | MONEY, and upstream of it. A purchase order is a priced COMMITMENT rather than a record of one -- the same rule this register already states for `bld_bids` -- and `sbThreeWayMatch` in `sairnbiz.html` validates the bill amount against this row before `sbPayBill` will settle it. Wrong in one direction it refuses a correct payment; wrong in the other it authorises one nobody committed to. Added 2026-09-14 with `sql/sairnbiz_po_recv_migration.sql`, when the collection first reached a server at all. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (MONEY_INTERNAL)** |
| `sb_recv` | **A** | **B** | A bill paid in full for goods that never arrived | A competitor or an unauthorised employee learns the company's own commercial figures. Commercially sensitive, not an elevated class: no PII, PHI, privileged communication or financial-account detail belonging to a third party sits on this row | MONEY. The receipt is the only record of what ACTUALLY ARRIVED, and `sbThreeWayMatch` compares the bill against the summed receipts for the PO -- so this row is the half of the control that catches a full invoice for a partial delivery. `sql/sairnbiz_po_recv_migration.sql` gave it a server for the first time on 2026-09-14; before that the check ran entirely on one workstation. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (MONEY_INTERNAL)** |
| `sb_perf` | **A** | **A** | A review row is wrong -- a score, a due date, a raise or PIP flag misrecorded; correctable operational data, and no money computes from it | A NAMED EMPLOYEE's performance review: `score`, `raise` flag, **`pip` flag** -- whether a colleague is on a performance-improvement plan is exactly the fact a coworker has no business reading | **RE-TIERED B -> A on CONFIDENTIALITY, 2026-09-25 (H1 log #533, corroborated H2 seq #217, read individually out of the app).** READ OUT OF THE APP: rows are `{emp, type, due, rev, score, raise, pip, status}` (`sairnbiz.html:448`, exported with those headers at `:4714`). **THE REGISTER ALREADY KNEW AND SAID SO ON THE ADJACENT ROW:** `sb_train`'s cell cites sb_perf's sensitivity as evidence while this row still carried "no PII... never individually read" -- the register disagreeing with itself one row apart. **AND THE GATE LANDED WITH THE TIER, 2026-09-25:** sb_perf rode the generic SAIRNbiz dispatch (session required, NO role check), so any signed-in `staff` employee could read every coworker's score and PIP flag. `api/sd-data.js` now refuses read AND write outside `{owner, hr, manager}` (403 FORBIDDEN, the SAIRNsenior minimum-necessary pattern; `accounting` deliberately out -- its business here is `sb_ap`, not personnel files), driven both directions plus a control proving `sb_incidents` still answers 200 for staff. Integrity stays B, individually read: no money or regulated verdict computes from a review row |
| `sb_ts` | **A** | **A** | Hours wrong, and the wages computed from them wrong | An identified employee's pay is disclosed. Individual compensation is the one personnel field whose disclosure is itself the harm, independently of whether the figure is right | MONEY, and the only record of it. Recorded timesheet hours are what `rTS()` multiplies by each employee's pay rate, and **nothing else in the app could reconstruct a week that was lost** -- an invoice has a customer copy, a PO has a vendor, a week of somebody's hours has neither. Added 2026-09-15 with `saveTimesheet()`, the write path this panel never had: before it, `rTS()` built five KPIs and every cell from a hardcoded array indexed by employee POSITION, so a real pay rate was multiplied by an invented week and deactivating one employee moved another's overtime onto them. `sql/sairnbiz_timesheet_schema.sql`; one row is one employee's one week, keyed `<emp>\|<week>` with the id DERIVED SERVER-SIDE and a unique constraint, because two disagreeing timesheets for one week is a payroll dispute with no tiebreaker. **No delete grant**, matching every non-`sc_*` schema since 2026-08-25 -- correcting a week is a re-save. **THE ROW WAS MISSING FOR SIX HOURS AND `criticality_tier_check.py` IS WHAT CAUGHT IT**: the resource was registered on 2026-09-15 and the register was not updated in the same change, which is the drift this file's own checker exists to refuse. Write gating matches `sb_payruns` -- session required, no role gate -- deliberately rather than by omission: payroll RUNS are already on that footing and splitting the two would be a decision nobody made. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (COMPENSATION). Confidentiality-A because the row's whole purpose is computing an identified employee's wages -- `rTS()` multiplies these hours by that person's pay rate. Hours alone would be B; hours keyed to one person and one week, as the input to their pay, are compensation data** |
| `sb_train` | **A** | **A** | A certification expiry is wrong, and a named employee shows as current on a safety qualification they no longer hold | A named employee’s training history is disclosed together with what the employer spent on them — employment data about an identified person, in the same panel as their performance score | **RE-TIERED B -> A on BOTH axes, 2026-09-23.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `sairnbiz.html:1976` seeds `{emp, cert, done, exp, prov, cost, hrs}` and `saveCertRenewal()` at `:4342` writes the renewal. **`exp` IS NOT A LABEL, IT IS THE ALARM.** `sbCertStatus()` derives the status from `exp` on every render — the row no longer stores a status word at all — and `checkAttentionItems()` at `:2053` raises a CRITICAL finding off it. So a wrong expiry does not mislabel a record, it **removes a person from the expiry alert entirely**, which is the one failure nothing on the screen reports. REGULATED: the seeded certs are *“OSHA 30-Hour Construction”*, *“OSHA 10-Hour”* and *“Forklift Operator Certification”* — powered-industrial-truck operator certification is an OSHA requirement an employer must be able to evidence for a NAMED operator, and this is that evidence. MONEY, secondary but real: `cost` is a dollar figure (180, 250, 95, 320 in the seed) and renewals ACCUMULATE onto it rather than replacing it, so the row is the running spend on that person's certification. **THE CONFIDENTIALITY CALL IS A REAL FORK AND IS MADE EXPLICITLY, because this register carries BOTH lines and picking one silently would be the defect.** `rf_certifications`, `alf_staff_credentials` and `sd_hr_certs` are Confidentiality-B on the ground that a professional licence is *“a public fact by design … what a customer or an inspector is meant to be shown”*. `grd_training_completions` and `bld_tna` are Confidentiality-A on the ground that a named worker’s training record is employment data. **sb_train is on the second line, and three things put it there:** it carries `cost` and `hrs` PER NAMED EMPLOYEE, which is the employer’s spend on that person and is nobody’s public fact; it includes purely internal training no inspector ever sees (`prov:'Internal'`, *“Wet Saw Operations”*); and it lives in the HR panel beside `sb_perf`, which holds review scores, raise flags and PIP flags. **NOT compensation, and that boundary is stated too** — `sen_pay_rates` and `sb_ts` are A because the row IS somebody’s pay; training spend is the company’s expense, not the employee’s income |
| `sb_vends` | **B** | **B** | A vendor contact row is wrong or lost -- correctable operational data | A vendor's business contact details and W-9 status -- commercially sensitive, not an elevated class | **CONFIRMED B/B, INDIVIDUALLY READ 2026-09-25 (H1 log #533) -- AND THE PROMOTION CASE DIED THE DAY BEFORE THE READ, which is why reading beats queueing.** The B -> A candidate argument was that `ytd` is real dollars and the "1099 Required -- $600+" KPI computes from it. **THAT WAS TRUE UNTIL 2026-09-24 AND IS NOT NOW:** the dead-YTD wiring fix (H2 seq #211) rerouted the KPI to derive from PAID `sb_ap` bills -- `sbVendorPaidYTD()` at `sairnbiz.html:3756` -- because the stored `ytd` was written only by the demo seed and by saveVendor-as-zero, i.e. it was the fabricated-KPI class, not live money. The stored field remains on the row shape for sync compatibility and NOTHING DISPLAYS IT (`:3745`, the fix's own comment). The money truth lives on `sb_ap`, which is already Tier A. NAMED TRIGGER: if any pricing or filing path ever reads `sb_vends.ytd` again, re-read this row -- that is the exact resurrection the fix's comment warns against |

### `sairnbuild` — 32 resources

| Resource | Tier | Confidentiality | Worst consequence if it is wrong or lost | Worst consequence if it is read by the wrong person | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `bld_bids` | **A** | **B** | A bid submitted at the wrong price | A competitor or an unauthorised employee learns the company's own commercial figures. Commercially sensitive, not an elevated class: no PII, PHI, privileged communication or financial-account detail belonging to a third party sits on this row | Money, and a bid is a priced commitment rather than a record of one. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (MONEY_INTERNAL)** |
| `bld_change_orders` | **A** | **B** | A change order lost, altered or recorded against the wrong job moves the CONTRACT VALUE, and the job totals derived from it | A competitor or an unauthorised employee learns the company's own commercial figures. Commercially sensitive, not an elevated class: no PII, PHI, privileged communication or financial-account detail belonging to a third party sits on this row | **RE-TIERED 2026-09-21 on the first A criterion — money, and anything upstream of money.** It was **B** on *"neither money nor a regulated record"*, and that sentence is simply false about this resource: `sairnbuild.html:3150-3153` stores a real dollar `amount` on every change order (6800, 4250, 11400, 8900, 5600 in the seed alone), and the app's own comment four lines above says why it matters — *"The accepted values feed Job Board / Dashboard through revisedValue(), so a change order is not a decorative log -- it moves the contract."* **The file being re-tiered and the file stating the contradiction were the same file.** **HOW IT GOT THE WRONG TIER, which is the part worth keeping:** the B rule is applied *by rule, not by reading* — the old evidence cell says so in its own words — so `bld_change_orders` was never opened, and a row that carries money was classified by a sentence asserting it does not. Same shape as `sd_exec_msgs` the same day: a B sentence that was true of the wrong axis. **DIFFERENT CRITERION THOUGH, and stated rather than borrowed:** `sd_exec_msgs` moved on the incident clause, with a reproduced exposure behind it. This one moves on the money clause and has **no incident** — it is a tier correction, not a finding, and calling it one would overstate it. **NOT a general promotion of the bld_ family:** `bld_draws` is already A on its own evidence; `bld_bids` and the rest stay B, because a bid is a proposal and a change order is an executed amendment to a signed contract. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (MONEY_INTERNAL)** |
| `bld_checks` | **A** | **A** | A payment issued is lost or wrong, and money is paid twice or not at all | Who was paid what, and the account detail it was paid on, is disclosed | **RE-TIERED B -> A on BOTH axes, 2026-09-22.** MONEY MOVES. The registry calls it &ldquo;Payments issued&rdquo;, which is the definition of the A rule&rsquo;s first clause rather than an interpretation of it. A duplicate or missing check is not recoverable by re-deriving it from anything else in the app. **Confidentiality individually read 2026-09-22** |
| `bld_comm_log` | **B** | **B** | A client message is lost -- the thread recording what was agreed with the homeowner is incomplete | A private builder-to-homeowner conversation is read by the wrong person. Commercially sensitive, but no elevated class: not privileged, not health data, not compensation, no government identifier | **INDIVIDUALLY READ OUT OF THE APP 2026-09-23 (Hank)** in the full SAIRNbuild re-audit -- all 15 `bld_` rows that carried *"Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read"*. **CONFIRMED AT B/B on an individual read, not re-classified by the rule.** `saveMsg()` at `sairnbuild.html:6790` writes `{id, job_id, date, author, message}`, where `author` is one of PM / Client / Office. **THE CONFIDENTIALITY FLAG ON THIS ROW WAS RAISED BY `tools/confidentiality_candidate_flagger.py` ON THE SIGNAL `name`, AND THE READ DOES NOT SUPPORT IT.** The Client Portal is an IN-APP panel (`nav('clientportal')` at `:299`, `rClientPortal()` at `:6759`) -- there is no external client login, no share token and no published URL, so there is no second party holding this data. The panel's own header comment at `:6752` records that cost, margin and internal notes are deliberately never rendered there. **THE ARGUMENT THAT WOULD HAVE PROMOTED IT IS NAMED AND REJECTED:** `message` is free text and free text CAN contain anything. That reasoning would make every `notes` column on this platform Confidentiality-A, and it is not the rule this register applies -- `rf_proposals` was promoted on a field DOCUMENTED IN ITS SCHEMA (`signature_data`), not on what free text might hold |
| `bld_costs` | **A** | **B** | A job cost wrong, and every bid built from it wrong | A competitor or an unauthorised employee learns the company's own commercial figures. Commercially sensitive, not an elevated class: no PII, PHI, privileged communication or financial-account detail belonging to a third party sits on this row | Money, upstream: five client write sites, and costs are what bids are computed from. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (MONEY_INTERNAL)** |
| `bld_daily_logs` | **B** | **B** | A daily log is lost -- the contemporaneous site record that would support a weather or delay position is incomplete | No elevated confidentiality class -- weather, a crew HEADCOUNT, a photo count and a free-text description of the day's work. No worker is named | **INDIVIDUALLY READ OUT OF THE APP 2026-09-23 (Hank)** in the full SAIRNbuild re-audit -- all 15 `bld_` rows that carried *"Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read"*. **CONFIRMED AT B/B on an individual read, not re-classified by the rule.** `sairnbuild.html:3254` seeds `{id, job_id, log_date, weather, crew, photos, work, delays}`. `crew:4` is a COUNT, not a roster, so nothing on the row identifies a worker. **THE STRONGEST A ARGUMENT IS NAMED AND REJECTED:** a daily log is the standard evidence for a weather-delay time extension, and a time extension is money. But `delays` is a SENTENCE (*"Rain delayed exterior work by half a day"*), nothing in the app computes from it, and the row carries no amount and no day count -- so the money is downstream of a human reading it, which is not the *"pricing that feeds them"* the A rule states. The KPIs it drives are This-Week and No-Recent-Log, both counts |
| `bld_deliveries` | **B** | **B** | A delivery record is wrong -- a purchase order shows as tracked when nothing was received, and the one gap alarm on it goes quiet | No elevated confidentiality class -- a PO reference, a job, two dates, a status and a note. No individual and no price | **INDIVIDUALLY READ OUT OF THE APP 2026-09-23 (Hank)** in the full SAIRNbuild re-audit -- all 15 `bld_` rows that carried *"Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read"*. **CONFIRMED AT B/B on an individual read, not re-classified by the rule.** **AND THIS IS THE CLOSEST CALL OF THE FIFTEEN, so the argument FOR promotion is recorded rather than omitted.** `saveDelivery()` at `sairnbuild.html:5967` writes `{id, po_id, job_id, expected_at, received_at, status, notes}`. `sb_recv` in SAIRNbiz is A/B on what looks like exactly this shape -- *"a bill paid in full for goods that never arrived"* -- so sibling consistency argues for A. **IT DOES NOT CARRY, AND THE DIFFERENCE IS MEASURABLE RATHER THAN A MATTER OF TASTE:** `sb_recv` is A because `sbThreeWayMatch` SUMS receipt quantities and compares them against a bill. This row has NO quantity and NO amount, and the only thing reading it across datasets is `posMissingDelivery()` at `:5804` -- a PRESENCE test driving the *"No Delivery Tracked"* count at `:813`. Nothing here feeds a payment. **THE REAL FINDING IS A PRODUCT GAP, NOT A TIER:** SAIRNbuild has priced POs (`bld_pos`, A) and a check register (`bld_checks`, A) and NO three-way match between them, so the receiving control is presence-only. Filed in `docs/SAIRN-OPEN-WORK-INDEX.md`. **If a quantity or a match ever lands on this row, it becomes A on the sentence `sb_recv` already carries** |
| `bld_documents` | **B** | **B** | A document version-history entry is lost -- an RFI, submittal or inspection citing a specific version points at nothing | No elevated confidentiality class -- names, categories and version notes only. THE DOCUMENT ITSELF IS NOT STORED ON THIS ROW | **INDIVIDUALLY READ OUT OF THE APP 2026-09-23 (Hank)** in the full SAIRNbuild re-audit -- all 15 `bld_` rows that carried *"Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read"*. **CONFIRMED AT B/B on an individual read, not re-classified by the rule.** `sairnbuild.html:3433` seeds `{id, job_id, name, category, versions:[{v, uploaded_at, uploaded_by, note}]}`. **THERE IS NO PAYLOAD: no URL, no blob, no file reference.** This is a version INDEX, so losing a row loses the history and not the drawing, which is what keeps both axes at B. **TWO A ARGUMENTS WERE CONSIDERED AND BOTH FAIL ON THE READ:** (1) `DOC-03` is *"Building Permit WL-26-0412"* with `uploaded_by:"City of Westlake"` -- a government authorisation -- but the authoritative copy is the city&rsquo;s and this row is metadata about a copy that is not here. (2) `rf_prequal_documents` was promoted B -> A on 2026-09-23 and is the closest sibling, but it was promoted for `expires_on` and `value` -- an expiry that lapses and a dollar figure -- and NEITHER FIELD EXISTS HERE. The cross-reference integrity is real (`ref_doc_id`/`ref_doc_v` on `bld_rfis`, `bld_submittals` and `bld_inspections`) and it is operational, not money and not regulated |
| `bld_draws` | **A** | **B** | A payment draw lost or altered, or a retainage release recorded that the store never accepted | A competitor or an unauthorised employee learns the company's own commercial figures. Commercially sensitive, not an elevated class: no PII, PHI, privileged communication or financial-account detail belonging to a third party sits on this row | **RE-TIERED 2026-09-22 on the first A criterion &mdash; money.** Same false B sentence as `rf_draws` above, same engine: `api/_lib/wip-accounting.js` names `bld_draws` explicitly &mdash; *"SAIRNbuild's bld_draws stores both `retainage_pct` AND `retainage_held`, which can disagree the moment anyone edits one"*. **TWO SEPARATE FIXES LANDED ON THIS RESOURCE ON 2026-09-21 ALONE,** both about money not surviving: `b5e20fe8` closed a check-then-write race where *"a retainage release could be lost"*, replacing a read-then-PATCH with `bld_release_retainage_atomic` under an advisory lock; and the same branch previously answered `ok:true` **while handing back the audit trail for a release the store never confirmed**. **A resource with two money-loss fixes in one day cannot be carrying "neither money nor a regulated record".** No incident clause needed and none claimed &mdash; it qualifies on the money criterion alone. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (MONEY_INTERNAL)** |
| `bld_equipment` | **A** | **B** | A rental accrual is wrong, so a job carries a cost it did not incur or misses one it did | Equipment names, rental rates and utilisation are disclosed. The company&rsquo;s own commercial figures &mdash; commercially sensitive, not an elevated class: no PII, PHI, privileged communication or third-party account detail sits on this row | **RE-TIERED B &rarr; A on the MONEY limb 2026-09-22 (Fourth).** `equipAccrual()` at `sairnbuild.html:6520` returns `days &times; rental_rate` for any row where `type===&#39;rented&#39;` and `status===&#39;active&#39;`, recomputed against `Date.now()` on every render. **So `rental_rate` and `rental_start` are not attributes of an asset, they are the inputs to a running charge against `job_id`** &mdash; a wrong rate or a wrong start date moves a job&rsquo;s cost every day it stays wrong, silently, because the figure is computed rather than stored. The seed&rsquo;s own comment at `:3388` says the accrual is *&ldquo;a genuine computed number, not a placeholder&rdquo;*. Same correction as `rf_draws` and `bld_draws`, on a row whose money is derived rather than entered. **Confidentiality individually read 2026-09-22 (COMMERCIAL_INTERNAL)** |
| `bld_incidents` | **A** | **A** | A safety incident log wrong | A named employee's workplace injury is disclosed. The OSHA 300 log is deliberately not the posted summary, and its privacy-case provisions exist because the injury itself is personal medical information | REGULATED, same OSHA shape as SAIRNbiz. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (OSHA_LOG)** |
| `bld_inspections` | **A** | **B** | A municipal inspection result is wrong or lost -- work is covered on a pass that never happened, or a failed item never gets re-inspected | No elevated confidentiality class -- the row names no individual at all: job, inspection type, scheduled date, result, re-inspection date and a defect note | **INDIVIDUALLY READ OUT OF THE APP 2026-09-23 (Hank)** in the full SAIRNbuild re-audit -- all 15 `bld_` rows that carried *"Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read"*. **RE-TIERED B -> A on the TIER axis; confidentiality individually read and left at B.** `saveInsp()` at `sairnbuild.html:4290` writes `{id, job_id, type, scheduled, result, reinsp, notes, ref_doc_id, ref_doc_v}`. **`type` IS WHAT SETTLES THIS, and it is the app's own vocabulary rather than an inference:** the seeded values at `:3231` are *"Rough Electrical"*, *"Framing"* and *"Final"* -- these are MUNICIPAL CODE INSPECTIONS, not internal QA, and `result` is the building official's code-compliance determination. A pass is what permits work to be COVERED UP or occupied, so a pass recorded that did not happen is not a wrong label, it is framing and rough-in closed behind drywall with no sign-off. **AND THE SEEDED FAIL IS A LIFE-SAFETY ITEM:** `INS-03` is *"Failed on missing arc-fault breaker in primary bedroom circuit"*; an AFCI is an NEC requirement, and `saveInsp()` REFUSES a fail with no re-inspection date (`:4295`) -- so the app itself already treats the fail-to-reinspection link as load-bearing. A lost fail is that re-inspection never happening. **Same REGULATED ground the register already gives `bld_incidents` (A/A) in this same app**; the axes differ only because no person is named here |
| `bld_jobs` | **A** | **A** | The central job record is lost or wrong, and every cost, draw and change order hangs off it | A named client&rsquo;s identity, site address and contract terms are disclosed | **RE-TIERED B -> A on BOTH axes, 2026-09-22.** BOTH AXES, AND THIS IS THE ROW THE OTHERS DEPEND ON. The file&rsquo;s own registry calls it &ldquo;THE BUSINESS RECORD&rdquo;, and `bld_costs`, `bld_draws`, `bld_change_orders` and `bld_lien_waivers` are all keyed to it -- a wrong or lost job row does not damage one record, it orphans the financial chain built on it. On the confidentiality axis it is the platform&rsquo;s holding of a named client&rsquo;s SITE ADDRESS joined to their contract value, which for a residential build is a private individual&rsquo;s home address beside what they are paying. **Confidentiality individually read 2026-09-22** |
| `bld_lien_waivers` | **A** | **A** | A lien waiver is lost or wrong, and a lien right is released or asserted that should not be | Which party waived what, on which job and for what consideration, is disclosed | **RE-TIERED B -> A on BOTH axes, 2026-09-22.** THE REGISTRY ALREADY SAYS IT: &ldquo;Legal instruments. A lost waiver is a real financial exposure.&rdquo; That sentence has been in the code since the resource was added and the register carried the row at B anyway -- a legal instrument is a regulated record under this table&rsquo;s own A rule, and the B sentence &ldquo;neither money nor a regulated record&rdquo; was false about it from the first day. **Confidentiality individually read 2026-09-22** |
| `bld_photo_analyses` | **A** | **A** | An AI site-photo finding is lost or wrong, and a site condition is recorded or missed on it | What an AI observed at a named client&rsquo;s site is disclosed | **RE-TIERED B -> A on BOTH axes, 2026-09-22.** The registry notes text only, no image bytes -- which bounds the disclosure but does not remove it: the TEXT is what the model saw at an identified site. On the integrity axis this is contemporaneous site evidence of the same class as `bld_daily_logs`, which the registry itself calls &ldquo;what construction disputes turn on&rdquo;. **Confidentiality individually read 2026-09-22** |
| `bld_pos` | **A** | **B** | A purchase order wrong on its amount, its vendor or its job -- a priced commitment the company is held to | A competitor or an unauthorised employee learns what the company committed to a vendor for. Commercially sensitive, not an elevated class: the row names a VENDOR and a job cost code, not a protected individual | **RE-TIERED B &rarr; A 2026-09-22 on the MONEY limb; confidentiality stays B.** `sairnbuild.html:5875` writes `{id, job_id, cost_code, vendor, description, amount, status, issued_at}` with real dollar amounts -- 18400, 11200, 9800, 42000 in the seed alone. **A PURCHASE ORDER IS A PRICED COMMITMENT RATHER THAN A RECORD OF ONE**, which is the rule this register already states for `sb_po` (*"MONEY, and upstream of it"*) and for `bld_bids`. Same app and same argument as `bld_change_orders`, re-tiered on 2026-09-21 because *"`sairnbuild.html` stores a real dollar `amount` on every change order"* -- a PO is the other half of that ledger and was left under a sentence asserting it holds no money. **NOT promoted on confidentiality:** the counterparty is a supplier, not a person, and nothing on the row is PII, PHI, privileged or an individual's financial-account detail |
| `bld_price_points` | **A** | **B** | Pricing wrong across every future bid | A competitor or an unauthorised employee learns the company's own commercial figures. Commercially sensitive, not an elevated class: no PII, PHI, privileged communication or financial-account detail belonging to a third party sits on this row | Money, upstream of bids. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (MONEY_INTERNAL)** |
| `bld_punchlist` | **B** | **B** | A punch item is lost -- a close-out defect is not tracked to completion | No elevated confidentiality class -- a defect description, the assigned party (a company or an in-house crew), a date and a status | **INDIVIDUALLY READ OUT OF THE APP 2026-09-23 (Hank)** in the full SAIRNbuild re-audit -- all 15 `bld_` rows that carried *"Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read"*. **CONFIRMED AT B/B on an individual read, not re-classified by the rule.** `sairnbuild.html:3223` seeds `{id, job_id, item, assigned, noted, status}`. Neither money nor a regulated record: no amount, no code determination, no individual. `assigned` names a COMPANY (*"ABC Cabinetry"*, *"Delgado Plumbing Co"*) or a crew, never a person. The nearest thing to a consequence is the Avg Days Open KPI, which is a count |
| `bld_referrals` | **A** | **A** | A referral recorded against the wrong customer, or a converted deal value wrong | The PHONE NUMBER of a private individual who is NOT a customer is disclosed, together with who referred them -- a social graph of which household recommended which other household | **RE-TIERED B &rarr; A 2026-09-22, BOTH AXES, AND THIS ROW WAS DELIBERATELY HELD BY ANOTHER SESSION ON 2026-09-22 AS "an opaque referral-source blob" THAT NO HONEST TIER A ARGUMENT COULD BE MADE FOR.** That call was made in good faith and the refusal to manufacture an argument was right in principle; the CHARACTERISATION is what the seed contradicts. `sairnbuild.html:3468` writes `{from_customer, to_customer, TO_PHONE, status, deal_value, date}` -- for example `{from_customer:'Hartley Residence', to_customer:'Reyes Residence', to_phone:'(440) 555-0341', status:'lead'}`. **A `lead` OR `lost` ROW IS A PRIVATE INDIVIDUAL'S PHONE NUMBER HELD BY A BUSINESS THEY NEVER ENGAGED**, obtained from somebody else, stored beside the name of who gave it up. That is third-party PII plus a relationship graph, which is a stronger confidentiality claim than `sd_customers` -- a customer at least chose to transact. The integrity limb is the weaker one: `deal_value` is money but a referral value is soft, and the Tier follows the confidentiality axis as the worse of the two |
| `bld_reviews` | **B** | **B** | A review record is lost or wrong -- reputation reporting is incomplete and an unanswered review is missed | No elevated confidentiality class, AND THE DECIDING FACT IS THAT THE CONTENT IS ALREADY PUBLIC: `source` is Google / Yelp / Houzz / Facebook, so the review text is published by the platform it came from | **INDIVIDUALLY READ OUT OF THE APP 2026-09-23 (Hank)** in the full SAIRNbuild re-audit -- all 15 `bld_` rows that carried *"Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read"*. **CONFIRMED AT B/B on an individual read, not re-classified by the rule.** `sairnbuild.html:3456` seeds `{id, job_id, customer, source, rating, date, text, responded}`. **THE ONLY THING NOT ALREADY PUBLIC IS THE JOIN** -- `customer:"Hartley Residence"` tied to an internal `job_id` -- and a customer name against a job is not one of the elevated classes this register names. No money: `rating` and `responded` drive counts, and the KPI definition (unanswered AND <= 4 stars) is a filter, not a figure |

THE JUDGEMENT, made rather than split: activity attendance is the gentlest fact this app records about a resident -- but it is still a record of WHERE AN IDENTIFIABLE RESIDENT WAS and what they did in a care facility, this app's own precedent tiers resident-linked rows A on confidentiality (`alf_mar`, `alf_incidents`, `alf_signals` as of today), and an attendance list is exactly the row a family dispute or an elopement investigation subpoenas. A carve-out for "mild" resident-linked rows would make every future row a negotiation about mildness. Integrity stays B, individually read: scheduling data, correctable, nothing computes from it |

A wrong entry is a wrong invoice. **CLASSIFICATION FIX, NOT A PROTECTION ONE:** the gate is already built and already enforced; what was wrong was the label. **Confidentiality individually read 2026-09-22 (PHI)** |

### `sairncode` — 28 resources

| Resource | Tier | Confidentiality | Worst consequence if it is wrong or lost | Worst consequence if it is read by the wrong person | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `sc_anesthesia` | **A** | **A** | An anaesthesia record is lost or wrong, and a subsequent procedure is planned on it | A named patient&rsquo;s anaesthesia history and reactions are disclosed | **RE-TIERED B -> A on BOTH axes, 2026-09-22.** BOTH AXES. An anaesthesia record states what agents a named patient received and how they responded -- the record a subsequent anaesthetist needs and the one whose absence is dangerous rather than merely inconvenient. It is PHI with a clinical event attached, on the SAIRNcode limb where `sc_claims` and `sc_compliance` are already A. **Confidentiality individually read 2026-09-22** |
| `sc_anesthesia_base_units` | **A** | **B** | A base-unit value is wrong, and every anaesthesia case coded from it afterwards is billed at the wrong amount | Nothing identifiable. A CPT code, a base-unit number and a citation the practice typed. No patient, no provider, no payer, and NOT the conversion factor -- the dollars-per-unit lives on `sc_anesthesia`, not here | **RE-TIERED B -> A on the INTEGRITY axis, individually read out of the app 2026-09-23 (cc). Confidentiality individually read and left at B. LINE CITATIONS RE-VERIFIED AND REPOINTED 2026-09-24 (Hank) while discharging the review obligation on this promotion: all nine had drifted 30-45 lines in under a day -- three sessions edit this 12,000-line file -- and every underlying FACT re-verified true by content before repointing. cc predicted exactly this in the obligation text.** THE ROW IS UPSTREAM OF MONEY AND THE PATH IS FOUR LINES LONG: `addAsaBaseUnit()` at `sairncode.html:4989` stores `{id, cpt, units, source}`; `anLookupBaseUnits()` at `:5008` matches on CPT and writes `match.units` straight into the Base Units field of a new anaesthesia case (`:5014`); `renderAnesthesia()` computes `totalUnits = a.base + timeUnits` (`:4889`) and `dollarAmount = totalUnits * resolvedCf` (`:4904`), renders it per row as currency (`:4906`) and sums it into the **Billed Amount** KPI (`:4905`). A wrong `units` is not a mislabelled reference row, it is a wrong dollar figure on every case that matches that CPT. **SAME LIMB AS `sd_pricing_rules`** -- *"one wrong rule is not one wrong record"* -- and the same shape as `sc_providers`, where a wrong `qpStatus` picks the wrong conversion factor. **AND THE AUTO-FILL ARRIVES WEARING A CITATION**, which is what makes a wrong value hard to catch: the UI says *"Base units filled from your reference table (source: X)"* in green (`:5016`). **TWO THINGS THAT BOUND IT, stated rather than left out:** the filled value stays editable, and it is COPIED into the `sc_anesthesia` row at save, so correcting this table later does not correct cases already entered -- the blast radius is every case entered while the wrong row exists, not every case ever. **LOSS IS HANDLED HONESTLY AND WRONGNESS IS NOT**, the same asymmetry `sc_providers` records: an unmatched CPT says *"No reference entry for this CPT code yet"* (`:5019`) and refuses to invent a number, while a wrong number is detectable by nothing |
| `sc_ar` | **A** | **A** | Receivables wrong | A named person's health-care payment record is disclosed. Payment for the provision of health care to an identified individual is protected health information in its own right, before any clinical detail is attached to it | Money. **Soft-delete-only since 2026-09-15 (item 97):** `api/_resources/sairncode.js` grants `soft_delete` and not `delete`, so the row is marked `_deleted_at` and excluded from reads rather than removed, and recovery needs no backup &mdash; which matters because `docs/2026-09-14-nightly-backup-design.md` records the nightly backup as never having run. The list is pinned to THIS TABLE by `tests/sairncode_gates.js`, both directions.**Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (PHI_PAYMENT)** |
| `sc_auth` | **A** | **B** | An expiration date is wrong or lost, and a lapsed authorisation still reads **Active** -- care is delivered under an authorisation that no longer covers it, and the claim is not payable | An authorisation number, a free-text procedure, a date and a unit count. NO patient and NO payer -- the two fields that make both its siblings Confidentiality-A are absent from this row | **RE-TIERED B -> A on the INTEGRITY axis, individually read out of the app 2026-09-23 (cc). Confidentiality individually read and left at B.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `addAuthEntry()` at `sairncode.html:5268` stores `{id, authId, proc, exp, units}` and the form at `:2493-2496` offers exactly those four fields and nothing else. **`exp` IS THE ALARM, which is why this is integrity-A rather than operational:** `authStatusFor()` at `:5220` derives Active / Expiring Soon / Expired from that date alone, and `renderAuth()` (`:5233`) turns it into the Active / Expiring Soon / Expired KPIs. A wrong date does not mislabel a record, it REMOVES the authorisation from the warning -- **the identical shape the register already gives `sf_vehicles`, `rf_safety_equipment` and `sb_train`**, and the same consequence its SAIRNsenior sibling `sen_authorizations` states: care delivered outside the authorisation. **THE CONFIDENTIALITY SPLIT IS THE JUDGEMENT AND IT IS DELIBERATE.** `sen_authorizations` is A because it carries `client_id`, `client_name` and `payer`; `sc_auth_requests` is A because it carries a named beneficiary and what care was sought. This row carries neither, so a reader learns that SOME authorisation exists for SOME procedure and cannot tie it to a person. `proc` is free text and a coder could type anything into it, but the field is labelled *"Procedure"* and the UI invites no identifier -- tiering on what a user COULD type would make every free-text field A |
| `sc_auth_requests` | **A** | **A** | A prior authorisation submitted wrong, or signed off by somebody not entitled to | A named beneficiary's authorisation request is disclosed -- what care was sought, for what condition, and whether a payer agreed. Money on the first A limb, protected health information on the second |
