| `sc_dme` | **A** | **A** | A DME record wrong on its beneficiary, its HCPCS code or its date of service, and a claim is billed or rejected on it | A Medicare beneficiary is disclosed together with the DURABLE MEDICAL EQUIPMENT billed for them and the date it was supplied, which states a condition as plainly as a diagnosis code does | **RE-TIERED B -> A on BOTH axes, 2026-09-23.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `addDmeRecord()` at `sairncode.html:11684` writes `{beneficiary, hcpcs, desc, dos, mode}`. **`beneficiary` IS CMS’S OWN WORD FOR A MEDICARE ENROLLEE, and the field name is the finding** — this is not a generic reference, it is an identified individual joined to a billed item and a date of service. Same family and same sentence as `sc_ar`, `sc_claims` and `sc_denial`: payment for the provision of health care to an identified individual is protected health information in its own right, before any clinical note is attached. MONEY: the panel exists for the DME MAC modifier rule the app quotes at `:10646` — *“Claim lines billed without a SC, GA, GY, or GZ modifier will be rejected as missing information”* — so a wrong row is a rejected or a wrongly-paid claim. **THE FAMILY TEST IS WHAT FOUND IT:** `sc_hcc` went A/A on a named patient joined to a diagnosis grouping and its dollar value; DME is the same pairing with a device in place of the grouping, and it sat at B beside it |
| `sc_drg` | **A** | **A** | A DRG or its severity tier is wrong, and an inpatient stay is billed on it | A named patient is disclosed together with a DRG -- a diagnosis grouping -- and whether that stay carried a complication or a MAJOR complication | **RE-TIERED B -> A on BOTH axes, 2026-09-23.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `addDrgEntry()` at `sairncode.html:4563` builds `{id, code, patient, tier, payment}`. **THE `tier` FIELD IS WHAT MAKES THIS BOTH AXES AT ONCE.** Its vocabulary is Base / CC / MCC -- complication-or-comorbidity and MAJOR complication-or-comorbidity -- so on the confidentiality axis the row states not merely that a named patient had an inpatient stay in a given diagnosis group, but that the stay was clinically complicated. That is the same pairing `sc_hcc` is already A for, a named patient joined to a diagnosis grouping and to the money it is worth, and `code` here is more specific than an HCC category. On the integrity axis the SAME field is the payment weight: moving a case Base -> CC -> MCC is what raises the amount billed for the identical stay, and `payment` is a real dollar figure that also drives the panel's Avg Payment/Case tile. A wrong tier is not a mis-typed label, it is the shape of an upcoded claim. **Confidentiality individually read 2026-09-23** |
| `sc_eligibility` | **A** | **A** | An eligibility result is lost or wrong, and a claim goes to a payer or plan that does not cover it -- or care is given believing coverage exists | A named patient is disclosed together with their PAYER, PLAN and coverage status, and with the service types the coverage was checked FOR | **RE-TIERED B -> A on BOTH axes, 2026-09-23.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `addEligibilityRecord()` at `sairncode.html:6242` builds `{id, patient, payer, status, plan, encounter_ref, service_types, checked_for, date}` -- RICHER THAN THE ROUTED DESCRIPTION, which named only patient/payer/plan/status. A named person joined to a payer and a plan is PHI on the ground `sc_claims` and `sc_compliance` already carry verbatim: payment for the provision of health care to an identified individual is protected health information in its own right, before any clinical detail is attached. **`service_types` AND `checked_for` GO FURTHER THAN THAT SENTENCE and are the reason this is not a borderline call** -- they record what care was being contemplated for that named person, which is a clinical fact about them and not merely a billing one. Same class as `sc_auth_requests`, already A, whose row is described as *what care was sought, for what condition, and whether a payer agreed*. On the integrity axis `encounter_ref` ties the result to a specific encounter, and this history is the evidence the check was run -- which is what gets produced when a denial is appealed. **WHAT THIS ROW IS NOT, stated so the tier is not read wider than the fact:** the authoritative answer lives at the payer and is re-askable; what is at stake here is the record of having asked, and what was asked about. **Confidentiality individually read 2026-09-23** |
| `sc_encoder` | **B** | **B** | Operational data lost or wrong | No elevated confidentiality class -- no PII, PHI, privileged communication, or financial-account detail on this row. Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read | Operational data lost or wrong: neither money nor a regulated record. Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read |
| `sc_fraud` | **A** | **B** | An amount at risk is lost or wrong, and a loss-prevention figure a practice reports is misstated | A competitor or an unauthorised employee learns the practice's own fraud-exposure figures. Commercially sensitive, not an elevated class: the row carries no name field | **RE-TIERED B -> A on the INTEGRITY axis only, 2026-09-23.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `addFraudAlert()` at `sairncode.html:4220` builds `{id, desc, conf, amount, status}`. Rows whose `status` is `'Verified Fraud'` have their `amount` summed into a **Prevented Loss** KPI, and the write calls `renderDashboard()` so the figure reaches the dashboard as well as the panel. That is MONEY_INTERNAL, the same shape `sd_order_history` and `sd_quote_requests` are already A/B for. **CONFIDENTIALITY LEFT AT B DELIBERATELY, and the reason is stated so it is checkable rather than forgotten:** `desc` is free text describing a suspected billing fraud and in practice will name a provider or a patient -- but the row has **no name field**, and tiering on what a free-text box might contain is a guess. **The distinction this register is drawing, since the neighbouring `sf_gaming_expenses` went the other way the same day:** a REQUIRED IDENTITY FIELD is not a maybe, an optional description is. `desc` would move this to A the day it becomes a structured subject reference. **Confidentiality individually read 2026-09-23** |
| `sc_hcc` | **A** | **A** | A risk-adjustment category or value is wrong, and reimbursement is claimed on it | A named patient is disclosed together with a DIAGNOSIS GROUPING and its dollar value | **RE-TIERED B -> A on BOTH axes, 2026-09-22.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `sairncode.html` builds `{id, cat, patient, value, status}` where `cat` is the HCC category. A Hierarchical Condition Category IS a diagnosis grouping -- it exists to encode how sick a named person is -- so this row is a named patient joined to a diagnosis and to the money that diagnosis is worth. PHI and revenue on one row, which is the pairing `sc_claims` and `sc_compliance` are already Tier A for. **Read from the app, not the schema; confidentiality individually read 2026-09-22** |
| `sc_pctc` | **A** | **B** | A code's PC/TC indicator is wrong, and `runPcTcPathCheck()` renders a WRONG legal-billing instruction -- "Bill globally", "append -26", "append -TC" or "cannot be split" -- on every future check of that code | A CPT-code-to-indicator table; no person on the row | **RE-TIERED B -> A on INTEGRITY, 2026-09-25 (H1 log #534, read individually out of the app -- simply never read before).** READ OUT OF THE APP: `{code, indicator}` rows (`sairncode.html:12041-12049`) drive `runPcTcPathCheck()`, whose output is a DEFINITIVE instruction rendered as a green box with a CMS citation (`:12143-12146`) -- "Bill globally -- no modifier", "Bill PC only -- append -26", "This code cannot be split". **A WRONG INDICATOR IS UNDETECTABLE WHERE AN ABSENT ONE FAILS CLOSED:** a missing code gets no instruction, but a wrong one renders with the same green confidence and the same citation as a right one, on every future check. Same reference-decides-the-verdict shape as `sc_providers`, `sc_anesthesia_base_units` and `sc_auth`, all already promoted on it. Confidentiality stays B, individually read: a code table |
| `sc_prebill` | **A** | **B** | A pre-bill scrub finding is lost or wrong, and a claim goes out with the error still in it | An unauthorised reader learns which claims the practice found errors on and what they were worth. Commercially sensitive, and no individual&rsquo;s record sits on the row &mdash; see the rejected argument in the evidence | **RE-TIERED B -> A on the TIER axis, confidentiality individually read and left at B, 2026-09-23.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `addScrubEntry()` at `sairncode.html:4321` builds `{id, claim, error, value, time, status}`. **TIER A ON THE CLAIMS LIMB and it is not marginal:** a pre-bill scrub is the LAST point an error is cheap. A finding lost here is not a lost note, it is a claim submitted with the error still in it -- which becomes a denial, a rework cost, or on the wrong error a false claim. `value` is the dollars at stake on that specific claim. Same family as `sc_rac`, already Tier A, one step EARLIER in the same pipeline: sc_rac is the audit after the fact, this is the catch before submission. **CONFIDENTIALITY IS THE ARGUABLE HALF AND I CONSIDERED THE OTHER SIDE AND REJECTED IT, which is why both are recorded here.** The argument FOR A: `claim` is a per-claim reference, so unlike `sc_rac` -- whose row is an aggregate dollar figure -- each row points at ONE patient encounter, and a claim number is re-linkable to a patient inside the practice&rsquo;s own systems the way an account number is. The argument AGAINST, which I took: the row carries no name, no diagnosis and no payment instrument, and its identifying power exists ONLY by joining to another system this register tiers separately. Admitting a bare reference number as an elevated class would also admit `job_id`, `matter_id` and every other foreign key on the platform, and the axis would stop discriminating -- the same reasoning settled on `bld_pos` and `sen_franchise_agreements` earlier today. **A reviewer who thinks the account-number reading should win has a real case and this cell is where to argue it**. **Read from the APP, not the schema** |
| `sc_providers` | **A** | **A** | A provider's QP status is wrong, and every unit billed under them is priced at the wrong conversion factor | A named provider is disclosed together with an internal PERFORMANCE judgement of their work | **RE-TIERED B -> A on BOTH axes, 2026-09-23.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `addProviderEntry()` at `sairncode.html:6533` builds `{id, name, specialty, license, cred, qpStatus, perf}`. **THE INTEGRITY ARGUMENT IS THE APP'S OWN SENTENCE, not this register's** -- `sairncode.html:2953` reads *"QP status is not cosmetic: from CY2026 Medicare pays two different conversion factors for the same code depending on it."* `qpStatus` is passed straight to `scConversionFactor()` at `:3725`, which returns **$33.5675 vs $33.4009** (RBRVS) or **$20.5998 vs $20.4976** (anesthesia), sourced in-file to CMS CY2026 PFS Final Rule CMS-1832-F and SSA s1848(d)(20). A wrong `qpStatus` is not a mislabelled field, it is the wrong dollar-per-unit rate on everything billed under that provider. **THE FAIL-CLOSED GUARD IS WHY THIS IS STILL A FINDING RATHER THAN WHY IT IS NOT:** the function returns `null` for unknown and the panel prints *"Unknown -- payment cannot be stated"*, so ABSENCE is handled honestly and a WRONG value is detectable by nothing. **CONFIDENTIALITY IS THE WEAKER HALF AND THIS CELL SAYS SO.** It rests on `perf` alone -- a performance percentage on a named provider, rendered per row and averaged into a KPI -- following `bld_tna`, already A for *"a record of where a NAMED PERSON is judged deficient"*. `license` does NOT carry it: `alf_staff_credentials` settled that a professional licence is a public fact by design. And `perf` is a bare percentage with no stated methodology where `bld_tna` has a structured instrument, so this is the thinner end of that precedent rather than a match to it. **Confidentiality individually read 2026-09-23** |
| `sc_query` | **A** | **A** | A physician query is lost or wrong, and a code is changed without the documentation that supports it — or a supported change is lost | A named patient and a named physician are disclosed together with what is AMBIGUOUS in the clinical record and the revenue turning on it | **RE-TIERED B -> A on BOTH axes, 2026-09-23. READ BY HANK, ROUTED, AND RE-VERIFIED AGAINST THE APP BEFORE LANDING** — `addQueryEntry()` at `sairncode.html:4621` writes `{id, patient, physician, type, value, status}`, matching the routed read exactly. A physician query is the coder asking the physician to clarify documentation, so the row joins a NAMED PATIENT to a NAMED PROVIDER, states what is unclear in that patient’s record, and carries the dollar `value` at stake. **That is PHI with a provider attached, and it is materially more direct than `sc_rac`, whose row is an aggregate figure with no individual on it at all.** On the integrity axis a query is the SUPPORT for a code change — same limb as `sc_hcc` and `sc_coded_items`, both already A. **AND IT HAS BEEN OPEN LONGER THAN ANY ROW HERE, WHICH IS ITSELF THE RECORD:** it was dispatched alongside `rf_prequal_documents`, reported blocked by an active claim, and when that dispatch was re-sent it went out with `rf_prequal_documents` alone and `sc_query` on neither the re-send nor the held list. **It fell between two hand-offs and stayed there across several rounds.** Written into the row rather than only into a commit, because the thing that nearly lost it was a fact living only in somebody’s working memory |
| `sc_rac` | **A** | **B** | A Recovery Audit Contractor determination is lost or wrong, and the appeal window with it | An unauthorised reader learns the practice's own audit exposure -- aggregate dollar figures, commercially sensitive, no individual's record on the row | **RE-TIERED B -> A on the TIER axis, confidentiality individually read and left at B, 2026-09-23.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `{id, ref, amount, status}` with `status` one of Active / Appealing / Reopened (`addRacEntry()`, `sairncode.html:4799`). TIER A ON THE MONEY LIMB: `amount` is a Medicare Recovery Audit Contractor overpayment determination -- money the practice must repay or appeal -- and `renderRac()` (`:4766`) sums it into the "Overpayments Found" and "Disputed Amount" KPIs at `:4746-4752`. A RAC appeal runs on a filing window, which is the limb `sc_denial` is already A on. Confidentiality-B for a specific reason rather than the default: the row carries NO beneficiary, claim or encounter identifier, only an audit reference and a dollar figure, so it is the `sc_revenue` shape and not the `sc_claims` shape. **NAMED TRIGGER, so this is not silently inherited: the day a RAC entry gains a claim, patient or encounter link it becomes Confidentiality-A like `sc_denial`, and this row must be re-read then** |
| `sc_revenue` | **A** | **B** | Revenue reported wrong | An unauthorised reader learns aggregate performance figures. Commercially sensitive, not an elevated class -- the row carries no individual's record | Money. **Soft-delete-only since 2026-09-15 (item 97):** `api/_resources/sairncode.js` grants `soft_delete` and not `delete`, so the row is marked `_deleted_at` and excluded from reads rather than removed, and recovery needs no backup &mdash; which matters because `docs/2026-09-14-nightly-backup-design.md` records the nightly backup as never having run. The list is pinned to THIS TABLE by `tests/sairncode_gates.js`, both directions.**Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (AGGREGATE)** |
| `sc_scrubrules` | **B** | **B** | Operational data lost or wrong | No elevated confidentiality class -- no PII, PHI, privileged communication, or financial-account detail on this row. Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read | Operational data lost or wrong: neither money nor a regulated record. Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read |
| `sc_settings` | **A** | **B** | The practice’s RECORDS-RETENTION PERIOD is wrong, and a purge built on it destroys coding records early or keeps them past their term | The practice’s own configuration. Commercially sensitive at most; no individual on the row | **2026-09-23 `*_settings` SWEEP, prompted by `rf_settings`.** **C -> A. IT IS NOT A PREFERENCE AND THE SERVER ALREADY TREATED IT AS SOMETHING ELSE.** `api/sd-data.js:13198` gates every write behind a session AND `role !== 'admin'` — *“Only a Compliance Admin can change practice-level settings”* — and `:13220` adds a **RETENTION FLOOR GUARD** refusing any `retention_years` below `SC_RETENTION_FLOOR_YEARS` with a dedicated `RETENTION_BELOW_FLOOR` error. **The guard’s own comment is the argument for the tier:** it is enforced server-side rather than in the UI because the value *“is intended to drive a purge that does not exist yet — by the time anything acts on it, nobody will remember who typed it, so a below-floor value must never reach storage in the first place.”* A records-retention term on a medical-coding practice is a regulated obligation, and this row is where it lives. **Nothing gets a role gate and a statutory floor for being a preference** |

Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read | Operational data lost or wrong: neither money nor a regulated record. Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read |
| `sc_telehealth` | **A** | **B** | A telehealth claim line carries the wrong dollar value, and the Compliance Value figure built from it is wrong | No elevated confidentiality class ON THIS ROW, and the arguable half is named rather than buried: the row holds a claim reference, a place of service and a modifier, and NO beneficiary name, date of birth or identifier. A claim reference is linkable to a beneficiary in the system that issued it, so a reader who thinks that linkability alone is enough to elevate this axis has a real argument -- it is refused here only because nothing on THIS row identifies a person | **RE-TIERED B → A 2026-09-23 (cc) on the MONEY limb, read against the code rather than matched to a sibling.** `addTelehealthEntry()` at `sairncode.html:4854` stores `value` as a `parseFloat` of a dollar amount per claim line; `renderTelehealth()` prints it as `$`+value per row and sums it -- `complianceValue = coded.reduce((s,t) => s + t.value, 0)` at `:4804` -- into a **"Compliance Value"** KPI rendered as dollars at `:4812`. That is money on the row, not upstream of it, and the A criteria name money first. **THE FRAMING I WAS GIVEN SAID "same MONEY_INTERNAL shape as `sc_fraud`" AND `sc_fraud` IS STILL B/B AND STILL DEFAULT-CLASSIFIED** -- so this is argued from its own code and carries no claim about that row. **One thing that is RIGHT and worth recording:** the value is coerced on the way in with `isNaN(value) ? 0 : value`, so the truthy-sum concatenation hazard fixed across StoneDesk and SAIRNbuild on 2026-09-22 cannot reach this sum |

### `sairndental` — 24 resources

| Resource | Tier | Confidentiality | Worst consequence if it is wrong or lost | Worst consequence if it is read by the wrong person | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `dnt_appointments` | **A** | **A** | An appointment record is lost or wrong, and a patient is not seen or is seen for the wrong thing | That a named person is a patient, and when and why they attend, is disclosed | **RE-TIERED B -> A on BOTH axes, 2026-09-22.** CLEAR-CUT PHI. The fact that an identified individual is receiving dental care, and the schedule on which they receive it, is protected health information before any clinical detail is attached -- the same argument already accepted for `sc_compliance` on this platform (&ldquo;payment for the provision of health care to an identified individual is PHI in its own right&rdquo;). Appointment data also carries the presenting reason in practice. **Confidentiality individually read 2026-09-22** |
| `dnt_ar` | **A** | **A** | Receivables ageing wrong | A named person's health-care payment record is disclosed. Payment for the provision of health care to an identified individual is protected health information in its own right, before any clinical detail is attached to it | Money. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (PHI_PAYMENT)** |
| `dnt_charges` | **A** | **A** | A patient charged wrongly | A named person's health-care payment record is disclosed. Payment for the provision of health care to an identified individual is protected health information in its own right, before any clinical detail is attached to it | Money. `api/_lib/dental-ledger.js` -- a negative charge shows credit on the billing panel while the ageing report does not move. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (PHI_PAYMENT)** |
| `dnt_complaints` | **A** | **A** | A patient complaint record is lost or wrong, and a regulatory or remedial response is built on it | A named patient&rsquo;s grievance against their provider is disclosed | **RE-TIERED B -> A on BOTH axes, 2026-09-22.** A complaint is PHI joined to an allegation about care, and it is the record a dental board or a malpractice enquiry asks for. Losing or altering one is not an operational inconvenience -- it is the destruction of the evidence in a dispute where the practice is an interested party. **Confidentiality individually read 2026-09-22** |
| `dnt_coverage_rules` | **A** | **B** | An insurance estimate locked onto a charge from a wrong rule | Nothing elevated is disclosed: the row is a rule or reference value, not data about a person | Money. `computeEstimatedInsurance()` LOCKS its result onto the charge and never recomputes. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (RULE_TABLE)** |
| `dnt_cred_rules` | **A** | **B** | A licensure requirement row is wrong, and EVERY dental provider&rsquo;s compliance verdict computed against it in that state is wrong &mdash; in either direction: a lapsed clinician reads as current, or a compliant one is flagged. It is the rule half of the pair whose record half (`dnt_credentials`) is already Tier A for exactly that alarm | Nothing elevated is disclosed: the row is a rule or reference value, not data about a person | **REGULATED: per-licence reference table under the load-state gate. RE-TIERED B -> A, 2026-09-25 (Hank), BY RECONCILING THE FAMILY RATHER THAN DECIDING THIS ROW ALONE.** **THE REGISTER HAD ALREADY DECIDED THIS TWICE UNDER A STAMPED NAME** &mdash; `rf_cert_rules` and `alf_compliance_rules`, both **A/B**, both carrying the sentence above. This is the third instance and it was sitting at B on the boilerplate default (*&ldquo;classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read&rdquo;*), which is how one member of a decided class keeps its old tier: the class is decided, the ROW was never opened. **THE THREE ARE STRUCTURALLY IDENTICAL, not merely similar, and that is checkable rather than asserted:** all three live in `api/sd-data.js`, all three have an `evaluate` branch that fetches the rules beside the records and maps them through the SAME seven-field projection (`rule_id, state, requirement_type, role/facility_class, effective_from, effective_to, status, data`), and all three hand the pair to their app&rsquo;s shared credentials engine &mdash; `evaluateBoard` at `api/sd-data.js:13219` for dental and `evaluateBoard` at `:6598` for roofing, with the SAIRNcare equivalent beside them. The dental branch is a line-for-line sibling of the roofing one. **AND THE PAIR ARGUMENT IS THE DECIDING ONE:** `dnt_credentials` is already A because *&ldquo;a lapsed clinical credential shown as current&rdquo;* is the failure. That verdict is not stored on the record &mdash; it is COMPUTED against these rules on every board render. A register that prices the record at A and the rule it is measured against at B is pricing the alarm and not the thing that decides it. Same sentence as `sd_pricing_rules` and `law_clerequirements`: **one wrong rule is not one wrong record.** **Confidentiality individually read and left at B (RULE_TABLE), matching both siblings:** no person appears on the row | 
| `dnt_credentials` | **A** | **B** | A lapsed clinical credential shown as current | Little or nothing. A professional licence or certification is a public fact by design, and this row is what a customer or an inspector is meant to be shown | REGULATED: licensure on a clinical role. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (LICENSURE)** |
| `dnt_denial` | **A** | **A** | An appeal deadline passes unnoticed | A named person's health-care payment record is disclosed. Payment for the provision of health care to an identified individual is protected health information in its own right, before any clinical detail is attached to it | Money. An unparseable `denied_on` left the row looking like a denial with a known window while dropping out of the closing-soon filter. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (PHI_PAYMENT)** |
| `dnt_gfe` | **A** | **A** | A federally required estimate issued incomplete | A named person's health-care payment record is disclosed. Payment for the provision of health care to an identified individual is protected health information in its own right, before any clinical detail is attached to it | REGULATED: 45 CFR 149.610(c)(1), enforced server-side in `api/_lib/dental-gfe.js`. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (PHI_PAYMENT)** |

Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read | Operational data lost or wrong: neither money nor a regulated record. Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read |

The guardian rule is enforced server-side in `api/_lib/dental-guardian.js`. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (PHI_CLINICAL)** |
| `dnt_payments` | **A** | **A** | A payment recorded wrongly | A named person's health-care payment record is disclosed. Payment for the provision of health care to an identified individual is protected health information in its own right, before any clinical detail is attached to it | Money. A negative payment made `dnAging()` report MORE outstanding than the charge it paid. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (PHI_PAYMENT)** |
| `dnt_procedure_types` | **A** | **B** | A procedure code, fee or effective date is wrong, and every claim and patient charge built from it is wrong | No elevated confidentiality class -- a procedure catalogue names no patient | **RE-TIERED B -> A on the TIER axis, confidentiality unchanged at B, 2026-09-22.** TIER A ON THE INTEGRITY AXIS ONLY, and the split is the point of the two-axis format. The row carries a CODE, a FEE, effective-from/effective-to dates and a supersede flag (sairndental.html:3258), so it is a fee schedule with effective dating: every claim and every patient charge is computed from it, and a wrong code or fee produces wrong claims at scale rather than one wrong record. Confidentiality stays B because a catalogue of procedures names nobody -- re-tiering that axis too would be the label-without-evidence this table forbids. **Confidentiality individually read 2026-09-22** |
| `dnt_provider_hours` | **B** | **B** | Operational data lost or wrong | No elevated confidentiality class -- no PII, PHI, privileged communication, or financial-account detail on this row. Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read | Operational data lost or wrong: neither money nor a regulated record. Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read |
| `dnt_providers` | **B** | **B** | A provider row or its `linked_employee_id` is wrong, and a clinician&rsquo;s patient visibility is mis-scoped -- the failure modes are LOUD by construction (403 PROVIDER_NOT_LINKED, 409 on a duplicate link), never a silently wrong patient set | No elevated confidentiality class -- a provider&rsquo;s name and a login link, the practice&rsquo;s own roster. No patient, no PHI, no financial detail on the row | **INDIVIDUALLY READ 2026-09-24 (Hank); B CONFIRMED, and the old &ldquo;classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read&rdquo; boilerplate REMOVED because it had gone false the quiet way -- the code has treated this row as a real ACCESS-CONTROL INPUT since 2026-09-11 and the register still said nobody had looked.** What the read found: **the roster IS SAIRNdental&rsquo;s patient-visibility scoping table** -- `dntLinkedProvider()` (`api/sd-data.js:11749`) resolves the caller&rsquo;s session to a provider row by `linked_employee_id`, and that result becomes the patient set every non-broad-read role may see. The protections are real and layered: management-only write (403 ROLE_NOT_PERMITTED since 2026-08-27), a duplicate `linked_employee_id` refused 409 EMPLOYEE_ALREADY_LINKED (so row ORDER can never decide scoping), `dntProviderProblem()` refusing a nameless provider and a non-string link id (which could never match the token&rsquo;s string and would read a linked clinician as unlinked), and the empty-session-id collision closed explicitly (an unlinked row carries `&#39;&#39;` and an empty session id matches NOTHING). **WHY B STANDS DESPITE BEING AN ACCESS-CONTROL INPUT, argued rather than defaulted:** the integrity failure modes are fail-CLOSED and NAMED -- a wrong link produces 403 PROVIDER_NOT_LINKED (a clinician who complains), not a wrong patient list served silently; the one silent direction (a duplicate link letting sort order pick) is refused at the write. A row whose corruption announces itself to the person affected is not the `sd_pricing_rules` shape, where wrongness folds into a believable number. The A trigger for this row is a change that lets a wrong link WIDEN visibility silently -- none exists today | 
| `dnt_recall_outreach` | **A** | **A** | A recall contact recorded against the wrong patient, or a recorded refusal lost | A named patient's recall history is disclosed -- that the practice contacted them about outstanding treatment and what they said back | **RE-TIERED B &rarr; A 2026-09-22, BOTH AXES, AND THE HANDLER ALREADY SAID WHY IN ITS OWN WORDS.** `api/sd-data.js:11292` puts this resource in the patient-scoped gate beside `dnt_patients` and `dnt_gfe` with the reason stated: *"it records that a named patient was contacted and what they said, which is about that patient and nobody else"*. A row that is about one identified patient and their outstanding treatment is protected health information; the code scoped it as such while the register filed it as operational data. **PROMOTED ON THE PROTECTED-DATA LIMB AND NOT ON MONEY, deliberately** -- the same handler comment is explicit that a recall row *"carries no charge and no estimate"*, and it is NOT behind the money gate because the front desk and the hygiene side both work the recall list |
| `dnt_referrals` | **A** | **A** | A referral is lost or wrong, and a patient is not sent for, or is sent for, the wrong care | A named patient&rsquo;s referral -- to whom, and for what suspected condition -- is disclosed | **RE-TIERED B -> A on BOTH axes, 2026-09-22.** CLEAR-CUT PHI, and the referral REASON is often the most diagnostically revealing field in the record: a referral to an oral surgeon for a biopsy states a suspicion the chart may not yet state. On the integrity axis a lost referral is a patient who does not get seen. **Confidentiality individually read 2026-09-22** |
| `dnt_revenue` | **A** | **B** | Revenue reported wrong | An unauthorised reader learns aggregate performance figures. Commercially sensitive, not an elevated class -- the row carries no individual's record | Money. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (AGGREGATE)** |
| `dnt_rollup` | **A** | **B** | An office comparison is wrong, or looks complete when it is not | An unauthorised reader learns aggregate performance figures. Commercially sensitive, not an elevated class -- the row carries no individual's record | Money, and a DERIVED resource -- it owns no table. Tier A because a practice-group owner makes staffing and investment decisions from per-office production; a figure that is quietly short by an unreadable table or by pre-stamp rows is the wrong number presented with the authority of a report. `api/_lib/dnt-rollup.js` therefore returns NULL and names the resource rather than 0, and `api/_lib/dnt-rollup-endpoint.test.js` pins both halves. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (AGGREGATE). Confidentiality-B as an AGGREGATE across offices, not because practice production is uninteresting. If the roll-up is ever made drillable to the patient level it becomes PHI and moves** |
| `dnt_settings` | **A** | **B** | The `booking_slug` is wrong or collides, and a member of the public booking an appointment — or filing a COMPLAINT — is routed into another practice’s tenant | The practice’s own public-facing details: name, phone, address, and which procedures may be booked publicly. Public by design | **2026-09-23 `*_settings` SWEEP, prompted by `rf_settings`.** **C -> A. THIS ROW IS THE TENANT KEY FOR AN UNAUTHENTICATED PUBLIC SURFACE.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `sairndental.html:3388` holds `{id, booking_slug, timezone, publicly_bookable_procedure_type_ids, practice_name, practice_phone, practice_address}`. **`resolveSlug()` in `api/_lib/dental-public.js:62` looks a practice up BY `booking_slug` and returns its `license_hash`** — so the slug is what decides whose tenant a public submission lands in, for both `sairndental-book` and `sairndental-complaint`. `api/sd-data.js:12266` already refuses a duplicate with `409 SLUG_TAKEN`, which is a uniqueness guard on a routing key, not on a preference. `publicly_bookable_procedure_type_ids` is separately an ALLOWLIST governing what an anonymous caller may book. Both write into `dnt_appointments`, already Tier A. Confidentiality-B: everything on the row is meant to be public |

Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read | Operational data lost or wrong: neither money nor a regulated record. Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read |

### `sairndesign` — 18 resources

| Resource | Tier | Confidentiality | Worst consequence if it is wrong or lost | Worst consequence if it is read by the wrong person | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `sdn_clients` | **A** | **A** | Operational data lost or wrong -- a client record or its assignment. The money lives elsewhere: budgets on the already-A `sdn_projects`, prices on the already-A `sdn_specitems`, so integrity individually read and left at B | A NAMED residential client with phone, personal email, HOME ADDRESS, free-text notes, and which employee they are assigned to -- an interior-design client&rsquo;s identity joined to where they live | **CONFIDENTIALITY RE-TIERED B -> A, individually read 2026-09-24 (Hank), and the deciding evidence is the APP&rsquo;S OWN CODE, not the field list.** `saveClient()` (`sairndesign.html:2494`) writes `{name, company, phone, email, address, status, notes, assigned_employee_id}` -- a person joined to a home address and private contact details. **THE PRECEDENT SPLIT THIS ROW SAT INSIDE:** `rf_buildings` stayed B carrying name+address, `sd_crm` went A carrying the same shape -- so name+address alone does not decide. **WHAT DECIDES IS THE TEST `sd_crm`&rsquo;S OWN PROMOTION ESTABLISHED: a SERVER-SIDE PER-EMPLOYEE RESTRICTION is the platform saying out loud that a row is not ordinary operational data.** `sdn_clients` has exactly that, three separate ways, all predating this read: **(1)** a bespoke privacy gate in `api/sd-data.js` (2026-08-20, &ldquo;Task 2 of the platform sales-lead-privacy rule&rdquo; -- `sd_crm` was Task 1) under which a non-management designer is served ONLY clients assigned to them, an unassigned client being management-only; **(2)** a management-only assignment gate on the write branch, citing `sd_crm` as its precedent in its own comment; **(3)** it was the ONE resource of thirty whose client call passed `withSession` before the 2026-09-23 session change -- the app treating this data as needing identity when nothing else did. A row the platform already restricts per-employee on the server, by three independent mechanisms, is a row whose confidentiality tier should say what the code already says. `rf_buildings` has none of those and stays B undisturbed | 
| `sdn_colorcodes` | **B** | **B** | Operational data lost or wrong | No elevated confidentiality class -- no PII, PHI, privileged communication, or financial-account detail on this row. Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read | Operational data lost or wrong: neither money nor a regulated record. Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read |
| `sdn_contracts` | **A** | **A** | A signed design contract wrong on its scope or its fee, or attached to the wrong client | An identified client's negotiated fee structure and signed scope are disclosed to somebody else -- the same shape as a per-customer price, which this register already rates Confidentiality-A | **RE-TIERED B &rarr; A 2026-09-22, BOTH AXES.** `sairndesign.html:3376` stores `{project_id, client_id, title, scope_text, fee_structure, status, esign_name, esign_at}` -- an executed agreement with a FEE STRUCTURE and a signature on it. Money on the first A limb: a contract is a priced commitment rather than a record of one, the same rule this register already applies to `bld_bids`. **The confidentiality half is `sd_negotiated_prices`' argument, not a new one** -- *"per-customer pricing is the shape where a leak is also a commercial disclosure"* -- and a signed scope names the client it binds |
| `sdn_discounts` | **A** | **B** | A discount applied wrongly | A competitor or an unauthorised employee learns the company's own commercial figures. Commercially sensitive, not an elevated class: no PII, PHI, privileged communication or financial-account detail belonging to a third party sits on this row | Money. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (MONEY_INTERNAL)** |
| `sdn_invoices` | **A** | **B** | An invoice wrong on a design engagement | A competitor or an unauthorised employee learns the company's own commercial figures. Commercially sensitive, not an elevated class: no PII, PHI, privileged communication or financial-account detail belonging to a third party sits on this row | Money. Eight client write sites. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (MONEY_INTERNAL)** |
| `sdn_moodboards` | **B** | **B** | Operational data lost or wrong | No elevated confidentiality class -- no PII, PHI, privileged communication, or financial-account detail on this row. Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read | Operational data lost or wrong: neither money nor a regulated record. Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read |
| `sdn_pos` | **A** | **B** | A purchase order is wrong on its total, its vendor or its project -- a priced commitment issued to a vendor, and the committed-spend figure the studio reads off it | No elevated confidentiality class -- vendor, project, and the studio's own WHOLESALE costs. Commercially sensitive (it is the margin the client is not shown) and no third party's protected data sits on the row, which is the same call already settled on `bld_pos` and `grd_boq_rates` | **RE-TIERED B -> A on the MONEY limb 2026-09-23 (Hank); confidentiality individually read and left at B.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `generatePOs()` at `sairndesign.html:2921` writes `{id, po_number, project_id, vendor, item_ids, total_cost, status, created_at}`. **`total_cost` IS COMPUTED AND THEN STORED, which is the whole argument:** `total_cost`, `:2936`, folds `items.reduce((s,x) =&gt; s + (Number(x.cost)&#124;&#124;0), 0)` across the spec items on the order and persists the result. It is NOT re-derived on render -- `rPOs()` at `:2974` sums the STORED `po.total_cost` across open orders into the **Total Committed** KPI -- the `po-total` tile at `:582` (*&ldquo;Wholesale, all open POs&rdquo;*, `$0` placeholder, `fmt()` currency), and the detail modal prints `fmt(po.total_cost)` at `:1156` under the label *&ldquo;Total (Wholesale)&rdquo;*. So a wrong figure is not one wrong row a reader can recompute; it is a committed-spend total that stays wrong. **AND IT IS A PRICED COMMITMENT RATHER THAN A RECORD OF ONE**, which is the exact sentence this register already uses for `bld_pos` and `sb_po`: `setPOStatus()` at `:2966` moves the row Draft &rarr; Sent &rarr; Confirmed &rarr; Received, so by `Sent` it has been issued to a named vendor. **THE ROW SITS DIRECTLY DOWNSTREAM OF A CLIENT&rsquo;S FINANCIAL COMMITMENT:** `approvedSpecItemIdsForProject()` at `:2915` makes only items on an **Approved** proposal eligible, and the code says why in its own comment &mdash; *&ldquo;nothing gets ordered from a vendor before the client has actually committed to paying for it&rdquo;*. **ONE THING WORTH SAYING THAT IS NOT AN ARGUMENT FOR A HIGHER TIER:** `total_cost` is a SNAPSHOT of the spec-item costs at generation time and nothing reconciles it afterwards. That is correct for a commitment &mdash; a PO is a price at a moment &mdash; and it is also why a wrong total is not recoverable by re-deriving it from the items. **Confidentiality individually read 2026-09-23** (MONEY_INTERNAL): the row carries no client PII, no PHI and no financial-account detail; the sensitive part is the studio&rsquo;s own wholesale pricing, which this register classes B by its own stated rule |
| `sdn_projects` | **A** | **B** | A project budget is wrong, and the over/under-budget determination the studio and its AI adviser both read off it is wrong with it | No elevated confidentiality class -- the row carries `client_id`, not a client name, and a project budget is a commercial figure rather than a protected class. Read with the knowledge that `sdn_clients` holds the name this joins to | **INDIVIDUALLY READ OUT OF THE APP 2026-09-23 (Hank).** **B -> A on the MONEY limb; confidentiality individually read and left at B.** `sairndesign.html:1591` seeds `{id, client_id, name, budget, target_install_date, status, notes, created_at}` with real figures -- 45000, 120000, 18000. **`budget` IS NOT A LABEL, IT IS A THRESHOLD.** `:2057` computes `overBudget = pj.budget > 0 && total > pj.budget` and `:2066` turns it into a finding that names the overrun to the dollar; `:2143` prints Over/Under on the spec panel. **AND IT IS SENT TO THE ADVISER:** `:2071` folds it into the AI prompt as *"Budget: $X"*, so a wrong figure does not produce one wrong row, it produces advice reasoned from a false constraint. Same shape the register already gives `bld_pos` and `grd_boq_rates` -- a number that DECIDES rather than records |
| `sdn_proposals` | **A** | **B** | A proposal's status or item list is wrong, and a client is recorded as having approved a priced offer they did not approve -- or the one-of-a-kind reservation it triggers is made against the wrong project | No elevated confidentiality class -- title, `client_id`, an item-id list and a decision date. No client name, no payment instrument | **INDIVIDUALLY READ OUT OF THE APP 2026-09-23 (Hank).** **B -> A on the MONEY limb; confidentiality individually read and left at B.** `:1603` writes `{id, project_id, client_id, title, item_ids, status, sent_date, decided_date, created_at}`. **THE ROW CARRIES NO TOTAL AND THAT IS WHY IT IS A, NOT WHY IT IS NOT:** `proposalTotal()` at `:2290` sums `sdn_specitems.retail` across `item_ids` on every render, so the ITEM LIST is the price -- add or drop an id and the quoted figure changes with no edit to any money field. **AND `status` DRIVES A REAL STATE CHANGE, not a label:** `:2375` on Approved reserves every `one_of_a_kind` spec item for that project and writes `sdn_specitems` back, stamping `decided_date`. So a wrong Approved both records a client decision that did not happen and takes unique inventory out of circulation for a different job. **Same class as `sd_quote_requests`** -- a priced offer with a client accept/decline workflow |
| `sdn_referrals` | **A** | **A** | A referral recorded against the wrong person, or a pipeline value wrong | The PHONE NUMBER of a private individual who is NOT a customer is disclosed, together with who referred them — a social graph of which household recommended which other household | **RE-TIERED B -> A on BOTH axes, 2026-09-23, AND THE PROMOTION IS DRIVEN BY THE CONFIDENTIALITY AXIS** — the case the second axis was added for. READ OUT OF THE APP: `saveReferral()` at `sairndesign.html:3476` writes `{from, to, phone, status, val, date}`, and the seed is explicit: `{from:'Sarah Whitfield', to:'Chris Webb', phone:'(440) 555-0155'}`. **THE REFERRED PARTY HAS NEVER ENGAGED THIS BUSINESS.** `to` and `phone` are a named private individual and their personal number, held by a studio they have no relationship with and did not give it to — and `from` records who handed it over. **THIRD CROSS-APP INSTANCE OF THE SAME SHAPE**, after `bld_referrals` (2026-09-22) and `dnt_referrals`. `bld_referrals` was HELD by an earlier session as an opaque blob no honest Tier A argument could be made for, and was promoted once somebody read the WRITER instead of the name. **That this shape has now been found three times in three apps is the finding, not the row** — a referral table is a place non-customer PII accumulates by design, and it should be looked for rather than waited for |

The margin between them is commercially sensitive and is the studio's own position, which this register classes B by its stated rule | **INDIVIDUALLY READ OUT OF THE APP 2026-09-23 (Hank).** **B -> A on the MONEY limb; confidentiality individually read and left at B.** `:1595` writes `{id, project_id, item, vendor, sku, cost, retail, status, lead_time_weeks, one_of_a_kind, reserved, reserved_for, notes, created_at}` -- **TWO dollar figures per row**, cost and retail, 2400/4200, 3100/5400, 5200/8900 in the seed alone. **THIS IS THE CATALOGUE BOTH ALREADY-A RESOURCES DERIVE THEIR TOTALS FROM, which is the whole argument:** `proposalTotal()` at `:2290` sums `retail` across a proposal, and `generatePOs()` at `sairndesign.html:2921` sums `cost` into `sdn_pos.total_cost`. One wrong row is therefore not one wrong record -- it is a wrong figure quoted to a client AND a wrong figure committed to a vendor, from a single edit. Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read |
| `sdn_timeentries` | **A** | **B** | A billable hour or rate is wrong, and the value of the month's billable work -- the figure the studio invoices from -- is wrong | A named team member's hours and CHARGE-OUT rate. Not an elevated class, and the boundary is stated because it is a real fork: `billable_rate` is what the CLIENT is charged, not what the employee is paid, so it is the studio's commercial position rather than an individual's compensation | **INDIVIDUALLY READ OUT OF THE APP 2026-09-23 (Hank).** **B -> A on the MONEY limb; confidentiality individually read and left at B.** `saveTime()` at `sairndesign.html:3114` writes `{id, employee_id, project_id, date, hours, billable_rate, billable, notes, created_at}` with `billable_rate: Number($('ttrate').value) &#124;&#124; 0`. **IT IS SUMMED INTO MONEY ON EVERY RENDER:** `:3136` folds `hours &times; billable_rate` across the month's BILLABLE entries into the value KPI, and `:3140` prints the rate as `fmt(rate)+'/hr'`. **THE CONFIDENTIALITY FORK IS MADE EXPLICITLY RATHER THAN BY ANALOGY, because a reader will compare this to `bld_timesheet` (A/A) and the two rows look alike.** `bld_timesheet` carries `rate: 42` against a `cost_code` of `01-Labour` -- a job-COSTING rate, which the register read as the employee's pay. This row carries `billable_rate: 125` beside a `billable` flag: it is the CHARGE-OUT rate, the studio's price for that person's hour, and it is not that person's income. Nobody's compensation is on this row. **If a pay rate is ever added beside the billable one, this row moves to A/A** on the same rule that put `bld_timesheet` and `sen_pay_rates` there |
| `sdn_vendors` | **B** | **B** | Operational data lost or wrong | No elevated confidentiality class -- no PII, PHI, privileged communication, or financial-account detail on this row. Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read | Operational data lost or wrong: neither money nor a regulated record. Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read |

| Resource | Tier | Confidentiality | Worst consequence if it is wrong or lost | Worst consequence if it is read by the wrong person | Evidence |

Commercially sensitive, not an elevated class: no PII, PHI, privileged communication or financial-account detail belonging to a third party sits on this row | Money. Matched as a C candidate by a keyword rule because "accounts" contains "counts" -- corrected by hand, which is why A is not rule-classified. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (MONEY_INTERNAL)** |
| `sf_bottle_fills` | **B** | **B** | A pour-level reading is wrong and a stock figure drifts | Bar stock levels. Operational | **2026-09-23 FULL `sf_*` RE-AUDIT, read individually.** **CONFIRMED B, individually read rather than defaulted.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `:4134` writes `{productId, date, when, percent, method}` with `method` recorded as *“Automatic, from photograph”* — a measurement of how full a bottle is. It feeds a stock figure and prices nothing |
| `sf_ceremonial_items` | **B** | **B** | A ceremonial item record is wrong | What regalia the post holds. Operational | **2026-09-23 FULL `sf_*` RE-AUDIT, read individually.** **CONFIRMED B, individually read rather than defaulted.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `:6010` writes `{item, kind, serial, inspected}`. An `inspected` date with nothing computing an interval from it. **NAMED TRIGGER: if an inspection interval is ever derived from that field, re-read this row** — that is precisely what moved `rf_safety_equipment` |
| `sf_disbursements` | **A** | **B** | A charitable distribution from gaming proceeds is wrong on its recipient, amount or purpose | A recipient organisation and an amount. Reportable detail, not an elevated class | **2026-09-23 FULL `sf_*` RE-AUDIT, read individually.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `:4890` writes `{recipient, amount, date, source, game, direction, purpose, natcat}`. Money leaving the post, and the `purpose`/`natcat` pair is what makes a distribution a QUALIFYING one under ORC 2915.10(A)(2) rather than merely a payment. Same limb as `sf_gaming_expenses`, on the other side of the account |
| `sf_district_imports` | **A** | **B** | A signed district report row is wrong or tampered, and a district&rsquo;s ORC 2915 net-profit figures, its per-capita pass-through liability, its distribution shortfall, or its compliance-issue count is misstated in the roll-up every district decision reads | Aggregate figures about a POST, not a person -- gaming net profits, per-capita money, shortfall, compliance flags. Commercially and legally sensitive aggregates with no individual on the row, the same confidentiality class this register gives `sc_rac` | **RE-TIERED B -> A on the INTEGRITY axis, 2026-09-24 (Hank), the row ACTUALLY READ THIS TIME.** **THE 2026-09-23 COULD-NOT-TELL IS EXPLAINED AND CLOSED: the writer exists and was invisible to the audit&rsquo;s grep because it writes through a CONSTANT ALIAS** -- `K_IMPORTS=&#39;sf_district_imports&#39;` at `sairnfreedom.html:6377`, written by `st(K_IMPORTS, imports)` in `sfImportReports()` (`:6671`) and cleared at `:6671`. A search for the literal name finds the sync list and never the writer; the honest could-not-tell was the right verdict for that method, and the correction is a lesson about the METHOD (grep the aliases too), not about the auditor. **WHAT THE ROW ACTUALLY CARRIES, which is not roster data:** a signature-verified per-post ANNUAL REPORT -- `report.gaming.netProfitPurposeRegime` and `netProfitTieredRegime` (the two ORC 2915 regimes, deliberately never summed anywhere), `report.money.perCapitaAccrued`, `report.distribution.shortfall`, and a `report.compliance` block -- keyed deterministically on entity+year so a re-import UPDATES rather than duplicates. **IT IS UPSTREAM OF EVERY DISTRICT FIGURE:** `sfRenderDistrict()` folds these rows into the district table and the KPI row -- *Per-capita owed upward* (&ldquo;Pass-through liability&rdquo;), the two regime columns, shortfall, and *Entities with issues* rendered red. A wrong row is not one wrong record; it is a district officer acting on a misstated statutory posture and a misstated amount owed upward. **Same limb as `sc_rac` (A/B)** -- aggregate money-and-compliance exposure, nobody named. **THE MITIGATIONS ARE REAL AND BOUND THE TIER ARGUMENT WITHOUT DEFEATING IT:** import is verify-first and REJECTS rather than caveats; trust-on-first-use records each entity&rsquo;s key fingerprint and a CHANGED key is accepted but flagged loudly (&ldquo;only you can tell which&rdquo;); the roll-up states its as-of honesty and that there is deliberately NO live cross-tenant read. Those defend the SIGNATURE boundary -- they do nothing about a correctly-signed-but-wrong report, a cleared store (`sfClearImports()` is one click), or a tampered LOCAL row after import, which is what the integrity limb prices. Confidentiality individually read and left at B: aggregates about an entity, no person on the row | 
| `sf_documents` | **B** | **B** | A governance document’s text or version is lost | The post’s own governing documents, which members are asked to read and sign | **2026-09-23 FULL `sf_*` RE-AUDIT, read individually.** **CONFIRMED B, individually read rather than defaulted.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `:5637` writes `{title, version, body, hash: docTextHash(body), added}`. **IT LOOKED LIKE `law_portalesign` AND IT IS NOT, which is why this cell is long:** `sf_signatures` (already A/A) snapshots `docId, docTitle, version` AND `hash` onto the signature at `:5665`, so a signature carries its own hash of the text it was made against. **Changing or losing this row cannot retroactively alter what somebody signed** — the design already removed the limb that would have made it A |
| `sf_donations` | **A** | **A** | A donation is recorded against the wrong donor or the wrong amount | A NAMED DONOR is disclosed together with what they gave. Donor identity and gift amount are not a public fact — the equivalent schedule is redacted from public disclosure for exactly this reason | **2026-09-23 FULL `sf_*` RE-AUDIT, read individually.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `:5153` writes `{donor, donorType, amount, date, use}`. Money on its face. Confidentiality-A on the identified-counterparty reasoning already settled for `sd_negotiated_prices`: the harm is not that a figure leaks, it is that an identified individual’s giving does |
| `sf_donor_awards` | **A** | **A** | A donor recognition record is lost or wrong, and an award or a standing is given or withheld wrongly | A named donor&rsquo;s giving history and recognition level are disclosed | **RE-TIERED B -> A on BOTH axes, 2026-09-22.** A recognition record states, by construction, how much a NAMED individual has given -- the award tier IS the amount, encoded. Donor identity joined to giving level is the field charitable organisations protect most carefully. Same limb as `sf_donations`, which the approved gate batch covered and this row was left out of. **Confidentiality individually read 2026-09-22** |
| `sf_donor_tiers` | **A** | **B** | A giving threshold is wrong, and every donor&rsquo;s recognition level is computed wrongly from it | No elevated confidentiality class -- a tier definition names no donor | **RE-TIERED B -> A on the TIER axis, confidentiality unchanged at B, 2026-09-22.** TIER A ON THE INTEGRITY AXIS ONLY. The thresholds are the rule every `sf_donor_awards` row is derived from, so one wrong boundary mis-states the standing of every donor at once -- a one-row error with an organisation-wide blast radius, which is the shape that earns A on integrity. Confidentiality stays B because the definition names nobody; `dnt_procedure_types` is split the same way for the same reason. **Confidentiality individually read 2026-09-22** |
| `sf_events` | **B** | **B** | A hall booking is wrong on its date or its details | What is booked at the post and when. An internal calendar | **2026-09-23 FULL `sf_*` RE-AUDIT, read individually.** **CONFIRMED B, individually read rather than defaulted.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `:4238` writes `{title, date, start, end, kind, alcohol, sessionId}`. **`alcohol` AND `sessionId` BOTH LOOK LIKE COMPLIANCE HOOKS AND NEITHER IS WIRED TO ONE TODAY** — no permit or licence determination reads this row. **NAMED TRIGGER: the day anything computes a permit or licensing conclusion from `alcohol`, re-read this row**, because at that moment it stops being a calendar |
| `sf_gaming_expenses` | **A** | **A** | A payment from the segregated gaming account is wrong, and the account Ohio requires be used exclusively is misstated | A NAMED PAYEE is disclosed against a payment from a post's segregated charitable-gaming account — and where that payee is a person on the operator roster, the disclosure is of a fact ORC 2915.09(D)(1) prohibits outright | **CONFIDENTIALITY RE-TIERED B -> A, 2026-09-23. INTEGRITY UNCHANGED at A** — the integrity half and its two statutory refusals were read in the full `sf_*` re-audit and stand verbatim below. READ OUT OF THE APP: `sfAddExpense()` at `sairnfreedom.html:3411` writes `{id, accountId, date, payee, amount, category, method, description}`, and **THE APP REFUSES ON THREE STATUTORY GROUNDS BEFORE IT WILL WRITE ONE** — a missing payee is refused citing ORC 2915.10(C) (*“permits payment only by check or electronic fund transfer”*), a payment exceeding the account balance is refused citing ORC 2915.10(C) (*“permits payment only from the segregated account”*), and an expense in a category it cannot validate against ORC 2915.01(GG) is refused unless the user supplies their own description. A resource guarded by three statutory refusals is not operational data. **WHAT MOVES THE CONFIDENTIALITY AXIS, and it is not that the row contains a name:** `payee` is a REQUIRED, STATUTORILY MANDATED IDENTITY FIELD — the code's own comment at `:3415` says *“2915.10(A)(2) requires the payee name on the record regardless”* and the app will not write without it. **That is the distinction this register drew against `sc_fraud` the same day, in the opposite direction:** a required identity field is not a maybe, an optional free-text description is. **AND THE JOIN IS THE SHARP EDGE.** ORC 2915.09(D)(1) forbids *“commission, wage, salary, reward, tip, donation, gratuity or other form of compensation”* to a bingo game operator, and `sf_operators` is already **A/A** because it holds named individuals with dates of birth and felony and gambling conviction flags. A gaming-account payment to a name on that roster is not a commercial figure, it is evidence of a prohibited act about a person the neighbouring resource already protects at A. **THE APP'S OWN POSTURE AGREES:** it holds ORC 2915.10(A)(3) winner Social Security numbers out of the product entirely, and its district report is aggregates-only *“by construction”* with no member, staff, operator, donor or winner names — so named individuals in the gaming record set are already treated as the thing not to hold. **WHAT THIS DOES NOT ESTABLISH:** the category vocabulary is only `canteen_facility` and `other_allowable`, neither of which names an individual, so a typical payee is a landlord or a supplier. The tier rests on the field being REQUIRED and identifying, not on a measurement of who real payees are. **Confidentiality individually read 2026-09-23** |

No elevated class | **2026-09-23 FULL `sf_*` RE-AUDIT, read individually.** **CONFIRMED B, individually read rather than defaulted.** READ OUT OF THE APP: stored under `K_DETAILS` (`:5496`) alongside the ceremonial family. Scheduling of honour-guard details; no money, no statutory determination, and nothing about a person beyond taking part |

A count, not a valuation — no price and no variance on the row, which is what separates it from `sf_shifts` |

Reference content. The DISBURSEMENT is Tier A; the vocabulary it picks from is not |
| `sf_officers` | **A** | **B** | The officer record is wrong, and a governance decision is recorded as granted by somebody who does not hold the office | Who holds which office at the post. Published by design in an organisation of this kind | **2026-09-23 FULL `sf_*` RE-AUDIT, read individually.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `:2704` writes `{name, capability, active, assigned}`. **IT IS AN AUTHORISATION MAP, which the register already files at A once** (`sc_credential_scope`): `capability` decides who may act, and `sfGrantWaiver()` will not record a waiver unless `grantedBy` is an ACTIVE officer from this list. Confidentiality-B rather than A, and the difference from `sc_credential_scope` is stated: a lodge’s officers are a published fact, where a credential scope map is reconnaissance |

A missing `dob` is handled as CANNOT-EVALUATE rather than as eligible, which is the right shape and is why the row is dangerous when WRONG rather than when empty |

| `sf_service_appointments` | **A** | **A** | An appointment record is lost or wrong, and a veteran referred to a service is not followed up -- or the post's record shows an appointment against an organisation it has NOT recorded as VA-accredited | That a named member sought help from a named organisation, on a date, and whether they attended or DID NOT ATTEND | **RE-TIERED B -> A on BOTH axes, 2026-09-23.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `sfLogAppointment()` at `sairnfreedom.html:5978` builds `{id, memberId, referralId, date, outcome}`. **THE ROW STORES TWO FOREIGN KEYS AND NO NAME, AND THAT IS NOT A DEFENCE -- THE JOIN IS THE DISPLAY.** `sfRenderServices()` resolves both against the roster and the referral directory and renders date / MEMBER NAME / ORGANISATION / outcome into `ap-tbody`, so the disclosure is what the panel already shows. `outcome` comes from `APPOINTMENT_OUTCOMES` and includes **`Did not attend`**: this is a named veteran's help-seeking history, including the times they did not go. Same test as `sen_referrals` and `sen_authorizations`, both already A -- a referral identifies a person as needing help before any service exists, and who they were referred to is the most revealing field. **ON THE INTEGRITY AXIS THE APP ITSELF NAMES THE STAKE, one function below:** the referral directory carries `ACCREDITATION_STATUSES`, and `sfRenderServices()` raises a flag counting entries *not recorded as VA-accredited*, citing 38 U.S.C. 5902(a)(1) and saying in its own words that *"typically" is not a record*. An appointment logged against the wrong directory entry attaches a member to an organisation the post has not verified. The same file refuses claim-strategy answers outright on 38 U.S.C. 5901 grounds, so this app already treats who-is-recognised as a statutory question rather than a label. **Confidentiality individually read 2026-09-23** |
