No individual named on the row | **2026-09-23 FULL `sf_*` RE-AUDIT, read individually.** **CONFIRMED B, individually read rather than defaulted.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `:5376` writes `{activity, date, members, nonmembers, minutesEach, hoursEach, ...}` — COUNTS of participants rather than a roster, so the row reports how many took part and not who |
| `sf_service_referrals` | **B** | **B** | A partner organisation’s referral details are wrong | A partner organisation, its contact and its verification status. An organisation, not an individual | **2026-09-23 FULL `sf_*` RE-AUDIT, read individually.** **CONFIRMED B, individually read rather than defaulted.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `:5919` writes `{org, status, ref, contact, verified}`. A directory of referral partners. The VA-adjacent OUTCOME for a named member lives on `sf_service_appointments`, which is already A/A — this row is the other side of that join and carries nobody’s record |
| `sf_sessions` | **A** | **B** | A bingo session is wrong on its prize cap, its receipts or its prizes | Session takings and prizes. The post’s own figures, no individual on the row | **2026-09-23 FULL `sf_*` RE-AUDIT, read individually.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `:3239` writes `{date, start, end, before, after, prizeCap, accountId, games, receipts, prizes, recorded}`. **`prizeCap` IS A STATUTORY LIMIT, NOT A HOUSE RULE**, and `receipts`/`prizes` are the figures a session is reconciled and reported on. This is the regulated event record the rest of the gaming family hangs off, and it sat at B while `sf_ledger` above it was already A |
| `sf_shifts` | **A** | **A** | A shift reconciliation is wrong, and a cash shortage is recorded against the wrong person or not at all | A named person is disclosed alongside a CASH VARIANCE attributed to their shift | **2026-09-23 FULL `sf_*` RE-AUDIT, read individually.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `:3996` writes `{date, by, float, cashSales, expected, counted, variance}` and the toast states the variance outright. Money, and the only record of it. **CONFIDENTIALITY-A ON THE ATTRIBUTION RATHER THAN THE AMOUNT, and the boundary is stated because it is a close call:** a till variance is a small number, but it is a number ATTACHED TO A NAMED EMPLOYEE and read as an accusation. `bld_tna` is Confidentiality-A for a named worker’s assessed gaps on the same reasoning, and a recorded shortage is more consequential than an assessment |
| `sf_signatures` | **A** | **A** | An executed signature lost, altered, or attached to the wrong document | A named person's executed signature is disclosed, together with the document it binds them to | **RE-TIERED B &rarr; A 2026-09-22, BOTH AXES, and the register already carried the argument against itself.** `docs/SAIRN-OPEN-WORK-INDEX.md` records an open finding in these words: *"A client's executed e-signature on a matter document is filed as Tier B 'operational data' -- by a rule that never asks whether a record is EVIDENCE"*. That is this row. A signature is not a record OF a commitment, it is the commitment, and it cannot be re-created after the fact from anything else in the app. **GATED 2026-09-24, and this cell's own argument is what closed it.** It was reachable on the licence key alone through 2026-09-22, when `sf_members`/`sf_donor_awards` were added to `SD_SESSION_GATED` and this row was explicitly left as an open gating decision. Closed now: a signature is not a record ABOUT a person but a REUSABLE INSTRUMENT that can be lifted off one document and re-applied to another, so leaving it on a browser-shipped bearer token is a sharper exposure than the identity rows gated beside it, not a softer one. `api/sf-session-gate.test.js` drives the refusal through the real dispatcher; `api/sd-data-sf-session-gate.test.js` holds the gated set equal to the approved set in BOTH directions |
| `sf_staff` | **A** | **A** | A staff record is wrong, and the ORC 2915.09(D)(1) cross-check that catches a paid canteen worker operating the game misses them | A named person and their DATE OF BIRTH. Personnel identity, the `sd_hr_employees` shape | **2026-09-23 FULL `sf_*` RE-AUDIT, read individually.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `:3793` writes `{name, dob, paid}`. **`paid` IS LOAD-BEARING ON A STATUTORY GATE** — `operatorEligibility()` matches this roster by name and raises the (D)(1) finding when a bingo operator also appears here as PAID. So a missing or mistyped name does not lose a staff record, it silently clears an operator the statute does not clear. Name + DOB is personnel identity regardless of the small field count |
| `sf_tickets` | **A** | **B** | A sale is recorded at the wrong price or against the wrong member, and the takings it rolls into are wrong | What a named member bought. Commercially sensitive, not an elevated class | **2026-09-23 FULL `sf_*` RE-AUDIT, read individually.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `:3884` writes `{date, time, memberId, staffId, productId, qty, rail, unitPrice, total}`. `unitPrice` and `total` are money on the row, snapshotted from the product at the moment of sale |
| `sf_vehicle_service` | **A** | **B** | An odometer reading is wrong, and the service-due flag on a post vehicle is wrong with it — this row is the only thing that ever advances the number that flag reads | What was done to a post vehicle and what it cost. Commercially sensitive, not an elevated class: no individual is named on the row | **RE-TIERED B -> A on INTEGRITY, 2026-09-23 — AND THE SAME-DAY REVIEW THAT HELD IT AT B WAS RIGHT ABOUT BOTH THINGS IT CHECKED. It checked the wrong two.** Its grounds were that `cost` *“reaches no invoice”* and that *“the expiry ALARM lives on `sf_vehicles`, not here”*. **BOTH HOLD. `cost` CARRIES NOTHING, and that is measured rather than conceded:** `getVehicleService()` has four call sites and NONE aggregates — `money(s.cost)` is rendered per row and summed nowhere, priced nowhere, and reaches neither `sf_ledger` nor the district report. And the REGISTRATION/INSURANCE expiry alarm really is date-based and really does live on `sf_vehicles`. **WHAT NEITHER GROUND REACHED IS THE `odometer` FIELD.** `sairnfreedom.html:6170` writes the reading BACK onto the vehicle — `veh.odometer=odo` — and there are exactly TWO writers of that number in the whole file: `:6140`, the add-a-vehicle form which sets it ONCE at creation, and this one. **So after a vehicle exists, this row is the sole thing that ever advances it**, and `:6222` computes *“N vehicle(s) are at or past their service odometer”* from it against `dueAt`. **THAT IS THE `law_bankstatements` SHAPE, ONE STEP FURTHER BACK:** the raw input to a row that is already A. `sf_vehicles` is A/B, and a service-due flag computed from a number only this row maintains cannot be more reliable than this row. **AND “REACHES NO INVOICE” IS NOT THE REGISTER'S TEST** — `sd_business_snapshots` was held at B on *“nothing is priced, billed or DECIDED from it”* and `bld_costs` moved to A because bids are COMPUTED from it. Something is decided from this row. **THE WRITE PATH ALREADY TREATS IT AS MORE THAN OPERATIONAL DATA:** it REFUSES a reading below the one on record — *“Refused: N is below the recorded odometer”* — a guard nobody writes for a display field. **Confidentiality individually read and UNCHANGED at B**, same MONEY_INTERNAL ground as `bld_costs`, which this row otherwise resembles |
| `sf_vehicles` | **A** | **B** | A registration or insurance expiry is wrong, and a post vehicle is driven uninsured or unregistered while showing as current | A vehicle, its plate and its paperwork dates. No individual on the row | **2026-09-23 FULL `sf_*` RE-AUDIT, read individually.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `:6104` writes `{desc, plate, odometer, dueAt, registration, insurance}`, and `:6189` computes a flag from those two dates — *“N vehicle(s) have registration or insurance expiring within thirty days, or already expired.”* **SAME SHAPE AS `rf_safety_equipment` AND `sb_train`: the date IS the alarm**, so a wrong one does not mislabel a record, it removes the vehicle from the warning |
| `sf_vendor_prices` | **A** | **B** | Vendor pricing wrong | A competitor or an unauthorised employee learns the company's own commercial figures. Commercially sensitive, not an elevated class: no PII, PHI, privileged communication or financial-account detail belonging to a third party sits on this row | Money, upstream. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (MONEY_INTERNAL)** |
| `sf_vendors` | **B** | **B** | A supplier record is wrong | Supplier names and whether they are licensed. Trade directory | **2026-09-23 FULL `sf_*` RE-AUDIT, read individually.** **CONFIRMED B, individually read rather than defaulted.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `:4514` writes `{name, category, brand, licensed}`. `licensed` is a flag ABOUT a supplier that nothing gates on today; the prices live on `sf_vendor_prices`, which is already A |
| `sf_waivers` | **A** | **A** | A dues waiver is lost or recorded against the wrong member, and a governance decision cannot be shown | A named member is disclosed as **sick or in distress**, or as on active duty or recently separated. Health information and military status about an identified person | **2026-09-23 FULL `sf_*` RE-AUDIT, read individually.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `sfGrantWaiver()` at `:2931` writes `{memberId, condition, grantedBy, granted}`, and **`WAIVER_CONDITIONS` at `:2153` is the whole argument** — the three values are `active_duty`, `recent_sep` and `distress`, labelled *“Sick or in distress”*. `memberId` resolves to `sf_members`, already A/A, so the join names the person. The app already treats this as weightier than a preference: it refuses to record one without an officer, because *“a waiver is a governance decision and must record who granted it”* |
| `sf_youth_participants` | **A** | **B** | A minor’s participation is recorded with no consent, or consent is lost | An age and a reference for a MINOR — **deliberately not a name**, and that is a design decision rather than an omission | **2026-09-23 FULL `sf_*` RE-AUDIT, read individually.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `:5806` writes `{programId, ref, age, division, consent, consentDate}`. TIER A because `consent` and `consentDate` are the record that a minor’s participation was permitted, and losing that is not an operational loss. **NOT promoted on confidentiality, and the reason is the app’s own restraint:** the field is labelled *“Participant reference”* with the placeholder *“your own reference, not a full name”*, so the post keeps the identifying half and this platform does not. That is minimisation done on purpose and it is what keeps this row at B |

### `sairngrounds` — 30 resources

| Resource | Tier | Confidentiality | Worst consequence if it is wrong or lost | Worst consequence if it is read by the wrong person | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `golf_zones` | **B** | **B** | A course zone record is wrong | A client’s course layout and its condition notes. Commercially sensitive to them | **2026-09-23 SAIRNgrounds re-audit, read individually.** **CONFIRMED B, individually read rather than defaulted.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `:2541` writes `{property_id, name, acreage, serviced, condition, points}`. **THE `acreage` SUSPICION WAS TESTED HERE TOO AND FAILS THE SAME WAY IT DID ON `properties`:** `computeBOQ()` takes every quantity from `grd_irr_zones`, never from this resource, so no rate multiplies this acreage. `serviced` is rendered -- **AND THE “nothing computes an interval” CLAUSE THIS CELL CARRIED WAS FALSE WHEN WRITTEN, corrected 2026-09-24 (H1 log #530): THREE computations derive an interval from it** -- `grdAttentionDigest()` (:1414) and `rInsights()`'s overdue-zones KPI (:2267) both filter on `(now - new Date(z.serviced)) > 7 days` with an ABSENT `serviced` counting as overdue, and `gcdConditionsFor()` (:2836) computes `daysSinceServiced` for the conditions text. **TIER B STILL HOLDS, on the corrected reason:** what those intervals drive is maintenance ATTENTION -- a digest row, a KPI, a hole-condition note -- so a wrong `serviced` mis-prioritises upkeep and moves no money and no regulated record. The old clause understated the row's blast radius; the tier survives because the blast radius lands on scheduling, not on the classes that make a row A |
| `grd_boq_rates` | **A** | **B** | Every BOQ line priced from it, on every job quoted afterwards | A competitor or an unauthorised employee learns the company's own commercial figures. Commercially sensitive, not an elevated class: no PII, PHI, privileged communication or financial-account detail belonging to a third party sits on this row | **RE-TIERED 2026-09-22 on the MONEY criterion, which the A rule already states as &ldquo;money, or anything UPSTREAM of money&rdquo;.** It was B on the generic sentence &ldquo;employee-auth-gated operational data: neither money nor a regulated record&rdquo; &mdash; a default applied to 299 rows, and this is one of the ones it was wrong about. `grd_boq_rates` holds the company&rsquo;s pricing rules for bill-of-quantities lines: it does not record a price, it DECIDES one, on every BOQ built afterwards. **Same shape as `sd_pricing_rules`, which this register already rates A for exactly that reason** &mdash; &ldquo;pricing rules feed quotes, so one wrong rule is not one wrong record&rdquo;. The consequence of a wrong rate is not a lost row, it is a quoted job priced wrong and the error repeating silently until somebody re-derives a total by hand. No incident behind this one; it is the money limb, read the same way for the same shape. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (MONEY_INTERNAL)** |
| `grd_cart_orders` | **A** | **A** | A charge is lost, duplicated or wrong, and an ordered delivery never reaches the person waiting on the course | A named player&rsquo;s PRECISE POSITION AND TIME is disclosed &mdash; latitude, longitude and accuracy metres captured at the moment of the order | **RE-TIERED B &rarr; A on BOTH axes 2026-09-22 (Fourth), each read against `sairngrounds.html` rather than inferred from the resource name.** **MONEY:** `gcdPlaceOrder()` at `:3132` writes `items` and `total` &mdash; `gcdOrderTotal()` sums `price &times; qty` &mdash; with `status` Open and a `delivered_at` stamp set by `gcdMarkOrderDelivered()`. This is the transaction record for food and beverage sold on the course, so a lost or duplicated row is a charge not made or made twice. **The app already treats it that way:** both write paths carry an explicit sync-failure message, and the delivered path got one in 2026-09-04 precisely because a silent failure left the shop showing Open while the cart showed Delivered. **CONFIDENTIALITY, AND THIS IS THE LIMB THE B SENTENCE WAS MOST WRONG ABOUT:** the row stores `lat`, `lng` and `accuracy_m` from `cmCaptureGps()` beside `player_label`, a free-text name or group typed at the start of the round. The old cell read *&ldquo;no PII ... on this row&rdquo;*. A named individual&rsquo;s coordinates and timestamp are PII by any reading, and the order REFUSES to send without them &mdash; *&ldquo;a delivery with no position is worse than none&rdquo;* &mdash; so the position is not incidental, it is the point. **Confidentiality individually read 2026-09-22 (LOCATION)** |
| `grd_designs` | **A** | **B** | A design is wrong on its approval, and a quote is raised on a plan the client never approved — carrying that claim in its own line items | A client’s design walk and who approved it. Commercially sensitive to them, not an elevated class | **2026-09-23 SAIRNgrounds re-audit, read individually.** **IT GATES QUOTE CREATION, which is why it is A rather than a drawing record.** `sendDesignToQuote()` at `:2475` refuses outright — *“Design must be approved first”* — unless `rec.approved`, then writes a `quotes` row (already Tier A) whose lines come from `rec.elements` AND appends a line reading *“Design approved by &lt;approved_by&gt; on &lt;approved_at&gt;”*. **So the approval attribution on this row is PRINTED ON A CUSTOMER-FACING QUOTE.** **STATED BECAUSE IT BOUNDS THE CLAIM: this row does NOT price anything** — every line it contributes carries `a:0` and the quote is priced elsewhere. It is A on the gate and the attribution, not on arithmetic |
| `grd_dreamclose` | **B** | **B** | A closed-deal join record is lost, and the link from quote to invoice to schedule with it | Who approved a deal and what it became. Commercially sensitive, the firm’s own record | **2026-09-23 SAIRNgrounds re-audit, read individually.** **CONFIRMED B, individually read rather than defaulted.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `:4267` writes `{property_id, created_at, approved, approved_by, quote_id, invoice_id, schedule_id}`. **IT LOOKED LIKE AN AUTHORISATION RECORD AND IS NOT THE ONLY ONE, which is the reason it stays B — the same shape as `sf_documents`.** The invoice it creates at `:4260` stamps the approver into its OWN `notes` (*“From DreamClose proposal, approved by …”*), so losing this row does not lose the authorisation behind a money document. `approved` is also written as a literal `true` at creation rather than being a state that can fail. It is a convenience join across three resources that each hold their own copy of what matters |
| `grd_ecosystem_reports` | **B** | **B** | An ecosystem report is lost | A narrative summary about a client’s property. Commercially sensitive to them | **2026-09-23 SAIRNgrounds re-audit, read individually.** **CONFIRMED B, individually read rather than defaulted.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `:4164` writes `{property_id, date, summary}` — a free-text summary with no figure and no determination on the row |
| `grd_invasive_sightings` | **A** | **B** | A statutory report is shown as filed when it was not, or a reportable species is lost from the list nobody files from | A property&rsquo;s infestation and treatment history is disclosed. Commercially sensitive to that owner, not an elevated class: no PII, PHI, privileged communication or financial-account detail sits on this row | **RE-TIERED B &rarr; A on the REGULATED-RECORD limb 2026-09-22 (Fourth).** The B sentence said *&ldquo;neither money nor a regulated record&rdquo;*, and the second clause is false. `saveInvasive()` at `:4081` writes `reportable` and `report_filed`, and the Compliance view at `:4062` lists every `reportable` sighting with Filed / Not Filed against it &mdash; so **this row IS the record of whether a report was made to a state agency**, not a note about one. A wrong or lost row means either a required filing never happened and nothing shows it, or the register says Filed when it was not. **The app&rsquo;s own structure is the argument:** one dataset, six views, and the sixth is named Compliance. Same correction as `msb_licenses` and `grd_invoices` in this app, on the limb neither of those used. **CONFIDENTIALITY STAYS B AND IS INDIVIDUALLY READ:** `species`, `location`, `severity`, `method`, `outcome` and free-text `notes` describe a PROPERTY, and no person is named on the row. **Confidentiality individually read 2026-09-22 (COMMERCIAL)** |
| `grd_invoices` | **A** | **B** | An invoice wrong on a grounds contract | A competitor or an unauthorised employee learns the company's own commercial figures. Commercially sensitive, not an elevated class: no PII, PHI, privileged communication or financial-account detail belonging to a third party sits on this row | Money. Six client write sites. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (MONEY_INTERNAL)** |
| `grd_irr_controllers` | **A** | **B** | A controller’s brand is wrong, and every BOQ line for the zones on it is priced against the wrong rate | A client’s irrigation hardware. Commercially sensitive to them, not an elevated class | **2026-09-23 SAIRNgrounds re-audit (PARTIAL -- see the rollup cell).** **PROMOTED ON A FIELD NOTHING IN THE ROW’S OWN PANEL SUGGESTS MATTERS, which is why it needed the engine read rather than the record read.** `:3701` writes `{property_id, name, brand, model, zones_supported, install_date, notes}` and reads as an asset register. But `computeBOQ()` builds `ctlBrandById` from this resource and passes `brand` into `findBoqRate(item_type, brand)` — so the brand SELECTS THE RATE, and a wrong one either prices against another brand’s rate or falls through to the generic bucket. Upstream of money by one more hop than `grd_irr_zones`, and invisible from the row alone |
| `grd_irr_schedules` | **B** | **B** | A watering schedule is wrong | When a client’s irrigation runs. Operational | **2026-09-23 SAIRNgrounds re-audit, read individually.** **CONFIRMED B, individually read rather than defaulted.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `:3955` writes `{zone_id, days, start, …}`. Run times and days. It shares a panel with `grd_irr_zones`, which IS Tier A — but only the zone carries the `linear_ft` and `head_count` the BOQ multiplies. A schedule contributes no quantity |
| `grd_irr_zones` | **A** | **B** | A zone’s linear feet or head count is wrong, and every BOQ line priced from it — and the quote built on that — is wrong | A client’s irrigation layout. Commercially sensitive to them, not an elevated class | **2026-09-23 SAIRNgrounds re-audit (PARTIAL -- see the rollup cell).** **THIS ROW IS THE QUANTITY SIDE OF THE BILL OF QUANTITIES, and `grd_boq_rates` — already Tier A — is only the rate side.** READ OUT OF THE ENGINE, not inferred: `computeBOQ()` at `sairngrounds.html` takes its quantities ENTIRELY from this resource — `z.linear_ft` into `drip_lf` or `pipe_lf`, `z.head_count` into the head item for `z.zone_type`, a `valve` per zone — and returns `extended = rate × qty`. `sendBoqToQuote()` then pushes that total into `quotes`, already A. **THE FIRST READING OF THIS ROW WAS POINTED AT THE WRONG FIELD AND IS RECORDED AS WRONG:** it was flagged as a candidate on `last_service` and `backflow`, the `rf_safety_equipment` shape. **That test FAILS here** — `last_service` is only rendered (`:3669`) with nothing computing an interval from it, and `backflow` is FREE TEXT (*“e.g. RPZ, last tested 2026-04-01”*), so the regulated test date is prose inside a note and nothing could compute from it either. The row is A for a different reason than the one it was suspected on |
| `grd_progress_photos` | **A** | **B** | A QC record is wrong, and finished work cannot be marked complete — or unfinished work can be | Photographs of a client’s property and the names of who captured and who approved them. Commercially sensitive, not an elevated class | **2026-09-23 SAIRNgrounds re-audit (PARTIAL -- see the rollup cell).** **THIS IS A GATE, NOT A RECORD, AND THE CODE SAYS SO IN ITS OWN WORDS.** `grdCheckCompletionGate()` at `sairngrounds.html:3492` — commented *“the completion gate itself -- real check, not a formality”* — requires a photo with `is_final===true` AND `qc_status==='approved'` to exist for a schedule before `grdMarkScheduleComplete()` will run. **AND IT CARRIES A SECOND-PERSON RULE:** the pending-QC list at `:3521` filters `p.captured_by!==me`, so the approver cannot be the capturer. READ OUT OF THE APP: `:3464` writes `{schedule_id, property_id, photo_b64, is_final, ai_analysis, qc_status, captured_by, qc_by, qc_at}`. A wrong or lost row does not lose a photograph — it either blocks completed work or passes uncompleted work. Same limb as `subcontractors`, whose server refuses a non-compliant sub |
| `grd_rounds` | **A** | **A** | The shot, pace and hole-arrival record for a round is lost; cart orders placed against it lose the context they attach to | A CONTINUOUS MOVEMENT TRAIL for a named player &mdash; every shot&rsquo;s start and end coordinates with a timestamp, and an arrival and completion time per hole, over the hours of a round | **RE-TIERED B &rarr; A 2026-09-22 (Fourth) ON THE CONFIDENTIALITY LIMB ALONE, and the Tier cell follows from it rather than from the integrity one.** I first wrote Tier **B** here with Confidentiality **A**, and `criticality_tier_check.py` refused it: *the Tier cell is DERIVED, never hand-entered*, and Confidentiality A computes to Tier A whatever the other limb says. That refusal is correct and the correction is recorded rather than quietly made &mdash; a derived cell I typed by hand would have been two answers to one question. **WHY CONFIDENTIALITY IS A:** `gcdStartRound()` at `:2950` opens a round against a free-text `player_label`; `:2979` pushes a hole with `arrived_at` and `completed_at`; `:3021` pushes each shot as `{from: fix, to: fix, meters, band_meters, at}` where each fix is a GPS reading. That is not a position, it is a TRACK &mdash; where a named person was, in sequence, for hours. The old cell read *&ldquo;no PII ... on this row&rdquo;*. **THE INTEGRITY-AVAILABILITY LIMB IS STILL B AND THAT IS NOT A FORMALITY, SAID PLAINLY BECAUSE IT DEPARTS FROM THE BATCH THIS ROW ARRIVED IN:** no money is on this row. `total` lives on `grd_cart_orders`, which keeps its own figure, so a lost round orphans context rather than losing a charge, and nothing here is a regulated record. **AND RAISING IT WOULD REOPEN AN ACCEPTED RISK RATHER THAN CLOSE ONE:** `gcdSaveRound()` writes on EVERY shot and hole arrival with no failure handler, by a decision recorded in the code &mdash; a per-call toast would run continuously and drown the cart-order messages somebody is actually waiting on. Reading the INTEGRITY limb as A would put that accepted silent failure back in scope on the strength of a tier label rather than a fresh judgement, which is the wrong order to do it in &mdash; so the Tier is A for a CONFIDENTIALITY reason, and anybody acting on this row should act on the track, not on the shot log. **Confidentiality individually read 2026-09-22 (LOCATION)** |
| `grd_schedule` | **B** | **B** | A recurring service schedule is wrong or lost | Which crew is at which property and when. An internal rota | **2026-09-23 SAIRNgrounds re-audit, read individually.** **CONFIRMED B, individually read rather than defaulted.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `:3332` writes `{property_id, freq, next, crew, quote_id, status}`. No money and no regulated determination on the row. **NAMED TRIGGER, because it sits next to one:** `grd_progress_photos` gates completion BY `schedule_id`, so if marking a schedule complete ever triggers billing rather than only a status, re-read this row |

A completion record names the PERSON and asserts they are qualified -- it is the evidence produced when a regulator or an insurer asks, and a wrong one puts an untrained worker on a task or denies a trained one. Same class as `mech_credentials` and `rf_certifications`, both already Tier A. **Read from the app, not the schema; confidentiality individually read 2026-09-22** |

The `required` flag is the rule every `grd_training_completions` row is judged against, so one wrong boundary mis-states compliance for the whole workforce at once -- a one-row error with an organisation-wide blast radius. Confidentiality stays B because a catalogue names nobody; `dnt_procedure_types` and `sf_donor_tiers` are split the same way for the same reason. **Read from the app, not the schema; confidentiality individually read 2026-09-22** |

Here `terms` is free text that nothing parses and no computation reads |

No money and nothing upstream of money, no regulated record, no irreversible act, no recorded exposure. (2) `computeMsbFoodCostPct()` at `:4845` builds its DENOMINATOR by matching each sold item back to this resource and counting only `p.category==='food'`, so a miscategorised product moves a reported percentage the panel presents as a real operating figure |

MONEY on its face — `total` and `payment`. **AND A SECOND LIMB THAT IS EASY TO MISS: `age_verified` is set only on the bar cart and is the record that an alcohol sale was age-checked** — the artefact a liquor authority asks for, not an internal preference. It also feeds a reported figure directly: `computeMsbFoodCostPct()` sums `i.qty*i.price` off these rows for its revenue side |
| `properties` | **B** | **B** | A client property record is wrong or lost | A client’s contact name, phone, email and address. The `sd_customers` class — commercially sensitive, not an elevated one | **2026-09-23 SAIRNgrounds re-audit (PARTIAL -- see the rollup cell).** **CONFIRMED B, individually read, AND THE CANDIDATE ARGUMENT AGAINST IT WAS TESTED AND FAILED.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `:2303` writes `{name, type, acreage, contact, phone, email, address, status, notes}`. The suspicion was that `acreage` is a QUANTITY `grd_boq_rates` multiplies, the `rf_roof_sections.area_sqft` shape. **IT IS NOT.** `computeBOQ()` never reads this resource: every quantity comes from `grd_irr_zones` (`linear_ft`, `head_count`, `zone_type`) with a brand from `grd_irr_controllers`. `acreage` is rendered in the roster (`:2286`) and exposed to the AI property-roster tool (`:1392`), and multiplied by nothing. **NAMED TRIGGER: if any pricing path ever takes a quantity from `acreage`, re-read this row** — that is the whole reason it was suspected |
| `quotes` | **A** | **B** | A quote wrong | A competitor or an unauthorised employee learns the company's own commercial figures. Commercially sensitive, not an elevated class: no PII, PHI, privileged communication or financial-account detail belonging to a third party sits on this row | Money. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (MONEY_INTERNAL)** |

| Resource | Tier | Confidentiality | Worst consequence if it is wrong or lost | Worst consequence if it is read by the wrong person | Evidence |

| `law_bankstatements` | **A** | **B** | The bank side of an IOLTA three-way reconciliation is wrong, and the trust account reconciles clean when it does not | The firm’s own trust-account balance is disclosed. Commercially sensitive, not an elevated class: a balance and a date, with no client named and no account number on the row | **RE-TIERED B -> A on the TIER axis, confidentiality individually read and left at B, 2026-09-23. READ BY HANK, ROUTED RATHER THAN LANDED OVER AN ACTIVE CLAIM, AND RE-VERIFIED AGAINST THE APP BEFORE LANDING** — `saveBankStatement()` at `sairnlaw.html:3462` writes `{id, statement_date, bank_balance, created_at}`, which matches the routed read exactly. **TIER A BECAUSE IT IS THE RAW INPUT TO THREE ROWS THAT ARE ALREADY A.** `law_trust_reconcile` (A/A) runs the three-way IOLTA reconciliation — bank against ledger against client allocations — and `bank_balance` IS the bank leg. A wrong figure produces a false CLEAN or a false discrepancy on the one balance a bar association audits, and neither is visible from inside the app. `law_trusttx` and `law_opaccounts` are already A too. **An input at B with all three of its consumers at A is the split-limb inconsistency `sv_labresults` was corrected for, one step further back.** **AND THIS ROW HAS A LIVE OPEN DEFECT AGAINST IT, which is corroboration rather than decoration:** the open-work index records that two statements sharing a date resolve by INPUT ORDER, that nothing validates `statement_date` or `bank_balance` on write, and that an unreadable statement is dropped silently with no `unreadable_rows` counterpart. A resource that can flip an audit verdict on PostgREST row order is not Tier B. CONFIDENTIALITY-B on the settled firm’s-own-figure call already made for `bld_pos`, `sen_franchise_agreements` and `rf_prequal_documents` |
| `law_barcerts` | **A** | **B** | A bar admission shown as current when it is not | Little or nothing. A professional licence or certification is a public fact by design, and this row is what a customer or an inspector is meant to be shown | REGULATED: licensure to practise. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (LICENSURE)** |
| `law_clecredits` | **A** | **A** | A CLE record is lost or wrong, and an attorney&rsquo;s bar compliance is claimed or missed on it | A named attorney&rsquo;s professional compliance standing, by jurisdiction, is disclosed | **RE-TIERED B -> A on BOTH axes, 2026-09-23.** FOUND ON THE SECOND ATTEMPT -- and the FIRST attempt is why this row was held: a search near the resource name returned an `ESIGN` record sitting beside it, not a CLE credit, which is exactly the near-miss that makes guessing unsafe. The real record is in `saveCle()` at `sairnlaw.html:4388`: `{staff_name, jurisdiction, course_title, category, hours, date}`. A named attorney&rsquo;s continuing-education compliance BY JURISDICTION is the evidence produced to a bar association, and a shortfall is a licensing problem for a regulated professional rather than an administrative one. Same class as `mech_credentials`, `rf_certifications` and `grd_training_completions`, all already Tier A. **Read from the APP, not the schema; confidentiality individually read** |
| `law_clerequirements` | **A** | **B** | A jurisdiction's `hours_required` is wrong, and EVERY attorney's bar-CLE compliance badge for that jurisdiction flips silently -- behind on a wrong high figure, or falsely compliant on a wrong low one | A jurisdiction and an hours figure -- no person on the row; the attorneys it affects live on `law_clecredits` | **RE-TIERED B -> A on INTEGRITY, 2026-09-25 (H1 log #535, read individually out of the app).** READ OUT OF THE APP: one row per jurisdiction of `{jurisdiction, hours_required, period_years}` (`sairnlaw.html:4403`), consumed by `cleShortfalls()` at `:4417-4432`, which sums each attorney's `law_clecredits` hours and compares against `hours_required` to produce the shortfall list and the "behind" count `rCerts()` renders. **THE OTHER INPUT TO THE SAME COMPUTATION IS ALREADY A** -- `law_clecredits` was promoted A/A -- and a reference table that decides the verdict is not lower-stakes than the transactions it judges: this is the reference-upstream-of-the-alarm shape `sc_pctc` and `grd_boq_rates` carry, where a single wrong figure is wrong on EVERY comparison until somebody re-reads the statute. Confidentiality stays B, individually read: nothing on the row identifies a person |
| `law_clients` | **A** | **A** | The record anchoring every matter, invoice and trust entry for a client is lost or wrong | THE EXISTENCE OF THE REPRESENTATION is disclosed &mdash; that this named person, with this address and telephone number, has retained this firm | **RE-TIERED B &rarr; A 2026-09-22 (Fourth).** `saveClient()` at `sairnlaw.html:3002` writes `name`, `type`, `phone`, `email`, `address` and free-text `notes`. **THE DUTY OF CONFIDENTIALITY COVERS THE EXISTENCE AND SUBJECT OF THE MATTER, NOT ONLY THE COMMUNICATIONS INSIDE IT** &mdash; that sentence is already in `law_portalmessages`&rsquo; and `law_portalesign`&rsquo;s evidence cells, and the client list is that fact at its most basic: a list of who this firm acts for. The old cell read *&ldquo;no PII ... on this row&rdquo;* over name, home address and telephone. **The integrity limb follows the same read:** `law_matters`, `law_invoices` and `law_trusttx` all key on `client_id`, so a lost client row orphans a matter file, its billing and its trust ledger at once. **Confidentiality individually read 2026-09-22 (PRIVILEGE)** |

Confidentiality-A on the duty of confidentiality rather than on privilege: a litigation calendar names WHO the firm acts for and in what matter. That a deadline is also on a public docket does not make the firm's own list of them disclosable** |

A lost document is also a real integrity loss in its own right, which is why this one is A on both limbs rather than only the second |
| `law_mattermilestones` | **A** | **A** | A matter milestone wrong or lost -- internal progress tracking, recoverable from the matter itself | The progress of a named client's matter is disclosed -- what stage it has reached and therefore what is happening to them | **RE-TIERED B &rarr; A 2026-09-22 ON THE CONFIDENTIALITY AXIS ALONE, AND THIS ROW IS WHY THE SECOND AXIS EXISTS.** Losing a milestone is an operational nuisance: the matter survives it and the stage can be re-derived. READING one is not. It says what is happening to an identified client and when, which is client-confidential under the duty of confidentiality the 3.2 pass applied across this app. **The Tier cell is DERIVED as the worse of the two axes**, so Confidentiality-A makes the Tier A even though the integrity limb would have stayed B on its own -- exactly the case a single tier could not express, and the reason `sd_exec_msgs` sat at B for as long as it did |

A lost or altered `conflict_check_result` means the firm cannot show what it knew when it accepted the engagement, which is the evidence a bar complaint turns on. **CONFIDENTIALITY:** the row names the client, the opposing parties and the practice area &mdash; the subject of the representation, which the duty covers for the same reason `law_portalesign` was raised. **Confidentiality individually read 2026-09-22 (PRIVILEGE)** |

A task list read by the wrong party describes a firm's litigation strategy on an identified matter. **Integrity stays the weaker limb and is not dressed up**: a lost task is re-creatable from the matter, and saying so is what keeps the A tier meaning something when it is applied to rows where the loss really is unrecoverable |

Confidentiality-B, and it is the deliberate contrast with `law_trusttx` one row away: an OPERATING account is the firm's own money, and no client's funds or identity sit on the row. Leaving the parent at A and the ledger at B was therefore inverted: the row that was raised holds the opening number, the row left at B holds every movement since. `saveOpTx()` at `:4493` writes `account_id`, `type`, `amount`, `category`, `date` and `description` and syncs to the server, so these are recorded movements rather than a display list. **Same correction as `sb_ap`, `sb_invs`, `bld_change_orders`, `rf_draws`, `bld_draws` and `law_pimedical` &mdash; now seven rows carrying money under that one B sentence.** Confidentiality UNCHANGED at B and individually read: the row names an internal account id and a category, never a client. **Confidentiality individually read 2026-09-22 (MONEY_INTERNAL)** |

MONEY, not merely operational: a wrong row does not lose a note, it mis-states how much of a settlement belongs to the client. CONFIDENTIALITY is the flagger's payload signal confirmed: injury detail is the client's medical story and `multiplier` is the firm's own case-valuation judgement -- work product a counterparty would pay to read |

Its old cell said "**CONFIDENTIALITY NOT YET INDIVIDUALLY READ** ... carried at the B default until somebody reads it", which was an honest could-not-tell; this is the read it was waiting for. **AND THE INTEGRITY HALF IS ITS OWN ARGUMENT:** an execution record is the evidence that a document was agreed to, so a lost or altered one is not operational data -- it is an engagement, a fee agreement or a settlement authority that can no longer be shown to have been signed. **Confidentiality individually read 2026-09-22 (PRIVILEGE). CLASSIFICATION FIX, not a protection one:** the resource is already genuinely session-gated in `LAW_RESOURCES` and driven to 401 with no session in `api/sd-data-sairnlaw-resources.test.js`, so nothing about its handling changes here |
| `law_portalmessages` | **A** | **A** | Attorney-client privileged communications are lost, wrong, or read by somebody not entitled to them | Client confidences held by a law firm are disclosed. Under the duty of confidentiality this covers the existence and detail of the matter, not only the communications inside it, and a privileged communication disclosed to the wrong party can waive privilege irrecoverably for the whole matter | **RE-TIERED B -> A 2026-09-22**, hover-audit confirmed, and it is a CLASSIFICATION fix rather than a protection one: the resource is already genuinely session-gated (`LAW_RESOURCES` in `api/sd-data.js`, driven to 401 with no session in `api/sd-data-sairnlaw-resources.test.js`), so nothing about its handling changes here. What was wrong was the LABEL. It carried the B rule's sentence -- "neither money nor a regulated record" -- about ATTORNEY-CLIENT PRIVILEGED COMMUNICATION, which is a regulated record in the strongest sense this platform touches: privilege is a rule of evidence, and a message disclosed to the wrong party can waive it irrecoverably for the whole matter. Classified by the stated B rule rather than read individually, which is exactly the failure mode the header paragraph describes. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (PRIVILEGE)** |
| `law_timeentries` | **A** | **A** | A billable hour is lost or wrong, and an invoice to a client is computed from it | The NARRATIVE of work done on a named client&rsquo;s matter is disclosed &mdash; attorney work product | **RE-TIERED B -> A on BOTH axes, 2026-09-22.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `{matter_id, attorney, date, billing_code, hours, rate, description, billable, invoiced}`. TWO LIMBS AT ONCE. Money: the invoice total is hours &times; rate, and `sairnlaw.html:1977` records a real prior defect where an empty `billing_code` was written AND SYNCED on a billable hour. Privilege: `description` is the narrative of what was done on the matter -- work product, and the text a client reads on their bill. The B sentence &ldquo;neither money nor a regulated record&rdquo; was false about this row on both halves. **Read from the app, not the schema; confidentiality individually read 2026-09-22** |
| `law_trusttx` | **A** | **A** | A client trust account transaction wrong | Client confidences held by a law firm are disclosed. Under the duty of confidentiality this covers the existence and detail of the matter, not only the communications inside it, and a privileged communication disclosed to the wrong party can waive privilege irrecoverably for the whole matter | REGULATED AND MONEY, and the least forgiving pair on the platform: a lawyer`s trust account is held for a client. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (PRIVILEGE)** |
| `law_trust_reconcile` | **A** | **A** | A trust reconciliation says the books balance when they do not, or says they do not when they do | Client confidences held by a law firm are disclosed. Under the duty of confidentiality this covers the existence and detail of the matter, not only the communications inside it, and a privileged communication disclosed to the wrong party can waive privilege irrecoverably for the whole matter | REGULATED AND MONEY, and DERIVED -- it owns no table. Tier A because a bar association audits this figure and because BOTH directions are expensive: a false AGREES lets a shortfall sit, and a false DISAGREES sends a firm looking for money that is not missing. The two failure modes it is built against are an unreadable `law_trusttx` reconciling as a $0 ledger (refused with 503, `api/_lib/law-trust-reconcile-endpoint.test.js`) and an absent leg reported as agreement (`status` is PARTIAL, never a single `matches` boolean). **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (PRIVILEGE)** |

That is a named individual with both dates -- the pair that makes a vital record a vital record. `aff004ea` already re-tiered three SAIRNlegacy vital records on this ground and the CASE they all hang off was left at B, which is the same split-limb inconsistency `sv_labresults` was corrected for. **Read from the app, not the schema; confidentiality individually read 2026-09-22** |

| `leg_clergybookings` | **B** | **B** | Operational data lost or wrong | No elevated confidentiality class -- no PII, PHI, privileged communication, or financial-account detail on this row. Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read | Operational data lost or wrong: neither money nor a regulated record. Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read |
| `leg_cremations` | **A** | **A** | A cremation authorisation wrong, missing, or attached to the wrong decedent | A deceased person's identity, cause or circumstances, or the government identifiers attached to them, are disclosed to somebody they were never for | **RE-TIERED 2026-09-22, funeral vital-records cluster (1 of 3).** **REGULATED**: a cremation is statutorily authorised, not merely scheduled. The record carries the authorisation and the identifiers it rests on, and a cremation is the one act in this app that CANNOT BE UNDONE &mdash; every other wrong record can be corrected against the thing it describes, and this one cannot. That is the same irreversibility the A criteria already recognise for controlled substances, expressed in a different statute. Gated since `760a34a9`; the tier is the half that decides who must review a change to it. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (VITAL_RECORD)** |
| `leg_custodylog` | **A** | **A** | The record of whose hands held a decedent, and when, wrong or unrecoverable | A deceased person's identity, cause or circumstances, or the government identifiers attached to them, are disclosed to somebody they were never for | **RE-TIERED 2026-09-22, funeral vital-records cluster (2 of 3).** **REGULATED, and it is the one this platform has already described in those terms.** api/sd-data.js’s own gate comment calls it &ldquo;the document that says which human remains were in whose hands, and when&rdquo;, and the critical defect record for `760a34a9` says the same. A register that rates it B on &ldquo;neither money nor a regulated record&rdquo; disagrees with the handler guarding it and with the incident register describing it &mdash; three places, one resource, two answers. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (VITAL_RECORD)** |
| `leg_deathrecords` | **A** | **A** | A decedent’s identity, date of death or SSN fragment wrong, or read by somebody it was never for | A deceased person's identity, cause or circumstances, or the government identifiers attached to them, are disclosed to somebody they were never for | **RE-TIERED 2026-09-22, funeral vital-records cluster (3 of 3).** **REGULATED AND IT HAS ALREADY FAILED ONCE**, which is the third A criterion rather than an argument: `760a34a9` records it readable and writable on the licence key alone, reproduced live &mdash; a deceased person&rsquo;s name, date of death and the last four of an SSN returned to anything holding a key shipped to the browser. The defect register carries that as CRITICAL. A resource with a critical exposure on the record cannot also be &ldquo;neither money nor a regulated record&rdquo;. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (VITAL_RECORD)** |
| `leg_dispatches` | **A** | **A** | A dispatch leg wrong or lost -- which vehicle, which staff member, and where a decedent was collected from | The address a decedent was collected from, the time, and the named staff member who did it, disclosed to somebody they were never for | **RE-TIERED B &rarr; A 2026-09-22, BOTH AXES. CLASSIFICATION ONLY -- nothing about its handling changes here.** The whole `LEG_RESOURCES` map sits behind one `verifySessionToken(..., 'sairnlegacy')` before the dispatch (`api/sd-data.js:10974`), driven by `api/sd-data-leg-session-gate.test.js` -- cited rather than asserted, because this table does not verify code facts and `tools/criticality_tier_check.py` refuses an uncited one. Read from the app's own seed rather than inferred: a row carries `case_id`, `vehicle`, `staff_assigned`, **`pickup_location`**, `pickup_time`, `status` and `gps_lat`/`gps_lng`/`gps_accuracy`/`gps_updated_at`. **THAT IS THE TRANSPORT LEG OF THE CHAIN OF CUSTODY**, which `api/sd-data.js`'s own gate comment describes for `leg_custodylog` as *"the document that says which human remains were in whose hands, and when"* -- and this row is where and by whom they were collected, with GPS. `leg_custodylog`, `leg_cremations` and `leg_deathrecords` were all re-tiered A on 2026-09-22 as the funeral vital-records cluster; this is the same chain one link earlier and it was missed because the name reads like scheduling |

| `rf_bonding` | **A** | **B** | A surety limit or expiry is wrong, and work is bid or taken that the bond does not cover | The firm’s bonding capacity and surety relationship are disclosed — its own confidential commercial position, and not one of the classes this rule names | **2026-09-23 FULL `rf_*` RE-AUDIT, read individually.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `saveBond()` at `sairnroofing.html:4032` writes `{bonding_id, surety, agent, single_project_limit, aggregate_limit, effective_on, expires_on, source}`, and the `capacity` action computes whether a candidate project value fits inside the remaining limit. Bonding capacity decides what the firm may bid, which is money and, on public work, a precondition of the award. **AND THIS RESOURCE ALREADY HAS A DOCUMENTED FAILURE AGAINST IT** (2026-09-13): an absent table answered `{ok:true, data:[], provisioned:false}` and the panel printed *“No bonding letter recorded.”* — *“A contractor reads that as ‘you have no bond’; the truth was ‘the table was never created’.”* The A rule names a documented incident as sufficient on its own. Confidentiality-B on the same call as `rf_prequal_documents`, its sibling in the same panel |
| `rf_buildings` | **B** | **B** | A building record in the asset register is wrong | A client’s name and the address of a building they own. Commercially sensitive, the same class as a customer list | **2026-09-23 FULL `rf_*` RE-AUDIT, read individually.** **CONFIRMED B, individually read rather than defaulted, and it is one of the three rows in this app that did NOT move.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `saveBuilding()` at `sairnroofing.html:4650` writes `{building_id, name, customer, address}` — no money field, no regulated content, no clock. **THE NEAR-MISS IS RECORDED BECAUSE IT IS INSTRUCTIVE:** the SERVER carries a `location_id` on this table and the roof-section entity filter (`bldLoc` at `api/sd-data.js:7671`, the rf_buildings select that feeds it) uses it to attribute sections into an entity's capital forecast, which is a real chain. **CITATION CORRECTED 2026-09-25 (H1 log #536): this cell said line 7164, which is now the rf_bonding write -- the line drifted 216 lines from its own commit, which is why the corrected form cites the CONSTRUCT beside the number.** It does not promote this row -- but the sentence that used to close this cell, "the app cannot set it wrong", WAS ONLY EVER TRUE OF THE UI FORM AND BECAME THE DEFECT: the write handler upserted location_id as trim-or-null FROM AN ABSENT PAYLOAD FIELD on EVERY write while the one live caller sends only {building_id, name, customer, address}, so RENAMING a building from the app silently nulled its server-side location assignment and detached it from that same forecast chain (`active` had the worse twin -- an absent field resolved to TRUE, reactivating deactivated buildings). FIXED 2026-09-25 in the same read: absent payload keys are now OMITTED from the upsert, so merge-duplicates leaves them untouched. **If a building's location ever becomes user-editable, re-read this row** -- the trigger stands, now with the null-overwrite lesson beside it |

Commercially sensitive, not an elevated class: no PII, PHI, privileged communication or financial-account detail belonging to a third party sits on this row | **RE-TIERED 2026-09-22 on the first A criterion &mdash; money, and anything upstream of money.** The B cell read *"neither money nor a regulated record"*, which is false about a payment draw. `rf_draws` is dispatched at `resource` `api/sd-data.js:7390` for `read`, `write` AND a `wip` action, and its figures run through `api/_lib/wip-accounting.js`, whose own header names the three fields: `retainage_held` (gross), `retainage_released` (paid back out) and `retainage_outstanding` (held &minus; released) &mdash; *"THIS is the number a screen should show under the words retainage held"*. **THE PLATFORM ALREADY TREATED IT AS MONEY-CRITICAL BEFORE THE TIER SAID SO,** which is the tell: `14137f40` is titled *"retainage went in and never came back out"* and `237458b8` built progress billing and retainage for this resource. Two engineering fixes on a resource whose tier row said it holds no money. **SAME CRITERION AND SAME MECHANISM AS `bld_change_orders` (2026-09-21):** the B rule is applied BY RULE, NOT BY READING &mdash; the old cell says so in its own words &mdash; so the file was never opened. **No incident behind this one:** a tier correction, not a finding. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (MONEY_INTERNAL)** |
| `rf_entities` | **A** | **A** | A legal entity record is lost or wrong, and consolidation, filing or liability is attributed to the wrong company | An entity&rsquo;s TAX IDENTIFICATION NUMBER is disclosed | **RE-TIERED B -> A on BOTH axes, 2026-09-22.** THE TAX ID IS THE WHOLE ARGUMENT ON THE CONFIDENTIALITY AXIS -- an EIN, and for a sole proprietor an EIN is frequently the proprietor&rsquo;s SSN. The redaction pass reviewed earlier today treats SSN/EIN as a REMOVE precisely because of this. On the integrity axis the entity row is what multi-entity consolidation attributes revenue and liability by (`api/_lib/roofing-consolidation.js`). **Confidentiality individually read 2026-09-22** |
| `rf_invoices` | **A** | **B** | An invoice issued wrong, or a payment against the wrong invoice | A property owner's insurance-claim detail is disclosed. Sensitive to the customer, not an elevated class: no health, legal-privilege, government-identifier or compensation data on this row | Money, and the strongest on the platform: the resource carries the verbs `[issue, add_payment, reconcile_claim]` and `sairnroofing.html:2912` calls `add_payment` live. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (PROPERTY_CLAIM)** |
| `rf_job_hazard_assessments` | **A** | **B** | A hazard assessment is lost or wrong, and a crew works a roof with no recorded briefing — or one that names the wrong hazards | Which crew acknowledged a briefing on which job. Attendance against a job, with nothing asserted about any individual beyond it | **2026-09-23 FULL `rf_*` RE-AUDIT, read individually.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `saveJha()` at `sairnroofing.html:4248` writes `{jha_id, job_id, assessed_on, competent_person, hazards, controls, acknowledged_by}`. **`competent_person` IS A DEFINED OSHA TERM, not a label somebody chose**, and `acknowledged_by` is the crew who signed the briefing — which the safety panel cross-checks against the crew actually SCHEDULED that day, its own description being *“who is on the roof without signing”*. This is the record an inspector asks for, and a missing name in it is a worker on a roof with no briefing on file. **NOT promoted on confidentiality, and the boundary against `bld_incidents` is stated rather than assumed:** that row is Confidentiality-A because an OSHA 300 entry is a named employee’s INJURY, which is personal medical information. An acknowledgement list is attendance |
| `rf_job_warranties` | **A** | **B** | A registration deadline or coverage expiry is wrong, and a customer&rsquo;s warranty is void with no way to reinstate it | A job&rsquo;s warranty registration is disclosed. The customer&rsquo;s identity lives on `rf_jobs`; this row carries `job_id`, not a name | **RE-TIERED B -> A on the TIER axis, confidentiality individually read and left at B, 2026-09-23.** READ OUT OF THE HANDLER: `{warranty_id, job_id, manufacturer, tier_id, tier_name, status, installed_on, **registered_on**, **register_within_days**, registration_number, coverage_years, **coverage_expires_on**}`. **`register_within_days` IS THE WHOLE INTEGRITY ARGUMENT:** a manufacturer warranty must be registered inside a window measured from installation, and a window missed is not recoverable by fixing the record afterwards -- the coverage simply does not exist, and the customer finds out when they claim. An irreversible consequence from a wrong date field. **THE PAIR IS REAL AND IS WHY THESE TWO LAND TOGETHER:** `tier_id`/`tier_name` resolve through `rf_warranty_tiers`, whose `requires_program_id` resolves to `rf_company_programs` -- so a wrong programme row upstream produces a warranty here that cannot be honoured downstream. **NOT PROMOTED ON CONFIDENTIALITY, and this is where it parts company with the siblings it was grouped with:** `law_clecredits` and `bld_subs` carry a named person and this row does not. It carries `job_id` and a `registration_number`, and admitting a bare reference as an elevated class would admit every foreign key on the platform -- the line held on `bld_pos`, `sen_franchise_agreements` and `sc_prebill`. **Read from the handler and the app, not the schema** |
| `rf_jobs` | **A** | **B** | A job’s location is wrong or absent, and every invoice raised against it is stamped into the wrong legal entity — or silently into the DEFAULT branch | A customer’s name and property address. Commercially sensitive, the same class as a customer list, not an elevated one | **2026-09-23 FULL `rf_*` RE-AUDIT, read individually.** READ OUT OF THE APP AND THEN TRACED THROUGH THE SERVER, because the client row alone does NOT justify this and the first reading of it was wrong. `sairnroofing.html:5254` writes `{id, name, address, job_class, status, assigned_employee_id, location_id}` — no money field on the row. **THE CHAIN IS WHAT MAKES IT A, and it was verified rather than assumed:** `api/sd-data.js` sets an invoice’s `location_id` from the gated job’s own `location_id`, falling back to `roofingLocations.DEFAULT_LOCATION_ID` when the job has none — so **the invoice INHERITS its branch from the job**, and a job with no location does not error — it picks the default branch. `roofing-consolidation.js` then buckets that invoice into a legal entity by exactly that field. The client already guards one half of this: `location_id` is omitted from a save unless the user actually picked one, *“what stops a blank dropdown from silently reattributing an edited job to the default branch”*. **A GUARD IN THE CLIENT IS EVIDENCE THE HAZARD IS REAL, not evidence it is handled** |
| `rf_locations` | **A** | **B** | A branch’s entity assignment is wrong, and every invoice raised there lands in the wrong legal entity’s book | The firm’s own branch and legal-entity structure is disclosed. Commercially sensitive, no third party’s data on the row | **2026-09-23 FULL `rf_*` RE-AUDIT, read individually.** READ OUT OF THE APP AND OUT OF THE ENGINE, because the tier turns on the engine: `sairnroofing.html:3769` writes `{id, name, active, entity_id}`, and `api/_lib/roofing-consolidation.js` states outright that **“entity_id lives ON THE location”** and that the `location_id -> entity_id` lookup is **“the entire mechanism”** of multi-entity financial consolidation. A branch with no `entity_id` is bucketed to *Unassigned* rather than dropped — the engine is careful — but a branch pointed at the WRONG entity moves its whole history with it, silently and with no backfill, which is exactly what the client comment describes when it says the reassignment is *“an ordinary write to the LOCATION”*. Money, upstream, and one field wide |
| `rf_photos` | **B** | **B** | A measurement photo or the AI’s raw reading of it is lost, and the provenance behind a quantity is gone | A photograph of a client’s roof and what an AI said about it. Commercially sensitive to that client, not an elevated class | **2026-09-23 FULL `rf_*` RE-AUDIT, read individually.** **CONFIRMED B, individually read, AND THIS IS THE ONE THAT LOOKED MOST LIKE AN A.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `saveMeasurementReading()` at `sairnroofing.html:5348` writes `{job_id, photo_base64, ai_analysis, parsed_quantities}` — and `parsed_quantities` is a quantity schedule, which is upstream of every estimate. **THE NEXT LINE IS WHY IT STAYS B:** the same function immediately writes the SAME values onto `rf_jobs.measurement_correction`, and that is the copy the app reads, corrects and prices from. This row is the photograph and the AI’s raw text kept beside it — the PROVENANCE of a quantity, not the quantity. **The boundary against `rf_claim_photos` (A/B) is the point:** those photos ARE the evidence submitted to an insurer, so losing them loses the claim. Losing these loses the audit trail behind a number that still exists |
| `rf_prequal_documents` | **A** | **B** | A prequalification document expires unnoticed, and a bid is submitted against a lapsed EMR letter, bond letter or financial statement | THE FIRM&rsquo;S OWN CONFIDENTIAL COMMERCIAL POSITION is disclosed &mdash; its financial statements and its workers&rsquo;-compensation experience rating. Commercially sensitive to the firm, and not one of the four classes this rule names | **RE-TIERED B -> A on the TIER axis, confidentiality individually read and left at B, 2026-09-23.** READ OUT OF THE HANDLER: `PQ_SELECT = document_id, kind, issuer, effective_on, **expires_on**, **value**, value_year, reference, source, notes`. **THE `kind` FIELD IS NAMED IN THE UI&rsquo;S OWN PLACEHOLDERS, which is the citation rather than an inference:** `sairnroofing.html:714` and `:5983` both read *&ldquo;emr_letter, financials, safety_program, references, bond_letter&rdquo;*. So this table holds the firm&rsquo;s FINANCIAL STATEMENTS and its EMR letter -- the workers&rsquo;-compensation experience modification rate, which encodes its injury history and is what a general contractor prices risk from -- with a dollar `value` and a `value_year` beside them. **TIER A ON THE INTEGRITY AXIS:** every kind carries an `expires_on`, and a bid submitted against a lapsed bond letter or EMR is one that fails prequalification or, worse, wins on a representation that is no longer true. **CONFIDENTIALITY LEFT AT B DELIBERATELY, and the comparison it was dispatched with SPLITS:** `alf_facility` is A/B for the same firm-not-person reason and agrees; `rf_entities` is A/A only because it carries a TAX ID, which for a sole proprietor is frequently an SSN. Nothing on this row is a government or personal identifier, so promoting it would break the line held on `bld_pos` and `sen_franchise_agreements`. **Read from the handler and the app, not the schema** |
| `rf_proposals` | **A** | **A** | A proposal issued at the wrong price, or an issued price changed after the fact | A customer's captured ACCEPTANCE SIGNATURE is disclosed, together with who accepted and by what method -- a reusable instrument, not a record about a person | **RE-TIERED B &rarr; A 2026-09-22 on the MONEY limb, then CONFIDENTIALITY B &rarr; A the same day on hank's routed correction, which is right and which corrects me.** `api/_resources/sairnroofing.js:56` states the money half: *"rf_proposals is APPEND-ONLY and every issued row SNAPSHOTS its price rather than pointing at the live estimate"* -- the row IS the priced commitment, the rule already applied to `bld_bids`. **MY CONFIDENTIALITY-B CALL WAS WRONG AND THE REASON IS WORTH KEEPING: I READ ONE OF THE TWO ROW SHAPES.** `sql/sairnroofing_billing_schema.sql:80-83` documents the `data` blob as carrying different fields per `event_type` -- an `issued` row holds `line_items, tax_rate, tax, issued_on, valid_until, terms, notes`, which is what I read and which genuinely carries no elevated class, but a `decision` row holds `decided_on, acceptance_method, **accepted_by, signature_data**, reason`, and the table's own 1.5MB cap exists because *"an acceptance signature is optional but when captured it is an image, not app data"*. **A STORED SIGNATURE IS NOT A RECORD ABOUT A PERSON, IT IS A REUSABLE INSTRUMENT THAT CAN BE LIFTED AND RE-APPLIED** -- the exact argument `sf_signatures` was re-tiered on earlier the same day, by me, which is how I came to apply it to one resource and miss it on another within one session. **THE GENERAL RULE THE CORRECTION ESTABLISHES: a tier must cover what the resource CAN hold, not the row shape whichever reader opened first** |
| `rf_roof_sections` | **A** | **B** | A roof’s recorded service life is wrong, and the capital replacement plan a client budgets from is wrong with it | A client’s building portfolio and its condition are disclosed. Commercially sensitive to that client, not an elevated class | **2026-09-23 FULL `rf_*` RE-AUDIT, read individually.** READ OUT OF THE APP AND THE ENGINE: `sairnroofing.html:4696` writes `{section_id, building_id, name, system_type, area_sqft, installed_on, expected_life_years, life_source, condition_score, condition_on, warranty_id, status}`, and `api/_lib/roofing-asset-registry.js` computes `replacement_year = installed_on + expected_life_years` and builds the capital/lifecycle forecast from it. **BOTH SIDES ALREADY REFUSE TO GUESS, AND THAT IS THE ARGUMENT RATHER THAN A MITIGATION:** the client refuses a garbled number because *“a garbled service life silently becoming null would drop this roof out of the capital plan without saying so”*, and the engine refuses for the same reason and files the section under `unplannable`. Two independent authors treated one field as load-bearing on a budget. That is upstream of money |
| `rf_safety_equipment` | **A** | **B** | A fall-protection item shows as in-date when its inspection has lapsed, and somebody goes on a roof wearing it | An unauthorised reader learns which equipment is out of inspection and who last signed for it. The inspector's name is a tradesperson doing their job, not an elevated class | **RE-TIERED B -> A on the TIER axis, confidentiality individually read and left at B, 2026-09-23.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `saveEquip()` at `sairnroofing.html:4213` writes `{equipment_id, kind, identifier, job_id, in_service_on, last_inspected_on, last_inspected_by, inspection_interval_days, interval_source, status}`. **THIS ROW IS AN INSPECTION CLOCK, NOT AN ASSET LIST, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE DIFFERENCE.** `last_inspected_on` plus `inspection_interval_days` IS the due date the safety board computes from (`rfDataRaw('board', ...)` at `:4090`), so a wrong interval or a wrong last-inspected date does not misreport a record -- it silently extends the service life of fall-protection equipment. REGULATED: periodic inspection of personal fall-arrest equipment is an OSHA requirement and this is the record of it. **AND THE APP ALREADY KNOWS IT IS LOAD-BEARING:** the panel refuses to imply a verdict without the board, printing *“Safety board not computed — nothing on this screen should be read as a compliance determination”* rather than a blank. A resource whose own UI carries that sentence is not Tier B. NOT promoted on confidentiality: no compensation, no medical detail, no protected individual. `bld_incidents` is Confidentiality-A because an OSHA 300 entry is a named employee’s INJURY; an equipment inspection log is not that |
| `rf_schedule` | **B** | **B** | A scheduled day is wrong, or a crew is booked against the wrong job | Which crew is on which job on which day. No elevated class — an internal rota | **2026-09-23 FULL `rf_*` RE-AUDIT, read individually.** **CONFIRMED B, individually read.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `sairnroofing.html:5073` writes `{id, job_id, scheduled_date, status, crew, notes}`. No money, no regulated content. **Checked specifically because the double-booking check LOOKS like a gate and is not one:** the server reports conflicts and deliberately does NOT refuse, on the stated ground that two jobs in a day is a normal roofing day — and `conflicts:null` is surfaced as *“double-booking not checked”* rather than shown as clear, which is the could-not-tell third state handled correctly. A resource whose check is advisory by design is not load-bearing on it |
| `rf_settings` | **A** | **B** | The company’s hail and wind damage thresholds are wrong, and roofs are assessed as claimable when they are not, or the reverse | The firm’s own assessment thresholds and the sources it cites for them. Commercially sensitive, no individual on the row | **2026-09-23 FULL `rf_*` RE-AUDIT, read individually.** **C -> A, A TWO-STEP PROMOTION, AND THE SHARPEST FINDING OF THIS PASS: THIS ROW WAS FILED AS A PREFERENCE AND IS A DECISION RULE ON INSURANCE CLAIMS.** The C tier is *“cosmetic, device state, or preference — the shape already excluded from server sync for being regenerable or per-device”*, and every clause of that is false here. READ OUT OF THE APP: `rfSaveThresholds()` at `sairnroofing.html:2472` writes `{setting_key:'damage_threshold', value:{hail:{hits_per_test_square, source}, wind:{…}}}` — the hits-per-test-square counts a slope is assessed against, feeding the damage assessment behind `rf_claims`, which is already Tier A. **THREE THINGS IN THE CODE SAY IT IS NOT A PREFERENCE:** it is owner/admin only (`rfDmgIsManagement()`); a threshold **cannot be saved without a cited SOURCE**, refused on the client AND on the server, which is something no preference has ever needed; and it is explicitly a COMPANY value that a per-claim `threshold_override` is measured against. A per-device preference does not get a provenance requirement. **HOW IT WAS MISSED IS THE GENERALISABLE PART:** the resource is named `rf_settings`, and the name is the only thing about it that says preference |
| `rf_supplier_documents` | **A** | **B** | A purchase order, receipt or invoice wrong on its price or quantity, and the three-way match that would have caught an unordered charge passes instead | A competitor or an unauthorised employee learns what the company pays its suppliers. Commercially sensitive, not an elevated class: the row names a SUPPLIER and an item code, not a protected individual | **RE-TIERED B -> A on the TIER axis, confidentiality individually read and left at B, 2026-09-23.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `sairnroofing.html:1826` writes `{document_id, doc_type, po_number, supplier, supplier_ref, doc_date, lines:[{item_code, description, qty, unit_price}]}`. **`unit_price` IS A REAL DOLLAR FIGURE ON A PO-SHAPED LINE ITEM, which is exactly why `bld_pos` was promoted on 2026-09-22** — same shape, same limb, and this one was left behind. **THE MATCH IS THE ARGUMENT, not the storage:** `rfSupplierMatch()` at `:1758` runs a three-way order/receipt/invoice reconciliation with quantity and price tolerances, and the server’s own refusal names the real finding — an invoice with no order behind it is an UNORDERED CHARGE. A resource whose purpose is catching money that should not be paid is upstream of money by definition. One thing found while reading and worth keeping: a blank quantity is stored as ABSENT and never as zero, because *“nobody wrote down what arrived”* and *“nothing arrived”* are different facts — the app says so in its own comment, and sending 0 would turn every unscanned delivery into a short shipment. NOT promoted on confidentiality: supplier pricing is the firm’s own commercial figure, the `bld_pos` and `sf_vendor_prices` shape |
| `rf_warranty_tiers` | **A** | **B** | A tier’s required certification is wrong, and a manufacturer warranty is offered that the firm cannot actually back | A manufacturer’s tier structure and what it requires. Reference content, not data about a person | **2026-09-23 FULL `rf_*` RE-AUDIT, read individually.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `saveTier()` at `sairnroofing.html:4843` writes `{tier_id, manufacturer, tier_name, requires_program_id, source}`. **IT DOES NOT RECORD A WARRANTY, IT DECIDES WHETHER ONE MAY BE OFFERED** — `requires_program_id` is the join to `rf_company_programs` and `rf_certifications`, both already Tier A, and the panel keeps the two questions deliberately apart because they fail differently: *“may we offer this tier at all”* versus the warranty itself. A wrong `requires_program_id` sells a homeowner a warranty with nothing behind it. Same upstream-of-money limb as `rf_cert_rules`, already A |
| `sub_assignments` | **A** | **B** | An assignment’s agreed amount or its payment history is wrong, and a subcontractor is under- or over-paid | What this firm agreed to pay a named subcontractor is disclosed. The firm’s own commercial terms with a vendor, not a protected individual’s data | **2026-09-23 FULL `rf_*` RE-AUDIT, read individually.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `sairnroofing.html:3539` writes `{assignment_id, sub_id, job_id, scheduled_date, status, amount, payments}`. MONEY, and the panel says so in its own words — the directory answers *“is this sub papered”* and this answers *“what is still owed him”*. **A REAL DATA-LOSS HAZARD IS ALREADY GUARDED HERE AND IS WORTH RECORDING RATHER THAN REDISCOVERING:** `payments` is written as a WHOLE ARRAY on a whole-row upsert, and the draft is seeded from what the server returned precisely so that adding one payment appends instead of erasing every payment already recorded. Confidentiality-B on the settled vendor-terms line (`bld_pos`, `sf_vendor_prices`) |
| `subcontractors` | **A** | **A** | A subcontractor shows as papered when their insurance or licence has lapsed, and they are assigned to a roof | A subcontractor’s licence number, insurer and compliance standing are disclosed — commercially sensitive to them, and the reconnaissance step for anyone deciding who to target | **2026-09-23 FULL `rf_*` RE-AUDIT, read individually.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `saveSub()` at `sairnroofing.html:3438` writes `{sub_id, name, trade, phone, coi_carrier, coi_expiry, licence_no, licence_expiry, w9_on_file, active}`. **THIS IS `bld_subs` IN ANOTHER APP, AND IT IS LOAD-BEARING ON A GATE IN THE SAME WAY.** `sub_assignments` sends `required` and `today` with every write specifically so the SERVER can refuse a non-compliant sub, and the panel shows the refusal verbatim — *“Cannot assign: coi is expired”*. So a wrong `coi_expiry` or `licence_expiry` does not mislabel a directory entry, it puts an uninsured or unlicensed crew on a roof, or blocks a compliant one from working. Confidentiality-A on `bld_subs`’s own reasoning, individually re-read here rather than inherited |

### `sairnscape` — 12 resources

| Resource | Tier | Confidentiality | Worst consequence if it is wrong or lost | Worst consequence if it is read by the wrong person | Evidence |

| `customers` | **B** | **B** | Operational data lost or wrong | No elevated confidentiality class -- no PII, PHI, privileged communication, or financial-account detail on this row. Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read | Operational data lost or wrong: neither money nor a regulated record. Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read |
| `invoices` | **A** | **B** | An invoice wrong | A competitor or an unauthorised employee learns the company's own commercial figures. Commercially sensitive, not an elevated class: no PII, PHI, privileged communication or financial-account detail belonging to a third party sits on this row | Money. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (MONEY_INTERNAL)**. **GATE ARMED 2026-09-25 (Cody), and the tier is what asked for it:** this row -- SAIRNscape's money row, stored as `scp_invoices` and dispatched under the BARE name `invoices` -- was authorised by the LICENCE KEY ALONE, a key shipped to the browser and readable by anyone who can open `sairnscape.html`. All twelve SAIRNscape resources were, and SAIRNscape was swept with neither the `law_trusttx` sweep (2026-09-16) nor the SF/SDN ones. `api/sd-data.js` now requires a verified `sairnscape` session on read AND write (`SD_SESSION_GATED` + `SD_GATE_APP`, 403 FORBIDDEN, plus the active-credential re-check), driven both directions in `api/sd-data-scp-session-gate.test.js`. **NO CLIENT CHANGE WAS NEEDED AND THAT WAS MEASURED, NOT ASSUMED:** `scpData()` attaches `X-SD-Auth` on every call whenever a token exists -- there is no per-call flag to forget, which is exactly what had to be repaired in `sairndesign.html` -- and both login paths store the token BEFORE the first server read. **NAMED TRIGGER: the RESOURCE is `invoices`, not `scp_invoices`.** A gate written against the storage-table name gates nothing and every refusal arm still passes; the suite carries an arm whose only job is that distinction |

| `scp_water_features` | **B** | **B** | Operational data lost or wrong | No elevated confidentiality class -- the row carries a `customer_id` FOREIGN KEY and no name, address or contact detail; the identity lives on the customer row, not this one. **Individually read 2026-09-23 (cc), not defaulted** | **INDIVIDUALLY READ 2026-09-23 (cc), AND IT STAYS B -- the read is the change, not the tier.** The record is `{id, customer_id, name, type, scale, size, pump, filter, last_service, condition, checklist}`: pond and fountain equipment, a service date and a condition note. No money and nothing upstream of money, no regulated record, no irreversible act, no recorded exposure. A wrong row means a feature is serviced on the wrong schedule. **WHY THIS ROW EXISTS AT ALL:** it was dispatched to me as a B→A promotion on the stated ground that its sibling was already A. Both siblings were B/B and BOTH carried "classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read" -- so the promotion would have propagated a tier that did not exist. Reading the code refused it. The tier is unchanged and is now EVIDENCED rather than defaulted |

### `sairnsenior` — 15 resources
