| `sen_applicants` | **B** | **B** | Operational data lost or wrong | A staff APPLICANT's name and phone number -- identity of a person who applied to work at the facility. **The “no PII” clause this row carried was stale and is corrected 2026-09-24 (H2 seq #210): the resource carries name+phone**, so the blanket sentence was wrong on its face | **INDIVIDUALLY READ TWICE, no longer “classified by the stated B rule”** -- H1 seq 492 and H2 seq 57, both landing **B** on the staff-side convention: applicant identity is the same class as the roster identity `sen_`/`alf_` staff rows already hold at B (employment-side contact detail, not resident PHI, not money, not a regulated record). Both clauses of the old cell were stale -- the “no PII” claim and the “never individually read” claim -- and a register row that is wrong twice about its own evidence is the under-tiering accident case item 97 names, which is why the correction records BOTH reads rather than only the verdict |
| `sen_authorizations` | **A** | **A** | An authorisation is lost or wrong, and care is delivered outside it or a client is reported exhausted when they are not | A named client, their PAYER, the service authorised and the period are disclosed | **RE-TIERED B -> A on BOTH axes, 2026-09-23.** FOUND ON THE SECOND ATTEMPT, at `sairnsenior.html:4929`: `{client_id, client_name, payer, auth_number, service_code, units_authorized, minutes_per_unit, start_on, end_on, active, notes}`. A named person joined to a PAYER and a service code is PHI in the same way `sc_compliance` is -- payment for the provision of care to an identified individual. On the integrity axis the app states the consequence in its own refusal text: a zero-unit authorisation *&ldquo;would report the client as exhausted while looking real&rdquo;*, and units are what billing burns down against. **Read from the APP, not the schema; confidentiality individually read** |
| `sen_branches` | **A** | **B** | A branch&rsquo;s two-letter `state` is wrong, and EVERY client of that branch is billed at another state&rsquo;s contract rate &mdash; or at zero &mdash; on every completed visit, silently. The row is a reference table upstream of money, not a location label | No elevated confidentiality class &mdash; a branch name, a state, a phone number and an address belonging to the AGENCY, not to a person. No client, no caregiver, no PHI | **RE-TIERED B -> A on the INTEGRITY axis, individually read 2026-09-25 (Hank). Confidentiality individually read and left at B.** **`state` IS A MONEY-RESOLUTION INPUT AND THE PATH IS THREE FUNCTIONS LONG.** `saveBranch()` (`sairnsenior.html:5160`) stores `{id, name, state, phone, address, active}` and already refuses a non-two-letter state on BOTH client and server &mdash; the app saying in its own code that this field is matched on, not displayed. `pcStateForClient()` (`:4146`) resolves a CLIENT&rsquo;s state by joining `sen_clients.branch_id` to this row. `pcResolveRate()` (`:4153`) then filters the in-force `sen_payer_contracts` to that state, preferring a state-scoped contract over a state-less one. `:3821` calls both per COMPLETED VISIT to set the claim&rsquo;s `rate` and `amount`. **A WRONG STATE FAILS TWO WAYS AND ONE OF THEM IS SILENT:** if the other state has a contract for that payer, the claim is written at THAT state&rsquo;s `rate_per_hour` &mdash; a plausible number nobody questions; if it does not, `pcResolveRate` returns `none`, the claim is created at ZERO, and the reason names the branch&rsquo;s state, which is at least visible. The dangerous half is the first. **FOURTH INSTANCE OF ONE FAMILY THIS ROTATION**, and the register has already decided the other three the same way: `sc_pctc` (A/B &mdash; a PC/TC indicator upstream of a path check), `law_clerequirements` (A/B &mdash; an hours figure every compliance verdict is computed against), and `dnt_cred_rules`. Same sentence as `sd_pricing_rules`: **one wrong rule is not one wrong record.** **WHAT BOUNDS IT, stated rather than omitted:** the two-letter format guard means a TYPO is usually refused, so the live failure is a VALID code for the wrong state &mdash; and the branch list is small and rarely edited, so the exposure is a rare edit with a wide blast radius rather than a common one | 
| `sen_caregivers` | **A** | **A** | A CPR expiry or background-check date is wrong or missing, and a named home-health aide is removed from the expiry alert entirely -- the one failure nothing on the screen reports -- or is shown as cleared to be alone in a client's home when they are not | A named individual's phone number, background-check date and STATE CAREGIVER REGISTRATION ID. A government identifier tied to a named person is on this register's stated Confidentiality-A list, and the row also asserts whether that person passed a background check | **INDIVIDUALLY READ OUT OF THE APP 2026-09-23 (Hank).** **B -> A on BOTH axes.** `saveCaregiver()` at `sairnsenior.html:2574` writes `{id, name, phone, cpr_expiry, bgcheck_date, status, notes, aide_type, program, state_caregiver_id, branch_id}`. **`cpr_expiry` IS NOT A LABEL, IT IS THE ALARM** -- the same sentence `sb_train` was re-tiered on, and it is true here for the same mechanical reason. `certDaysUntil()` derives the badge at `:2593` (Expired / Nd / a date), `:1978` raises an attention item for anything inside 30 days, and `:5476` counts expired and expiring on the dashboard. The row stores no status word: a blank or wrong expiry yields `null` days and the caregiver simply does not appear in any of the three, which is a silent removal from the alert rather than a visible gap. **AND IT IS THE EVIDENCE AN AGENCY PRODUCES WHEN A SURVEYOR ASKS.** `bgcheck_date` and `state_caregiver_id` are the two facts a state asks for about a named aide entering a client's home. **CONFIDENTIALITY A:** a state registration number is a government identifier attached to a named person, which this register's own A definition names; the row additionally asserts the outcome of a background check about that person. Same class as `grd_training_completions` and `sb_train`, both A/A |
| `sen_claims` | **A** | **A** | A claim wrong or misrouted | A named person's health-care payment record is disclosed. | `sen_clients` | **A** | **A** | A home-care recipient&rsquo;s record is lost or wrong, and care is planned and delivered on it | A named person&rsquo;s identity, home address and care needs are disclosed together | **RE-TIERED B -> A on BOTH axes, 2026-09-22.** THE CODE ALREADY TREATED THIS AS PHI AND THE REGISTER NEVER ABSORBED IT. `api/_resources/sairnsenior.js` says it in its own words: &ldquo;built with a real HIPAA MINIMUM-NECESSARY privacy gate from day one -- a caregiver only ever sees clients assigned to them&rdquo;. A minimum-necessary gate is a HIPAA control and it is not built for operational data. So the app enforced a PHI posture while the register carried the row under &ldquo;neither money nor a regulated record&rdquo;, and the two have disagreed since the resource existed. This is the register catching up with the code, not a change of view. **Confidentiality individually read 2026-09-22** |

FOUND at `sairnsenior.html:4567`: `{branch_id, branch_name, unit_code, franchisee_name, royalty_pct, ad_fund_pct, royalty_base, effective_on, term_on, notes}`. It is a LEGAL INSTRUMENT with an effective period -- the app refuses one with no start because *&ldquo;an agreement with no start cannot govern a period&rdquo;* -- and the percentages are what every royalty payment is computed from, so one wrong field mis-pays a branch for as long as it stands. **NOT PROMOTED ON CONFIDENTIALITY, and that follows the `bld_pos` precedent settled earlier today:** commercial sensitivity is not one of the four classes this rule names, and if it qualified, half the register would become Confidentiality A and the axis would stop discriminating. **Read from the APP, not the schema; confidentiality individually read** |

IT HOLDS A FEDERAL MEDICAID COMPLIANCE CONFIGURATION.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `saveEvvConfig()` at `sairnsenior.html:5630` writes `{setting_key:'evv_config', value:{state, aggregator}}`, alongside `agency_profile`. **THE SERVER TREATS IT AS AUTHORITY, WHICH IS THE TELL:** `sen_settings` `api/sd-data.js:5474` says outright that *“the configured operating state comes from sen_settings, never the caller’s payload”* and reads `setting_key=eq.evv_config` before evaluating anything. `api/_lib/sen-evv-readiness.js` then scores each visit against the federal floor at **42 U.S.C. §1396b(l)(5)(A)(i)-(vi)**, added by Pub. L. 114-255 §12006 — Electronic Visit Verification, which a State must REQUIRE for personal-care and home-health visits. The join here is not theoretical -- `trRender()` resolves `caregiver_id` to the caregiver's name in the same store on every render, so the row is about an identified person in practice and not only in principle |
| `sen_training_rules` | **A** | **B** | A required-hours figure or its period is wrong, and every caregiver's compliance verdict computed against it is wrong -- in either direction, since one of these rules is a statutory CEILING as well as a floor | No elevated confidentiality class -- the row is a statute table. No person appears on it at all | **INDIVIDUALLY READ OUT OF THE APP 2026-09-23 (Hank).** **B -> A on the TIER axis; confidentiality individually read and left at B.** `:3432` seeds `{id, jurisdiction, program, aide_type, kind, hours, period_months, ceiling, citation, citation_url, effective_from, notes, seeded}` carrying **real statutory citations with URLs** -- `42 CFR 484.80(d)` (12 in-service hours per 12 months, RN-supervised), `ORC 5164.913(A)(1)` capped by `(A)(2)`, and the OAC rules beside them. **THIS ROW DECIDES A COMPLIANCE VERDICT RATHER THAN RECORDING ONE**, which is the `sd_pricing_rules` / `grd_boq_rates` argument on a regulatory axis instead of a money one: `sen_training_records` is measured AGAINST these hours, so a wrong `hours` or `period_months` does not mislabel one row, it moves every caregiver across the compliant line at once. **AND `ceiling:true` MAKES IT WRONG IN BOTH DIRECTIONS, which is unusual and is why it is worth naming:** the row itself records that ORC 5164.913(A)(2) *bars the department from requiring MORE than six hours*, so a wrong figure can make an agency demand training the statute forbids requiring, as well as accept too little |

Discharging cody&rsquo;s `2026-09-22T15:18:11Z` obligation I confirmed FROM THE DIFF that `sen_visits` and `sen_pay_rates` are the two inputs and the output is what each caregiver is PAID -- hours &times; rate, with FLSA weighted-average overtime computed on top. A wrong or lost visit is simultaneously a mispriced invoice and an underpaid person, and an underpayment carries liquidated damages regardless of intent. **THE CONFIDENTIALITY HALF IS THE EVV PAYLOAD:** `SEN_VISIT_EVV_FIELDS` is `clock_in_at, clock_in_lat, clock_in_lng, clock_out_at, clock_out_lat, clock_out_lng, services_notes, status`, so the row records the GPS COORDINATES OF A NAMED CLIENT&rsquo;S HOME, the times somebody was inside it, and notes on the care delivered. `sen_clients` was re-tiered for being a named person&rsquo;s identity and care needs; this is that same person&rsquo;s location, on a schedule. **Read from the handler and the app, not the schema** |

### `sairnvet` — 42 resources

| Resource | Tier | Confidentiality | Worst consequence if it is wrong or lost | Worst consequence if it is read by the wrong person | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `sv_audit_log` | **A** | **B** | An audit row wrong, and wrong everywhere except where it was corrected | An unauthorised reader learns aggregate performance figures. Commercially sensitive, not an elevated class -- the row carries no individual's record | REGULATED. Additive-only hydration means a correction never reaches a second workstation -- disclosed on screen in `ad588e6c`. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (AGGREGATE). Confidentiality-B on the reading that an audit row records WHO DID WHAT to a record rather than reproducing the record. If audit rows carry clinical payloads they inherit `sv_soapnotes`' tier** |
| `sv_billing` | **A** | **B** | A client billed wrongly | A competitor or an unauthorised employee learns the company's own commercial figures. Commercially sensitive, not an elevated class: no PII, PHI, privileged communication or financial-account detail belonging to a third party sits on this row | Money. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (MONEY_INTERNAL)** |
| `sv_boarding` | **A** | **B** | A boarding rate or a stay length is wrong, and the month's boarding revenue is misstated | A competitor or an unauthorised employee learns the practice's own commercial figures. Commercially sensitive, not an elevated class: the row names an ANIMAL and carries no owner, no financial-account detail belonging to a third party, and no clinical record | **RE-TIERED B -> A on the INTEGRITY axis only, 2026-09-23.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `addBoarding()` at `sairnvet.html:8544` writes `{id, patient, service, checkin, checkout, rate}`, where `rate` is a real `$/day or flat` figure the user types. `renderBoarding()` folds it into a **Revenue MTD** KPI as `rate x days`, deriving `days` from the checkin/checkout pair -- so a wrong rate AND a wrong date both misstate the same money figure, which is what puts this past `sd_business_snapshots`'s test of *“nothing is priced, billed or decided from it”*. **A CORRECTION TO THIS CELL, 2026-09-23, MADE THE SAME DAY IT WAS WRITTEN.** It said the fold being uncoerced put this figure at risk of the concatenation class *“the exact shape fixed in `stonedesk.html` the same day”*. **IT DID NOT, AND THAT WAS MEASURED:** the expression is `s + (b.rate&#124;&#124;0)*days`, and `*` has no string overload, so the multiplication coerces before the addition ever sees the value — string rates of “500” and “250” over 3 days give **2250**, where the bare fold gives **“0500250”**. `truthy_sum_check.py` reports it anyway and its own docstring says it does; it calls itself a HAZARD reporter, not a defect detector. **WHAT WAS REAL is a different and smaller thing:** the multiplication rescued the STRING case and not the JUNK case — `“abc” * 3` is NaN, and one NaN poisoned the whole reduce so the KPI rendered `$NaN` and every other booking's revenue vanished with it. Coerced and un-grandfathered in `tests/sv_boarding_revenue_coercion.js`. **THE TIER IS UNAFFECTED** — it rests on `rate x days` being the Revenue MTD figure, which is true either way. Recorded rather than quietly edited, because overstating evidence for a tier is the same error as understating it. **CONFIDENTIALITY STAYS B AND THE REASON IS THE FIELD LIST, not the app:** `patient` is the animal's name and the owner is on `sv_clients`, not here. Same MONEY_INTERNAL ground `sv_billing` was cleared on. **Confidentiality individually read 2026-09-23** |
| `sv_clients` | **A** | **A** | A client's outstanding balance is wrong, and they are billed, chased or written off on it | A NAMED CLIENT is disclosed together with what they OWE the practice, their animals and when they last came in | **RE-TIERED B -> A on BOTH axes, 2026-09-23, AND THIS CLOSES A COULD-NOT-TELL RATHER THAN A PASS.** `tools/confidentiality_candidate_flagger.py` flagged this row on the `name` signal and it had been carried at the B default ever since, with the cell saying in its own words that the default was the thing that might be wrong. **The flagger was right.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `addClient()` at `sairnvet.html:6910` writes `{id, client, patients, lastVisit, balance, status, since}`. **THE CONFIDENTIALITY AXIS TURNS ON THE REGISTER'S OWN B DEFINITION, WHICH THIS ROW FAILS BY ITS OWN WORDING:** B is *“no PII, PHI, privileged communication or financial-account detail belonging to a THIRD PARTY”*, and `sv_billing` was cleared to B on precisely that sentence. A named person joined to the sum they OWE is third-party financial detail -- it is not the practice's own commercial figure, it is somebody's debt. Same family test as `sc_drg` and `sc_eligibility`: a named individual joined to the money attached to them. **ON THE INTEGRITY AXIS** `balance` is rendered per row as `$X.XX` and summed into a **Total Balance** KPI, so a wrong figure is a client billed or chased wrongly. **A CONTRAST WORTH RECORDING, because it cuts the other way for once:** that sum is written `typeof c.balance==='number' ? c.balance : 0` -- properly guarded, unlike `sv_boarding`'s `(b.rate&#124;&#124;0)` two panels away. **Confidentiality individually read 2026-09-23** |
| `sv_coggins` | **A** | **A** | An EIA (Coggins) test record is lost or wrong, and an animal is moved or admitted on it | A named horse, its owner and its testing history are disclosed | **RE-TIERED B -> A 2026-09-22.** REGULATORY rather than merely clinical. A Coggins certificate is the document required for interstate movement and for entry to most events; a wrong or missing record is a horse that cannot legally travel, or one that travels on a test that does not say what the record claims. A regulated record is not &ldquo;operational data lost or wrong&rdquo; under this table&rsquo;s own B rule. **Confidentiality individually read 2026-09-22 (CLINICAL)** |
| `sv_comms` | **B** | **B** | Operational data lost or wrong | **INDIVIDUALLY READ TWICE, and the flagger's hit is a FALSE POSITIVE of its name signal (2026-09-25; H2 seq #224, H1 log #543, independent).** The could-not-tell is resolved, not carried. READ OUT OF THE APP rather than off the schema: the only writer is `sairnvet.html:8480`, which pushes `{id, type, recipients, sent, openRate, campaign}` -- an AGGREGATE CAMPAIGN ROW. `recipients` is a COUNT (28, 46), not a list; there is no recipient name, no address, no phone, and **no message content anywhere on the row** -- the app stores that a reminder went to 28 people and 94% opened it, never who or what it said. The flagger fired on the RESOURCE NAME (`comms` reads as correspondence) and the name is the whole signal; this is the shape the flagger's own header calls a candidate for reading, not a finding. B is CORRECT on the confidentiality axis and now rests on two reads instead of a default | Operational data lost or wrong: neither money nor a regulated record. A lost campaign row costs a marketing statistic |

| `sv_invoicing` | **A** | **A** | An invoice amount or status is wrong, and a client is billed, chased or written off on it | A NAMED CLIENT is disclosed with what they were billed and whether they have paid -- including that they are OVERDUE | **RE-TIERED B -> A on BOTH axes, 2026-09-23.** READ OUT OF THE APP: the row is `{id, num, client, amount, status, date}` (`sairnvet.html:6936`), and `renderInvoicing()` at `:6947` computes FOUR money figures from it -- Invoiced Today, Outstanding, Avg Invoice and a Collection Rate of paid over billable. This is the literal invoice record; there is no lighter reading of it. **CONFIDENTIALITY FOLLOWS `sv_clients`, PROMOTED THE SAME DAY, AND IS STRONGER HERE:** the register's B wording excludes *“financial-account detail belonging to a THIRD PARTY”*, and a named person joined to a sum they were billed is exactly that. `status` carries `Overdue`, so the row does not merely disclose a debt, it discloses a DELINQUENT one against a name. **A CONTRAST WORTH RECORDING, because it cuts the other way from `sv_boarding`:** every one of the four folds here is written `typeof v.amount==='number' ? v.amount : 0` -- guarded at each site, not once. Whoever wrote this panel did the thing `sv_boarding` two panels away did not. **Confidentiality individually read 2026-09-23** |
| `sv_labresults` | **A** | **A** | A laboratory result is lost or wrong, and a diagnosis or a dose is set from it | A named patient's laboratory results are disclosed -- values a clinician reads as the animal's current state | **RE-TIERED B -> A 2026-09-22**, and it is the SAME correction `sv_soapnotes` already carries, applied to the row that FEEDS it. The patient's identity (`sv_patients`) is Tier A and the clinical assessment written about them (`sv_soapnotes`) is Tier A; the laboratory result the assessment is MADE FROM was B. **THE INCONSISTENCY IS THE ARGUMENT, for the third time in this app:** the register protected the name and the conclusion while leaving the evidence the conclusion rests on one tier lower. A wrong or lost result is not an inconvenience -- it is a diagnosis made on a number that was never true, and on a veterinary record there is no patient who can say the value looks wrong. Its old cell read "No elevated confidentiality class -- no PII, PHI, privileged communication, or financial-account detail", which is the B rule's own sentence applied to a named animal's clinical results. **Confidentiality individually read 2026-09-22 (CLINICAL). This is a CLASSIFICATION fix, not a protection one:** `sv_labresults` is in `SV_RESOURCES` and has required an employee session since `6fb6d696`, so nothing about its handling changes here -- what was wrong was the label |
| `sv_lameness` | **A** | **A** | A lameness grading is lost or wrong, and a soundness judgement is made from it | A named horse&rsquo;s soundness history is disclosed &mdash; which is commercially material to its owner | **RE-TIERED B -> A 2026-09-22.** The confidentiality half here is NOT only clinical. A lameness record is the history a buyer would want and a seller would not volunteer, so disclosure has a direct commercial consequence for the client on top of the clinical one. Same reasoning `sv_prepurchase` carries, arrived at from the other direction. **Confidentiality individually read 2026-09-22 (CLINICAL)** |
| `sv_mobilevet` | **B** | **B** | Operational data lost or wrong | No elevated confidentiality class -- no PII, PHI, privileged communication, or financial-account detail on this row. Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read | Operational data lost or wrong: neither money nor a regulated record. Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read |
| `sv_multisite` | **A** | **B** | A manually-typed location revenue is wrong, and the cross-location total and the BEST-LOCATION ranking computed from it are wrong with it | A competitor or an unauthorised employee learns the practice's own per-site commercial figures. Commercially sensitive, not an elevated class: the row names a CLINIC, not a person | **RE-TIERED B -> A on the INTEGRITY axis only, 2026-09-23.** READ OUT OF THE APP: the row is `{id, location, revenue, patients, status}` (`sairnvet.html:7599`), and `revenue` is **TYPED BY HAND** into `ms-edit-revenue` at `:7653` -- a number input labelled *“Revenue MTD”*. **NOTHING COMPUTES IT FROM `sv_invoicing`, AND THAT IS THE ARGUMENT RATHER THAN A COMPLAINT:** it is a SECOND FACT ABOUT THE SAME MONEY, so it can disagree with the invoices silently and in either direction. **WHAT IS DECIDED FROM IT** is what carries the tier past `sd_business_snapshots`'s test of *“nothing is priced, billed or decided from it”*: `renderMultisite()` folds it into a cross-location total AND reduces the list to a single BEST LOCATION by revenue. A best/worst ranking across sites is what an owner uses to decide where to invest or which to close, and it is computed from a figure nobody verified. **Confidentiality individually read 2026-09-23: B**, same MONEY_INTERNAL ground as `sd_order_history` -- the practice's own figures, no individual on the row |
| `sv_patients` | **A** | **A** | A patient record wrong | A named patient's clinical record is disclosed -- the diagnosis, the treatment, or the record the next clinician will act on | PROTECTED: clinical records. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (PHI_CLINICAL). Veterinary records are not PHI -- the patient is an animal -- but the register already designates this row PROTECTED, and the owner's identity and contact detail are carried with it. Confidentiality-A on the register's own existing designation, not on HIPAA** |
| `sv_peerconsults` | **A** | **A** | A second opinion sought on a case is lost or wrong, and the treating clinician acts without it | A named patient&rsquo;s case is disclosed to, and discussed with, a clinician outside the practice | **RE-TIERED B -> A 2026-09-22.** This is the clinical record LEAVING the practice: the whole point of a peer consult is that a third party sees the case. It carries the same content as `sv_soapnotes` plus a disclosure event, and the record of WHO was consulted about WHICH patient is itself the sensitive fact. **Confidentiality individually read 2026-09-22 (CLINICAL)** |
| `sv_petinsurance` | **A** | **B** | A claim or reimbursement amount is wrong, and the Avg Reimbursement percentage computed from BOTH of them is wrong with it | A competitor or an unauthorised employee learns which insurers the practice bills and what it recovers. Commercially sensitive, not an elevated class: the row names an ANIMAL and a COMPANY, and no person appears on it | **RE-TIERED B -> A on INTEGRITY, 2026-09-23, AND THE CONFIDENTIALITY COULD-NOT-TELL IS RESOLVED TO B BY A READ RATHER THAN LEFT AT A DEFAULT.** READ OUT OF THE APP: the row is `{id, patient, provider, amount, status, submitted, reimbursed, reimbursedDate}` (`sairnvet.html:5765`, written at `:5818`). **THE FLAGGER WAS RIGHT TO FIRE AND THE DEFAULT WAS RIGHT TO HOLD** -- `tools/confidentiality_candidate_flagger.py` raised this on the `name` signal, and reading it shows the names are `Max` and `Whiskers`: `patient` is the ANIMAL, the owner lives on `sv_clients`, and `provider` is an insurer. That is the same ground `sv_boarding` was confirmed B on the same day, and it is the opposite of `sv_clients` and `sv_farmcalls`, which carry a named CLIENT. A could-not-tell resolving to B is a resolution, and the cell now says so as a finding rather than as a default nobody had tested. **ON THE INTEGRITY AXIS THERE ARE TWO MONEY FIELDS, NOT ONE, AND THE KPI USES BOTH:** Avg Reimbursement is `reimbursed / amount * 100` averaged across reimbursed claims, so a wrong `amount` skews the recovery rate even when the reimbursement is right -- the error enters through the DENOMINATOR, where it is least visible. A third KPI averages turnaround days from `submitted` to `reimbursedDate`. **AND ONE UNGUARDED CALL, recorded as evidence rather than as a separate complaint:** `:5784` renders `c.amount.toFixed(2)` with no type check, so a row whose `amount` is absent throws and takes the whole table with it. The add path guards (`parseFloat(...)&#124;&#124;0` plus a required check at `:5816`), but a row arriving from the server does not pass through it. Not fixed here -- a tier read does not edit an app. **Confidentiality individually read 2026-09-23** |
| `sv_portal` | **A** | **A** | The client-facing record of what an owner was shown or told is lost or wrong | A named CLIENT&rsquo;s identity and contact details are disclosed together with their animals&rsquo; records | **RE-TIERED B -> A 2026-09-22.** The row in this app whose subject is the PERSON rather than the animal. A client portal holds owner identity and contact detail joined to patient records -- the JOIN is the sensitive object, not either side alone. `law_portalesign` and `law_portalmessages` were re-tiered on the same reasoning about a client portal in another app. **Confidentiality individually read 2026-09-22 (CLINICAL)** |
| `sv_prepurchase` | **A** | **A** | A pre-purchase examination is lost or wrong, and a sale proceeds on it | A named animal&rsquo;s examination findings are disclosed to a party in a transaction | **RE-TIERED B -> A 2026-09-22.** A pre-purchase exam is a professional opinion relied on by a BUYER in a transaction, which makes it the veterinary record with the most direct financial consequence and the clearest liability exposure. Disclosure to the wrong side of a sale is itself the harm, independently of accuracy. **Confidentiality individually read 2026-09-22 (CLINICAL)** |
| `sv_referrals` | **A** | **A** | A referral is lost or wrong, and a patient is not sent for, or is sent for, the wrong specialist care | A named patient is disclosed together with the SPECIFIC CLINICAL REASON they are being referred, and the outside practice receiving them | **RE-TIERED B -> A on BOTH axes, 2026-09-23.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `getReferrals()` at `sairnvet.html:5772` seeds and stores `{id, patient, specialist, reason, status, sent, returned}`. **THE `reason` FIELD IS THE ARGUMENT, and the app&rsquo;s own seed data shows what it holds:** *&ldquo;DCM workup&rdquo;* (dilated cardiomyopathy) and *&ldquo;TPLO consult&rdquo;* (a cruciate repair). A referral reason is frequently the most diagnostically revealing field in the whole record -- it states a SUSPICION the chart may not yet state -- and it is recorded here beside the patient name and the outside practice it is being sent to. That is the identical argument `dnt_referrals` carries and the identical test `sv_teleconsults` was re-tiered on: the clinical record LEAVING the practice, named. On the integrity axis a lost referral is a patient who does not get seen, and `status` and `returned` are how a practice knows whether that happened. **Read from the APP, not the schema** |
| `sv_reminders` | **A** | **A** | A due date is wrong or a reminder is lost, and a legally required vaccination or a regulated test lapses unnoticed | A named patient is disclosed together with the specific immunisation or test due for them, and their owner is identifiable through it | **RE-TIERED B -> A on BOTH axes, 2026-09-23.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `getReminders()` at `sairnvet.html:5953` seeds and stores `{patient, type, due, status, contacted}`, and **the app’s own seed shows what `type` holds** — *“Rabies Booster”* and *“Coggins Renewal”*. **NEITHER OF THOSE IS A CONVENIENCE REMINDER.** Rabies vaccination is legally mandated for dogs in most US states, and a Coggins (EIA) test is a regulated equine requirement whose own resource, `sv_coggins`, is ALREADY TIER A on this register. So this row is the clock on two regulated obligations, and `renderReminders()` computes an `overdue` count straight off `due` — a wrong date does not misreport a record, it removes an animal from the overdue list. CONFIDENTIALITY-A on the same sv_ cluster test that took `sv_referrals` and `sv_patients` there: a named patient joined to a specific clinical item. **THE INCONSISTENCY IS THE ARGUMENT, again:** the regulated test was Tier A and the record of WHEN IT IS DUE was Tier B |

Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read | Operational data lost or wrong: neither money nor a regulated record. Classified by the stated B rule rather than individually read |

Same dual argument as `sv_lameness`. **Confidentiality individually read 2026-09-22 (CLINICAL)** |

| `sv_scribe_consent` | **A** | **A** | The record that a client was ASKED before an exam room was recorded is lost or wrong, and a lawful recording becomes an unprovable one | A named client is disclosed together with a personal decision they made at a medical visit -- including that they REFUSED | **NEW RESOURCE, 2026-09-23, tiered as it landed rather than defaulted and revisited.** READ OUT OF THE APP: `{asked_at, date, patient, answer, statement_version, asked_by_role, asked_by_employee_id}` (`scribeClientAnswer()`, `sairnvet.html`). **NO AUDIO IS ON THIS ROW AND THE SCHEMA ENFORCES IT** -- `sql/sairnvet_data_schema.sql` carries a `sv_scribe_consent_no_audio` CHECK, the belt-and-braces shape `sql/biometric_consent_schema.sql` uses for its retention ceiling. TIER A ON THE REGULATED LIMB: an exam room is an IN-PERSON conversation, SAIRNvet asks for ALL-PARTY consent everywhere by design (3 of `docs/2026-09-23-sairnvet-ambient-scribe-consent-scoping.md`), and this row is the practice's only evidence that it did. Losing it does not lose a record of the visit -- it loses the defence for having recorded one, which is a statutory question and not an operational one. CONFIDENTIALITY-A on `sv_portal`’s reasoning rather than on clinical content, of which this row has NONE: the subject here is the PERSON, not the animal, and the join of an identified client to their answer is the sensitive object. **That a client DECLINED is the more sensitive half and is stored deliberately** -- a register holding only agreements could not show the ask was ever real |

SOAP notes are the VETERINARY CLINICAL RECORD -- subjective, objective, ASSESSMENT (the diagnosis) and PLAN (the treatment) -- for the same patient whose `sv_patients` row is ALREADY TIER A. **THE INCONSISTENCY IS THE ARGUMENT:** the identity of the animal was Tier A while the diagnosis and treatment plan attached to it were B, so the register protected the name and not the medicine. A wrong or lost assessment is acted on by the next clinician to open the chart, which is the harm B's sentence does not cover: a clinical record IS a regulated record, and this row was classified by the stated rule rather than read. **Confidentiality individually read in the 3.2 pass, 2026-09-22 (PHI_CLINICAL). Same basis as `sv_patients`: not HIPAA, but the clinical record of an identified owner's animal, and the register already rates the identity A. Protecting the name and not the medicine was the inconsistency this row was re-tiered to fix** |

| `sv_surgery` | **A** | **A** | A surgical record is lost or wrong &mdash; what was done, under what anaesthetic, and what was left in | A named patient&rsquo;s surgical history is disclosed | **RE-TIERED B -> A 2026-09-22.** The surgical record is the one a subsequent clinician cannot reconstruct by examination: implants, ligatures, what was resected. It is also the record a malpractice question turns on. Tier A on the integrity axis alone, before confidentiality is considered. **Confidentiality individually read 2026-09-22 (CLINICAL)** |
| `sv_teleconsults` | **A** | **A** | A remote consultation record is lost or wrong, and it is the only record of the advice given | A named patient&rsquo;s consultation is disclosed, together with the client who sought it | **RE-TIERED B -> A 2026-09-22.** A teleconsult is frequently the ONLY record of the encounter -- there is no paper chart from a physical visit to fall back on -- so a lost row is not a degraded record, it is an absent one. It also ties the owner to the presenting complaint, which is client PII and not only patient data. **Confidentiality individually read 2026-09-22 (CLINICAL)** |

It is a clinical record routinely relied on OUTSIDE the practice, which is exactly why it has to be right. **Confidentiality individually read 2026-09-22 (CLINICAL)** |

## The gaps — read this section first

- **All 17 apps are re-tiered: 388 resources, 259 A, 129 B, 0 C.** **RE-DERIVED 2026-09-24 (Cody) while discharging a review that asked for exactly that, and it was stale by ONE: it read 247/141 against an actual 248/140 after `sv_petinsurance` moved that morning. THE FIFTH RECORDED DRIFT OF THIS ONE SENTENCE, and the last one nobody had to find by hand -- `tools/criticality_tier_check.py` now re-derives it on every run and raises HEADLINE when it disagrees, the same guard the per-app rollup count has had since day one and the rollup LIST got on 2026-09-23. It is a problem line and NOT a `--fix`: this sentence is a restatement wrapped in an argument, and a tool editing numbers inside somebody's prose is the regenerate-the-judgement failure the checker refuses by design.** That is **64% Tier A**, and the distribution is the point. **RE-DERIVED 2026-09-23 (cc) after promoting `sc_anesthesia_base_units` and `sc_auth`, and it had ALREADY MOVED BY FOUR from other sessions' promotions before mine arrived -- 229/159 against an actual 231/157 once my two landed. Re-run rather than adjusted by two, which is the only way to tell the difference.** **RE-DERIVED 2026-09-23 (Hank) by the command in the gaps note below, not adjusted by the size of the edit that touched it — and it was ALREADY STALE BY THREE when that edit arrived: it read *"195 A, 189 B"* against an actual 198 / 186, from promotions that landed earlier the same day without this sentence moving. The SAIRNbuild re-audit's own three (`bld_inspections`, `bld_toolbox_talks`, `bld_warranty`) account for exactly HALF of the six-row correction, and saying which half is the only way a reader can tell a re-derivation from an arithmetic adjustment.** **AND IT CONFLICTED IN THE REBASE, WHICH IS THE PART WORTH KEEPING:** another session re-derived this same line concurrently and got *"201 A, 187 B, 0 C"* while this one got *"201 A, 183 B, 4 C"* — two sessions re-deriving one count from one table, in the same hour, disagreeing on BOTH the B figure and on whether any C rows were left. Neither was right about the MERGED tree. Re-derived once more after the merge rather than picking a side, which is the only resolution that is a measurement: **204 A, 184 B, 0 C**. A count that two correct-looking derivations disagree about is a count that has to be re-run on the tree it is being written into, not on the tree it was computed from. **AND IT CONFLICTED IN A REBASE AGAIN, 2026-09-25, WHICH IS THE RECORDED FAILURE REPEATING ON SCHEDULE:** hank re-derived 250/138 after promoting `sf_district_imports` and `sdn_clients`; this session re-derived 255/133 after promoting seven more; NEITHER was right about the merged tree, so the figure above was re-derived once more ON the merge -- the only resolution that is a measurement.**

That is the second time this sentence has been found drifting from its own table, which is the argument for the command in the gaps note below rather than arithmetic. The app-level version put 21 of 22 verticals at A and told a reader nothing; this says which fifth of the platform carries the consequence.

- **Tier A is hand-verified, one written evidence each. B and C are classified by the stated rule.** A row's tier is a judgement either way, but the A rows are the ones a reader acts on, so they carry individually-written evidence naming a commit, an incident, a regulation or a registry line. **Saying all 382 had each been individually read would not be true**, and the checker enforces the difference: a Tier A row with an empty evidence cell is refused.
- **⚠ THE KEYWORD PASS WAS WRONG IN BOTH DIRECTIONS, which is exactly why A is not rule-classified.** It proposed `sf_accounts` and `law_opaccounts` as Tier C because *"accounts"* contains *"counts"* — two money resources one character away from being filed as preferences. It also missed `dnt_coverage_rules`, `dnt_txplans`, `leg_preneed` and `rf_contingency_rules`, all of which are money and none of which match a money keyword. Every one was corrected by hand, and each correction is named in its own row.
- **Every hand-written A entry was reconciled against the registries and none was orphaned.** An entry naming a resource that does not exist would be evidence written for nothing; the generator fails if one appears.
- **The B tier is 129 rows and it is the honest weak point of this file.** (Re-derived 2026-09-25 on a merged tree after two sessions promoted rows concurrently -- hank two (`sf_district_imports`, `sdn_clients`), this session seven. **THE PREVIOUS VERSION OF THIS SENTENCE DISAGREED WITH ITSELF INSIDE ONE PAIR OF BRACKETS** — it said *"The B tier is 189 rows"* and then *"it read 283 against an actual 210"*, three different figures for one count in one sentence, none of which was the table's 186 at the time. Corrected by re-running the derivation, not by reconciling the three.) **AND THE SAIRNBUILD RE-AUDIT IS THE ARGUMENT FOR THIS BULLET RATHER THAN A FOOTNOTE TO IT:** all 15 `bld_` B rows carried the identical rule sentence, nobody had opened any of them, and three were wrong — `bld_warranty` had a `cost` column summed into a Total Cost KPI, `bld_inspections` held municipal code determinations, `bld_toolbox_talks` was OSHA instruction evidence. A 20% error rate on one app's B tier, found by reading. Each says the same thing — not money, not regulated — because that is what the rule says, not because 189 files were read. **`bld_change_orders` is the second row in two days to be found carrying money under that sentence**, and unlike `sd_exec_msgs` it was found by reading the app rather than by an incident — which is the cheaper of the two ways and the only one that scales. A resource misfiled as B is the failure mode that matters, and the only defence here is that the A criteria are broad on purpose: money *and* anything upstream of money, regulated data *and* licensure, plus any resource with a documented incident.
  - **`sd_exec_msgs` is what that weak point looks like when it fires**, which is why it is named here and not only in its own row. It sat in this tier for eleven days on *"an internal message lost"* — a sentence that is true, says nothing about who may read the thing, and invited nobody to check. The row moved to A on 2026-09-21 only after the exposure was found and reproduced. **Nothing mechanical found it and nothing mechanical would have**: the checker verifies that every row HAS a tier and that every A row cites evidence, never that a B sentence describes the right axis of failure.
  - **This count is prose and the tool does not check it.** It read 299 against an actual 298 before an earlier re-tier — already stale by one when that session arrived, from a row move that corrected the table and not the sentence. **IT WENT STALE AGAIN THE SAME DAY, BY THREE, AND THAT IS THE PART WORTH READING.** `exec_context`, `law_portalmessages` and `sv_soapnotes` moved B → A in `707b5796`; the headline bullet above was re-derived in that commit and says so, and this sentence three bullets below it was not. One number in a document corrected and its sibling left behind is how a count stops being evidence, and it happened here between one bullet and another in a single edit. Corrected to 290 on 2026-09-22 by the command below rather than by subtracting three. **AND IT WAS FOUND STALE BY 107 ON 2026-09-23 (Hank), which is a fourth drift of the same number and the largest yet** — it read 290 against an actual 186, while its own PARENT bullet said 189 in the same breath. Both re-derived to **184** in the SAIRNbuild re-audit by re-running the command, not by reconciling the two figures against each other. **AND IT DRIFTED A FIFTH TIME, FROM ITS OWN PARENT, WHICH IS THE PAIR THIS BULLET IS ABOUT.** On 2026-09-23 the parent bullet read **159** while this sentence still ended on **184** -- a bullet and its own child, twenty-five apart, inside the paragraph whose subject is that exact failure. Found by cc while reviewing hank's SAIRNbuild obligation and corrected here to **154** by re-running the command below on the tree it is being written into. **A number recorded in two bullets is a number that will disagree with itself**; this pair has now done so twice. Re-derive it from the table when a tier changes rather than adjusting it by the size of your own edit: `python -c "import re;print(__import__('collections').Counter(re.findall(r'^\| \`[^\`]+\` \| \*\*([ABC])\*\* \|', open('docs/CRITICALITY-TIERS.md',encoding='utf-8').read(), re.M)))"`.
- **A WORKED EXAMPLE OF WHY A FINDING GETS WRITTEN DOWN EVEN WHEN IT IS OUT OF SCOPE — `leg_gplservices` and `leg_florists`, 2026-09-23.** Both were found while reading the four rows a claim covered, and both were OUTSIDE that claim. They were logged in this section as candidates rather than edited silently, and landed in a second claim an hour later. **That is the whole mechanism: the session that finds a thing is usually not the session entitled to fix it, and the gap between the two is where findings die.** The two are not incidental extras either — each is the other half of a pair whose first half had just been promoted (`leg_gplservices` with `leg_merch_catalog` is one GPL; `leg_florists` is the rate upstream of `leg_floristorders`), so leaving them unstated would have opened the same split-limb gap those promotions closed. The register has now recorded that gap opening three times: `sd_exec_msgs`, `leg_cases`, and `leg_catererorders` promoted a day before its identical sibling `leg_floristorders`.
- **THE C TIER NOW HAS ZERO REGISTERED MEMBERS, AND THAT IS A PROPERTY OF ITS OWN DEFINITION RATHER THAN AN ACCIDENT (2026-09-23).** C reads *"cosmetic, device state, or preference — **the shape already excluded from server sync** for being regenerable or per-device"*, and **the unit of this register is the REGISTRY**. So a registered, server-synced resource filed at C is a contradiction in terms, and all five were. The `*_settings` sweep read every one: `rf_settings` holds the hail/wind thresholds behind a claim assessment, `sc_settings` the records-retention term behind a purge, `sen_settings` the EVV operating state behind a Medicaid readiness grade, `dnt_settings` the slug an unauthenticated public surface resolves a TENANT by, and `style_profile` is keyed to an employee id while its cell claimed nothing on the row was about a person. **THE RULE WAS NEVER WRONG — ITS APPLICATION WAS**, and the proof is that the four genuinely-C collections (`sd_settings`, `sd_alert_settings`, `sd_ai_counts`, `sd_market_history`) are correctly NOT REGISTERED and appear in the bullet below rather than in the table. **THE COLUMN STAYS.** An empty tier that a future resource could legitimately fall into is not dead weight, and deleting it would remove the vocabulary that makes the paragraph below sayable. **WHAT TO DO WITH THIS ELSEWHERE:** the generalisable finding is that **a resource’s NAME was the only evidence for its tier** in all five cases. `settings` reads as preference. Nothing else about any of them did. — and note that three of the five carried a SERVER-SIDE ROLE GATE or a statutory guard while sitting at the lowest tier in the register, which is the single cheapest tell to grep for next time.
- **Four StoneDesk collections are deliberately absent**: `sd_settings`, `sd_alert_settings`, `sd_ai_counts`, `sd_market_history`. Real data, and Tier C by the rule, but **not registered resources** — excluded from the server backup on purpose with the reason recorded. The table's unit is the registry; stretching it would make the counts mean two things at once.
- **`sairncash` has zero registered resources.** Nothing to tier today; it becomes relevant the day a Stripe key is set.
- **The earlier platform count of 405 was wrong and is corrected to 382.** A hand grep matched quoted strings that are not resource names; the checker counts from the registries themselves.

## What a tier is FOR

It is not a badge. It decides three things, and it should be cited when it does:

1. **How much verification a change needs before it ships.** A change touching a Tier A resource is where independent review and a live check stop being optional.
2. **Whether a guard belongs in the blocking set.** `GUARD_TESTS` in `tools/sairn_push_gate_hook.py` is the blocking registry; a Tier A resource with no guard is a finding.
3. **What an incident costs, before it happens.** Written down so nobody has to form that judgement during the incident, which is the worst moment to be forming it for the first time.
